# Find A Crib — growth decision journal

Append-only. One entry per review. The ledger (`techniques.json`) holds what each
technique *is* and how it scored; this holds *why* — what was observed, what was
concluded, what changed as a result, and what to watch next.

Written by the daily 6am review. Do not rewrite past entries; if a conclusion
turns out to be wrong, say so in a new entry.


## 2026-07-26 — Engine goes live
*claude (setup session)*

**Observed.** Organic search is ~3 visitors/day against 47k indexed pages; revenue is $0 (the 6 active subs are the owner plus 5 comps); the Developer API has a working Stripe checkout and zero real signups.

**Concluded.** The bottleneck is indexing and freshness, not content volume — the SEO rebuild ran monthly so IndexNow fired ~once a month. Revenue is a demand problem, not a product problem.

**Changed.** Shipped five daily techniques (voucher pages, dated briefs, llms.txt, daily sitemap, IndexNow), an Enterprise API tier, and drafts-only B2B outreach.

**Watching.** Whether /section8/ earns owned traffic within the 21-day grace period, and whether organic/day moves off ~3 once IndexNow runs daily instead of monthly.


## 2026-07-27 — First cycle: no post-launch data yet, ship Dataset/FAQ schema and 8 keywords
*6am review*

**Observed.** This review ran ~3.5 hours after the engine went live (setup committed 2026-07-26 20:29 ET). The droplet's own crons (05:40 UTC build, 06:00 ET measure) had not fired even once by the time this review ran (checked: 2026-07-27 00:32 UTC, before either scheduled time), so results.jsonl still ends at 2026-07-25 (14 visitors/day baseline, 3 organic) and state.json has no 'lastmod' key at all -- meaning no real (non-dry) build has ever executed. All 5 active techniques (T001-T005, T008) show 'activated: 2026-07-26' with zero days of post-activation owned_visitors data.

**Concluded.** There is nothing to judge yet -- not 'too early' in the usual 21-day-grace-period sense, but literally zero completed cycles. Nothing in the 07-24/07-25 numbers reflects the new techniques at all; that window is pure pre-launch baseline. The one actionable thing this absence itself proves: if next review still shows no lastmod state and no fresh results.jsonl rows, the droplet cron is not actually running (wrong crontab, deploy-key auth failure, or growth.env missing) and needs the owner's hands-on check -- I have no SSH/droplet access to diagnose it myself.

**Changed.** Two additive, low-risk changes verified via py_compile + build --dry-run (and a real non-dry build in an isolated scratch copy, which confirmed valid JSON-LD output on section8 hub/borough pages): (1) Added schema.org Dataset + FAQPage JSON-LD to the /section8/ hub and per-borough pages, and Dataset JSON-LD to /brief/<date>/ pages -- deliberately no 'license' field since the site has never stated a data license and I won't assert one. This targets a real gap: 2026 GEO guidance says structured, extractable data is what AI answer engines cite, and llms.txt alone (T003) only hands over prose, not machine-typed facts about the dataset itself. (2) Added 8 real tracked keywords to growth/keywords.py SEED and regenerated keywords.json (additive-only diff, verified with git diff -- did not touch any existing entry's 'covered' status): section8 borough pages for Queens/Bronx (existing pages, previously untracked), 'voucher friendly apartments nyc', SF/LA/DC exemption and just-cause-eviction queries (real tenant search intent the guide pages don't yet title-target), and two developer-intent queries ('rent stabilization data api', 'nyc housing data api') aimed at the API's demand problem (T007). Did NOT run keywords.check_coverage() against a real docroot -- this sandbox has no built HTML pages (only raw buildings.min.json/s8.json), and I initially ran it against '.' by mistake, which wiped out real coverage data with false negatives; I reverted that and left all 'covered' fields exactly as the droplet last computed them. The next real run's check_coverage(docroot) will fill in the 8 new entries' coverage honestly.

**Watching.** (1) Whether results.jsonl gains rows past 2026-07-25 and state.json gains a 'lastmod' key by the next review -- if not, escalate the cron-not-running hypothesis to the owner directly, don't just keep noting it. (2) Once owned_visitor data exists for T001/T002, whether it's nonzero at all (even 1-2/day would be a real signal this early). (3) Whether the new Dataset/FAQPage JSON-LD validates in Google's Rich Results Test once section8 pages are live and indexed. (4) Coverage delta for the 8 new keywords once check_coverage runs against the real docroot -- 'section 8 apartments queens/bronx' should come back covered=true since those pages already exist; if not, the page title/h1 needs the borough name to match harder.


## 2026-07-26 — Correcting yesterday's review: the crons were running
*claude (setup session)*

**Observed.** The 2026-07-27 review concluded 'no real (non-dry) build has ever executed' from an absent lastmod key in state.json. That was wrong. Builds had run and deployed on 07-26 (IndexNow accepted 8 URLs, HTTP 200) and results.jsonl held a 21-day backfill. state.json is gitignored — it carries per-URL content hashes and visitor ids — so the cloud agent literally cannot see it, and read invisible as nonexistent.

**Concluded.** The review reasoned correctly from what it could see; the fault was mine, in giving it no way to distinguish 'the cron did not fire' from 'that file is not in the repo'. Left unfixed it would have escalated a false cron failure. A second, sharper flaw surfaced the same run: check_coverage() run against a bare checkout marks every query uncovered, silently replacing real coverage history with false negatives — the review did this, caught it, and reverted, but the next one might not.

**Changed.** Added growth/last_run.json, tracked in git and written by build and measure: what ran, when, and what it did, with no hashes or ids. check_coverage() now refuses to run unless the target actually looks like a built docroot. Also fixed a deploy bug this exposed — growth_run.sh existed untracked on the droplet and blocked git pull, so the review's changes did not reach production until removed; the pull-failure fallback did its job and the build still ran on the local copy.

**Watching.** That the next review reads last_run.json and does NOT repeat the cron-failure alarm. Its own predictions still stand: whether /section8/ and /brief/ earn any owned visitors, and whether the new Dataset/FAQPage JSON-LD (verified live and valid on /section8/ — 4 blocks) shows up in Rich Results.


## 2026-07-27 — Found the real reason last_run.json never arrived: growth_run.sh's git add list missed it; ship a rendered FAQ and 8 keywords
*6am review*

**Observed.** One full day of post-activation data exists: 2026-07-26 (activation day) had 11 visitors, 1 organic, 0 AI — below the pre-launch baseline of ~14/day, ~3 organic. fresh_section8 and daily_brief show owned_visitors=0 for 07-26, the only post-activation row. That is one noisy day, not a trend. growth/last_run.json — the ground-truth file the 2026-07-26 correction commit (e38e539) added specifically so this review can tell a real cron failure from an invisible gitignored file — was itself absent from this checkout. Root cause found by reading growth_run.sh: write_last_run() writes it correctly on the droplet, but the nightly commit line only runs 'git add -A growth/techniques.json growth/keywords.json growth/results.jsonl growth/journal.md' — last_run.json was never added to that list, so it is generated every night and never pushed. Separately, the /section8/ hub page emits FAQPage JSON-LD (SECTION8_FAQ, 2 Q&A pairs) but never rendered that text anywhere in the visible HTML body — schema describing content that does not exist on the page.

**Concluded.** The cron-failure question from the last two entries is still genuinely unresolved by data, but for a newly-diagnosed reason: the messenger was broken, not the signal. This was not a repeat of the 07-26 false alarm (that one misread an invisible gitignored file as absent; this one is a real, verifiable bug in a tracked file, confirmed by reading growth_run.sh directly). It is too early to judge T001/T002/T004/T005 on one day of data regardless. The hidden-FAQ gap is a real, if modest, GEO/schema-compliance defect: AI answer engines extract from rendered text far more reliably than JSON-LD, and Google's own guidance expects structured data to reflect visible content.

**Changed.** (1) Fixed growth_run.sh: added growth/last_run.json to the nightly git add list, so tonight's build/measure run should finally push it and next review can read real droplet ground truth instead of inferring from its absence. (2) Rendered SECTION8_FAQ as visible HTML (new _faq_html() in techniques.py, styled via .faq-item) on the /section8/ hub page, directly beneath the existing content — the FAQPage JSON-LD now describes real on-page text instead of standing alone. Verified via a real (non-dry) build into an isolated scratch docroot: 2 faq-item blocks rendered with the exact question/answer text, all 4 JSON-LD blocks (BreadcrumbList, ItemList, Dataset, FAQPage) still parse. (3) Added 8 additive-only tracked keywords to keywords.py SEED: NYC rent-guidelines-board and lease-renewal queries (timely — RGB sets its annual allowable increase around this time of year), 'how much can rent go up' variants for SF/LA/DC to match the pattern the NYC list already had but the other three cities lacked, and one more developer-intent query ('affordable housing dataset api') for T007's demand problem. Verified via git diff that no existing keywords.json entry's covered/position/etc. fields were touched — did not run check_coverage() against this checkout.

**Watching.** (1) Whether growth/last_run.json actually appears in the repo after tonight's droplet run — if it still doesn't, the bug diagnosis here was wrong and needs revisiting, not the git-add fix repeated. (2) Whether 07-27 and 07-28 owned_visitors for fresh_section8/daily_brief stay at 0 or turn nonzero — two data points is still not a trend, but a third and fourth zero should start to weigh against the hypothesis. (3) Whether the visible FAQ block shows any measurable effect on ai_visitors (T003's metric) — unlikely to be visible in days, but worth a first look after ~2 weeks. (4) Coverage numbers for the 8 new keywords once a real check_coverage(docroot) runs on the droplet.

## 2026-07-27 — Anthropic key installed; scout runs; three bugs it exposed
*claude (setup session)*

**Observed.** Key installed to keychain and the droplet. First API call returned 'credit balance is too low' (a real 400 at that moment); minutes later calls succeeded, so credits landed or propagated. First real scout run then failed parsing its own output — web-search answers overran max_tokens=4000 and truncated the JSON mid-object. Second run proposed 3 techniques (T015 Dataset schema on all 47k building pages, T016 embeddable address-lookup widget for reporters/tenant orgs, T017 one-time paid Building Report) and 23 keywords.

**Concluded.** T017 is the sharpest finding so far and reframes the revenue problem: most visitors need this data exactly once, before signing a specific lease, so a $4.99/mo recurring ask is wrong at the highest-intent moment. That is a better explanation for zero paid conversions than 'not enough traffic'. Separately, 8 of the 23 proposed keywords were paraphrases of tracked ones ('...rent controlled sf' vs '...san francisco') because the scout prompt listed existing techniques but never existing keywords — padding the universe with restatements would make the 90%-share denominator meaningless.

**Changed.** max_tokens 4000→8000 and extract_json now salvages truncated JSON by walking back to the last balanced position, dropping the partial entry rather than guessing it. Anthropic errors now carry the API's own message instead of a bare 'HTTP 400', and the daily report gained a RESEARCH JOBS BLOCKED section so a job that is merely out of credit no longer looks like one with nothing to say. Scout prompt now lists tracked queries. Added a fingerprint dedup to keywords.add(). NOTE: my first dedup attempt was too aggressive — it treated 'lookup'/'list'/'for rent' as noise and destructively dropped 10 entries including 'rent stabilized apartments for rent', a listings query merged into an explainer. Restored from git and narrowed the noise list to genuinely contentless words; only 1 real paraphrase now dedupes. Universe: 68 queries, 50% covered.

**Watching.** Whether tomorrow's 6am review proposes something overlapping T015 (the cloud agent already shipped Dataset/FAQPage markup on /section8/ — the scout does not yet know what the review agent built). Whether the salvage path ever fires again now that max_tokens is 8000. Whether keyword coverage moves off 50% as the review agent targets uncovered queries.


## 2026-07-27 — Search Console wired: the 90% goal now measures 0.0%, and indexing is the proven bottleneck
*claude (setup session)*

**Observed.** GSC live as of today. Last 7 days: 8 clicks, 27 impressions, avg position 13.7. Of ~47,600 published pages, 89 earned a single impression (0.19%). Of the 74 tracked rent-stabilization queries across NYC/SF/LA/DC, ZERO rank at all — not one in the top 10, not one in the top 100 with impressions. The only clicks the site gets are for 'jayshomefinder' / 'jays home finder' (7 of 10 in the wider window), which is the DEAD BRAND of the previous site on this domain, not rent-stabilization intent.

**Concluded.** The coverage proxy was flattering us badly: it read 89.5%, actual top-10 share is 0.0%. Coverage measures whether we wrote a page, not whether anyone finds it. The engine's founding hypothesis — indexing, not content volume — is now confirmed with a number rather than inferred: 47.6k pages, 89 serving. No amount of new content matters until that ratio moves. Also worth noting the site's entire measurable search identity is currently a decommissioned brand, so there is no organic rent-stabilization presence to build on yet — we are starting from zero, not from a low base.

**Changed.** Added growth/searchconsole.py: pulls positions for every tracked query, serving-page count, and page-2 opportunities into the ledger daily (window ends 2 days back — GSC lags, and asking for today returns a partial day that reads as a cliff). Wired into the measure step and the daily report, which now shows real share, indexing ratio and search traffic instead of the coverage proxy. Key deployed to the droplet at growth/.gsc_key.json (600, gitignored) with SC_KEY_FILE in growth.env.

**Watching.** gsc_serving_pages is now THE number to watch — if daily IndexNow and the daily sitemap are working, it should climb from 89. If it is flat in two weeks, indexing needs a different attack (internal linking depth, or the 47k pages may be judged thin/duplicate by Google). Also watch whether any tracked query ever appears: the first non-zero tracked_ranking is the first real evidence the SEO corpus can rank at all.


## 2026-07-27 — Lifecycle email built — aimed at buyers, not free accounts
*claude (setup session)*

**Observed.** T011 had sat as a candidate all session while everything else shipped. The original scope (onboarding + re-engagement for registered users) targets 3 accounts with saves out of ~19 registered — too small for the 21-day review to ever find a signal, so it would have been retired for lack of evidence rather than lack of merit.

**Concluded.** Re-scoped to Building Report buyers. They paid at the highest-intent moment in the funnel, they hand over an email via Stripe, and they each have one specific building they care about — a base that grows with revenue instead of ahead of it. Two steps only, both genuinely useful: day 3 nudges the free DHCR rent-history request (the one action that establishes overcharge, and the easiest to put off), day 14 asks what happened and offers saved-building alerts.

**Changed.** growth/lifecycle.py + migration 0003 (sent_steps, unsub_token, unsubscribed_at) + /reports/unsubscribe endpoint, wired into the daily pass and gated on the ledger. Verified live: both steps sent, step recorded so a re-run advances rather than resends, unsubscribe works on GET and one-click POST and excludes the buyer afterwards, unknown tokens return the identical 200 so the endpoint is not an enumeration oracle over buyer records. Found and fixed a silent-skip bug: PostgREST returns '+00' offsets, which datetime.fromisoformat rejects before Python 3.11 — a bare except turned that into 'nothing due', which is indistinguishable from working. The droplet runs 3.12 and would have masked it entirely.

**Watching.** First real buyer through the sequence — whether day3 gets opened at all, and whether anyone replies to day14 (it invites a reply, which is the cheapest qualitative signal available). Also whether sent_steps stays consistent: the write happens after the send, so a failure there means a duplicate tomorrow, and one duplicate is the acceptable side of that trade.


## 2026-07-27 — Free-account onboarding, one email design, and scheduled revisits
*claude (setup session)*

**Observed.** Supabase auth runs mailer_autoconfirm with no SMTP host, so a new account gets no confirmation and no welcome — signup is followed by silence. 17 of 20 accounts have never saved a building; week-over-week retention is 6%. Meanwhile three senders existed with two different looks: notify_saved_listings.py had a house style, the buyer sequence and the report delivery were unstyled plain text.

**Concluded.** The free-account audience is small enough that I argued against it earlier on measurement grounds, and that reasoning still holds — but 'a new user hears nothing at all' is a product defect independent of whether a drip campaign moves a metric, so it was worth fixing on those terms. The sharper risk was scope: gating only the WELCOME on account age would still have fired day-3 and day-21 at 17 cold accounts the first night, which is exactly the backfill blast the owner ruled out. A hard cutover date, not just per-step age gates, is what makes 'new signups only' actually true.

**Changed.** growth/emailkit.py (shared renderer: inline CSS since Gmail strips <style>, no images, real plain-text alternative, List-Unsubscribe) with the buyer sequence and report delivery retrofitted. growth/accounts.py + migration 0004: welcome / activate / lapsed, behaviour-gated, hard CUTOVER 2026-07-27. Separate lifecycle opt-out so stopping onboarding mail doesn't cost someone their saved-building alerts. revisit_on in the ledger (30 days), surfaced in the report and given a step in the 6am agent prompt. SECURITY: lifecycle_accounts_due() was briefly callable with the browser anon key and returned every user's email — Supabase default privileges grant EXECUTE to anon/authenticated explicitly, so 'revoke from public' removed nothing. Found by testing with the anon key rather than trusting the migration.

**Watching.** First real signup through the sequence — does welcome get opened, and does anyone come back within 7 days. The revisit dates land 2026-08-25/26: that is the first real test of whether the revisit mechanism produces a decision or just a printed line. Also whether any of the 20 pre-cutover accounts ever receives anything (they must not).


## 2026-07-28 — Fixed two mis-flagged 'candidate' techniques already live in prod; added FAQ content/schema to hub pages after AEO research
*6am review*

**Observed.** No technique has a revisit_on due today (earliest is 2026-08-25). gsc_serving_pages has exactly one data point (89/47,600 = 0.19%, measured 2026-07-27) -- one day is not a trend. growth/last_run.json still shows only 2026-07-27 timestamps as of this review at 2026-07-28 10:03 UTC, past both stated cron times (05:40 UTC and ~09:00 UTC for 05:00 ET) -- not yet 'several days' stale per the standing instruction, so not raising a cron-failure alarm, but it is a watch item. T010 (gsc_integration) and T017 (one_time_building_report_product) were both marked status=candidate in techniques.json despite being demonstrably live: gsc_* metrics have flowed into results.jsonl and last_run.json's searchconsole section since 2026-07-27, and api_server.py/lifecycle.py carry a working Stripe one-time-checkout + buyer sequence for the Building Report. The 2026-07-27 commit (09a0c20) claimed to fix 'two stale ledger records' for exactly these two but its diff never touched techniques.json.

**Concluded.** The stale-ledger claim in 09a0c20 was aspirational, not applied -- a real (if small) trust gap between commit messages and the ledger they describe, worth checking on any future commit that claims a ledger fix. Research on 2026 large-site indexing: Google's own Search Relations team frames 'Crawled - currently not indexed' as a quality/trust signal, not a technical bug, and crawl budget for a new/low-authority domain (this one is a repurposed dead domain, 'jayshomefinder') is small regardless of sitemap volume -- confirms the existing plan (T016 widget + T008/T009 outreach for backlinks/authority) is the right lever, more than adding pages. Separately, AEO research: FAQ content carries the highest individual per-page signal weight (~20%) for AI-answer-engine citation, while llms.txt itself has 'essentially no impact' on citations per multiple 2026 studies (T003 stays -- it's free and also does real work as an explicit crawler-permission signal, but its citation-boosting rationale doesn't hold up and future reviews shouldn't lean on it). The FAQ finding pointed at a real, honest gap: only individual building pages carried a FAQPage (one Q&A each); every neighborhood page (~250+), all 5 borough hub pages, and every ZIP hub page -- the higher-authority tier of the internal-linking structure -- had no FAQ content or schema at all, despite already holding the real numbers (building counts, median build year) needed to answer real questions honestly.

**Changed.** Added faq_jsonld()/faq_html() helpers to build_seo.py, mirroring the pattern growth/techniques.py already used for /section8/ (schema must have a visible on-page counterpart -- learned from the 07-27 hidden-FAQ bug, not repeating it). Added 1-2 real, per-page-unique FAQ pairs (building count, and median build year where available) to every neighborhood page, all 5 borough hub pages, and every ZIP hub page. Verified with a full local 'python3 build_seo.py' run against the real buildings.min.json/listings.json checked into this repo: 47,596 pages generated; spot-checked neighborhood/borough/zip output by hand -- FAQ text renders visibly in the body and FAQPage JSON-LD parses alongside the existing BreadcrumbList. Also fixed the two stale ledger records 09a0c20 claimed but didn't deliver: T010 and T017 flipped candidate to active in techniques.json, revisit_on set to 2026-08-27, with a why-note on each explaining what confirmed they were live and that the earlier commit's claim never touched this file.

**Watching.** Whether gsc_serving_pages moves off 89 over the next 1-2 weeks -- FAQ content on hub pages is unlikely to move it alone (the real unlock is domain authority via T016/outreach), but it's cheap and stacks with that work; a first real look makes sense once there are ~2 weeks of post-change data. Whether growth/last_run.json shows a 2026-07-28+ timestamp on the next review -- if it's still stuck on 07-27 after another full day, that crosses into a genuine cron-failure concern. Whether T010/T017 stay 'active' after tonight's droplet run rather than getting silently reverted by a code path that doesn't know about today's ledger edit.


## 2026-07-29 — Guides for SF/LA/DC — the site had none, and 11 map-targeted queries were being scored as covered anyway
*6am review*

**Observed.** Both droplet crons fired today (last_run.json: measure 2026-07-29T05:00:06Z, build 05:40:07Z), which resolves the last entry's cron watch item. Scout also ran clean at 05:04Z, adding T020/T021/T022 and 23 keywords (universe 74 -> 97) -- so the 'API usage cap until 2026-08-01' in the standing brief is NOT currently blocking that job; I could not read scout_last (state.json is gitignored) but commit 2afd88e is direct evidence it succeeded. THE GATING NUMBER MOVED THE WRONG WAY: gsc_serving_pages 89 -> 77 (windows 07-18..07-25 -> 07-20..07-27), clicks 8 -> 6, impressions 27 -> 25, avg position 13.7 -> 14.2. Two data points, but after three days of daily IndexNow, daily sitemap, FAQ blocks and hub answer blocks, the count of pages earning any impression fell 13%. Traffic: 12 visitors / 2 organic on 07-28, 11 / 1 on 07-26, against a pre-ad-flight baseline of ~11-18 (the 07-17/18 spike of 288/279 is the paused paid campaign and is excluded). Revenue /bin/bash, 0 paying subs, 7 comped, 3 accounts with saves, 3 API keys. owned_visitors is 0 for fresh_section8 and daily_brief on every measured day since activation (07-25, 07-26, 07-28 -- four rows, all zero) and 0 for hub_direct_answers on its first day; only city_seo_expansion shows 1. One measurement day is missing entirely: results.jsonl has no visitors row for 2026-07-27 and last_run measure jumps 07-27 -> 07-29, so the 07-28 measure never ran or never pushed. Checked the last entry's claims against the repo: T010 and T017 are genuinely status=active in techniques.json, and last_run.json shows hub_direct_answers at 368/368 hub pages -- both claims hold, unlike commit 09a0c20's.

**Concluded.** Two things are not working and one thing was measuring itself dishonestly. (1) Not working: the indexing push. Adding pages, pinging IndexNow daily and thickening hub pages has not moved gsc_serving_pages up; it went down. I am not calling 77 a trend on two points, but the standing hypothesis 'daily freshness signals will get the corpus indexed' now has three days of evidence against it and none for it. (2) Not working: fresh_section8 and daily_brief, four measured days at zero owned visitors each. They are inside the 21-day grace period (retirement eligible ~08-16) so I am not retiring them, but a fifth and sixth zero should be treated as a verdict, not as 'too early'. (3) Dishonest measurement, found today: coverage was counting the SF/LA/DC city map pages as answers to queries like 'sf rent control exemptions' and 'los angeles rent control rules'. Those three pages are the same single-page-app shell with a different <title> -- their visible text is NYC borough filters, HPD violation controls and 'Every NYC landlord must accept vouchers by law'. 11 of 13 such queries were marked covered=true. So the site had SIX guide pages, all New York, while three of its four cities and 42 of its 97 tracked queries had no explanatory page at all, and the coverage metric hid that. This is the same failure the Search Console wiring exposed in July (coverage said 89.5%, real top-10 share was 0.0%): coverage measures whether a page exists, not whether it answers. Separately, a second invisible-file bug of exactly the 07-27 last_run.json kind: growth/gsc_pages.json has been written every night since commit 5f7f4b3 and holds which pages Search Console actually serves plus up to 60 queries we earn impressions for without tracking -- and growth_run.sh never added it to the nightly commit, so no review has ever seen it. That file is the direct answer to 'which 77 pages', which is the question that should drive the next several runs.

**Changed.** Shipped the first non-NYC editorial content on the site: three cornerstone guides in seo_guides.py, one per uncovered city -- /guide/is-my-apartment-rent-controlled-san-francisco/ (750 words: the June 13 1979 certificate-of-occupancy rule, Costa-Hawkins exemptions and vacancy decontrol, just-cause reaching further than price control, AB 1482), .../-los-angeles/ (672 words: the October 1 1978 RSO rule, City vs County, LAHD registration, AB 1482 and the Just Cause Ordinance) and .../-washington-dc/ (626 words: pre-1976, the four-or-fewer-units natural-person exemption, and the registration rule -- an unregistered unit is treated as covered, which is the single most useful thing a DC tenant can know). Every year-varying number (RGB order, AB 1482 CPI figure, DC CPI cap) is linked to its source rather than asserted, and each guide states this site's own limits before the map link: SF is anonymized to the block, LA is derived from assessor criteria and labelled 'likely RSO', DC reflects an official registry. Supporting changes: guides now carry a "city" field, and _related() links within a city then offers one entry point per other city (an SF reader was going to get six NYC links); the /guide/ hub is grouped by city with its h1/title naming all four. growth/keywords.py: 13 seed queries retargeted from the city maps to the guides that actually answer them, and seed() now refreshes the target of seed-sourced entries instead of skipping any query it has already seen -- previously retargeting in SEED silently did nothing -- and clears the stale coverage verdict when it does. Honest consequence: coverage fell 68.0% -> 56.7% today and will read lower again tomorrow. T023 city_guides added to the ledger (active, revisit 2026-08-28) with a verifier in techniques.py that checks the three pages exist in the docroot AND still contain their caveat text; tested both paths, it returns ok=False when the 'likely RSO' caveat is removed. growth_run.sh now commits growth/gsc_pages.json. scripts/refresh_seo.sh now does a guarded, non-fatal git pull before building, because it builds from /root/dhcr-build and nothing was pulling review-agent changes into that path. Verified: py_compile clean on all changed files, bash -n clean on both scripts, 'build --dry-run --docroot . --build-dir .' passes, and a real 'python3 build_seo.py' produced 47,599 pages (up from 47,596) with Article+BreadcrumbList JSON-LD parsing on all three guides and all three listed in sitemap-guide.xml. Did NOT run keywords.check_coverage() against anything; I simulated its matcher by hand against the built guides and all 13 retargeted queries match on title.

**Watching.** (1) THE DEPLOY PATH IS THE RISK, and it is self-diagnosing: last_run.json will carry a city_guides line every night. If it says 'missing: dc, la, sf' for more than a day or two, /root/dhcr-build is not pulling this repo and the guides never shipped -- that is a deploy problem to fix at the droplet, not a reason to rewrite the guides. (2) Whether growth/gsc_pages.json appears in this repo after tonight's run. If it does, the next review should open it FIRST and let the actual serving URLs and the discovered_untracked query list drive the work, instead of reasoning from 77 as a bare number. If it does not, this entry's git-add diagnosis is wrong and needs re-diagnosing, not repeating. (3) gsc_serving_pages: a third point. 89 -> 77 -> ? If it keeps falling, the corpus is being judged thin and the answer is fewer, better pages, not more of them. (4) Coverage should dip on 07-30 (the 05:00 measure runs before the 05:40 build and before the SEO rebuild, so the 13 retargeted queries will find no page at their new target) and recover to roughly 68% on 07-31. A dip is expected; a failure to recover means the guides are not live. (5) fresh_section8 and daily_brief owned_visitors -- two more zeros and they should be judged, not excused.


## 2026-07-30 — gsc_pages.json arrives and reframes the problem: the pages that serve DO rank — the queries have no volume. Shipped the aggregate browse tier for SF/LA/DC
*6am review*

**Observed.** growth/gsc_pages.json appeared in the repo after last night's run, resolving the 07-29 watch item, and it is the most useful file this engine has produced. Of the 82 pages earning any Search Console impression (window 07-21..07-28): 75 are individual building pages, 5 are ZIP hubs, 1 is a neighborhood hub, 1 is the homepage. 58 of the 82 sit at position 10 or better and 16 are in the top 3; median position is 8. Total impressions across all 82 pages: 131 in eight days — the 75 building pages account for 103 of them, 1.4 impressions each. The only non-brand queries visible per page are literal street addresses ('228 e 3rd st ny' pos 34.5 / 6 impr, '481 4th ave brooklyn ny' pos 37, '200 west 72' pos 63); 56 of 60 pages have their queries anonymized by GSC. Brand residual is still jayshomefinder (8+5+1 impressions), excluded per standing instruction. THE GATING NUMBER, third data point: gsc_serving_pages 89 -> 77 -> 82, so it did not keep falling, but clicks went 8 -> 6 -> 4 and impressions 27 -> 25 -> 23, both monotonically down. search_share_pct 0.0, tracked_ranking 0 of 111. Traffic 22 visitors / 8 organic on 07-29 (12/2 on 07-28, 11/1 on 07-26) against the ~11-18 pre-ad baseline. Revenue $0, 0 paying subs, 7 comped, 3 accounts with saves, 3 API keys. Both crons fired (measure 05:00:06Z, build 05:40:06Z). No technique had a revisit_on due (earliest 2026-08-25). Last entry's claims checked and they hold: gsc_pages.json is committed, so its git-add diagnosis was right. city_guides reports 'missing: dc, la, sf' — I checked this rather than assuming: a full local 'python3 build_seo.py' exits 0 and DOES write all three guides at exactly the paths the verifier checks, with their caveat text, and t_city_guides run against that output returns ok=True 3/3. So the generator is correct and the docroot is stale. For contrast, class='answer' entered build_seo.py at 07-28 20:18Z and was live on 368/368 hub pages by the 07-29 05:40Z build — nine hours; seo_guides.py's commit landed 07-29 10:18Z and is still not deployed 19 hours later.

**Concluded.** The corpus's problem is arithmetic, and I have been describing it wrongly. 'Only 82 of 47,600 pages earn an impression' reads as a quality or trust failure, and for three days the engine has been treated that way — thicken the pages, ping IndexNow, add freshness signals. The per-page data says the opposite: the pages that DO serve rank well (58 of 82 in the top 10, 16 in the top 3). Ranking ability is not the constraint. Addressable search volume is. A page whose only possible query is one street address cannot produce traffic even at position 1, because almost nobody types that address in a given week — 75 building pages earned 1.4 impressions each in eight days. Multiply by 47,000 and you still get roughly nothing. So gsc_serving_pages is a real ceiling but a misleading diagnosis: it will stay near zero for as long as the corpus is mostly address pages, no matter how good they get, and no amount of depth on a per-address page changes the volume of the query it targets. The tier that can work is the one that aggregates, and the evidence for that is in the same file: 5 ZIP hubs at positions 2, 2, 3.5, 4 and 11, and a neighborhood hub at position 2. Small counts, but the only pages on this site ranking top-5 for something other than a decommissioned brand name. And only 6 of 368 hub pages serve at all, while three of the four cities in the dataset had no aggregate tier whatsoever — /sf/, /la/ and /dc/ were one JavaScript map shell each, which is also why 11 city queries were scored 'covered' by a page that cannot answer them (found 07-29). That is the gap worth filling, and it is not the same move as 'add more pages': it is extending the only tier with demonstrated ranking ability to where it does not exist. Not working, stated plainly: fresh_section8 and daily_brief are at 0 owned_visitors on every measured day since activation (07-25, 07-26, 07-28, 07-29 — four zeros each). No new measured day landed today, so the count is still four and I am not calling it, but the next zero should be treated as the verdict rather than as 'too early' for a third consecutive review. b2b_api_licensing: 24 straight days at zero. hub_direct_answers: 0 on both its days, genuinely too early (activated 07-28). city_guides: 0 on its one day, and the pages are not even live. Also worth recording for a future review: the daily report's own 'pages with headroom' panel now lists 9 individual building pages at positions 10.7-13 with 1-3 impressions each. That panel is telling us to rewrite pages whose queries have no volume — it is the same misread, built into the report.

**Changed.** Shipped the aggregate browse tier for the three non-NYC cities, activating T013 (city_seo_expansion) which has sat as a candidate since 2026-07-26. build_seo.py:city_hub_pages() generates 252 place pages plus a browse hub per city — 37 SF neighborhood hubs, 112 LA ZIP hubs, 103 DC neighborhood hubs, /sf|la|dc/buildings/ — total 47,599 -> 47,854 pages, each city in its own sitemap shard (sitemap-sf/la/dc.xml). Grouped on the dimension each city's data actually carries: SF and DC records hold a neighborhood, LA records hold only a ZIP. Every page carries only stats its city holds — LA has no reported rents, DC has no build years — plus a unit-size distribution (SF, DC) or decade-built distribution (LA), and no place with fewer than 5 records gets a page (the browse hub states how many were left off rather than hiding it). Deliberately NOT building-level: SF is anonymized to the block and LA is a derived 'likely RSO' list, so the aggregate is the honest unit of publication for those two. Accuracy work that came out of it: (a) page() now takes a per-city provenance footer, because every non-NYC page was footed 'Data: NYC DHCR 2024... NYC PLUTO... NYC HPD' — which also means the three city guides shipped on 07-29 were claiming NYC sources, now fixed; (b) LA wording says 'a build year before 1979', not 'built before 1 October 1978', because the assessor records carry a year and not a date, and the pages send the reader to LAHD/ZIMAS for the actual certificate-of-occupancy test rather than settling it; (c) the SF FAQ states the pre-1979 build-year share but says explicitly that the legal test is the certificate-of-occupancy date, not the assessor year; (d) DC address labels stop at the first comma, so the pages publish the property, not 'Ste 501'; (e) _city_labels() dedupes — SF repeats a block-side once per reported building — and sorts by street then house number, since a string sort files every '0 Block of...' first. No Dataset schema.org markup: still no stated license, holding the 07-28 standard. Internal linking, because the research is blunt that a page with no internal links is invisible: the new hubs are reachable from /buildings/ (priority 0.9), from each city's guide, from each city's SPA shell nav (build_city_pages.py), from their own browse hub, and from up to 12 sibling hubs each. growth/techniques.py: t_city_seo_expansion verifies the tier in the live docroot and deliberately distinguishes the two failure modes — all cities empty while /guide/ exists means the SEO rebuild has not deployed, some cities empty means the generator broke. Tested both branches by hand plus the ok=True path against real output (255 pages). growth/keywords.py: 8 new tracked queries at the neighborhood/ZIP level for SF/LA/DC (none a paraphrase of a tracked query — the universe had zero non-NYC aggregate queries), and 6 'list' queries retargeted off the map shells onto /<city>/buildings/, for exactly the reason the explain/check queries were retargeted on 07-29. Universe 103 -> 111. Verified: py_compile clean on all changed files; 'build --dry-run --docroot . --build-dir .' exits 0; a real build_seo.py run exits 0 at 47,854 pages with 0 bad JSON-LD anywhere and only 1 pre-existing page rewritten, so the honest-lastmod property from 5f7f4b3 survives; the report renders both parts. Did NOT run keywords.check_coverage().

**Watching.** (1) THE DEPLOY PATH IS NOW THE WHOLE STORY AND IT IS SELF-DIAGNOSING. Two techniques report it every night: city_guides and the new city_seo_expansion. build_seo.py demonstrably generates both correctly here, so if tomorrow's last_run.json still says '0 of 3 city guides' and 'docroot has no city hub pages at all', the SEO rebuild has not deployed since 2026-07-29 morning and that is a droplet-side fix (/root/dhcr-build not pulling, or refresh_seo.sh not firing) — not a reason to rewrite any of this content. Note the asymmetry to explain: a build_seo.py change propagated in 9 hours on 07-28, a seo_guides.py change has not in 19. If it deploys, coverage should climb as the 14 retargeted/new queries find real pages; if it does not, coverage will fall and that fall is a deploy signal, not a content signal. (2) Whether any /sf/, /la/ or /dc/ hub appears in gsc_pages.json at all within ~2-3 weeks, and at what position. The prediction being tested is specific: aggregate pages in these cities should behave like NYC's ZIP hubs (positions 2-5) rather than like its building pages, because the query has volume. If they land at position 2-5 with real impressions, the aggregate tier is the lever and the next several runs should build it out — LA has no neighborhood tier at all, and NYC's own 165 ZIP hubs are barely serving. If they rank as poorly as the tracked head terms, then this domain's authority is the binding constraint and content of any shape is the wrong lever. (3) gsc_serving_pages, fourth point: 89 -> 77 -> 82 -> ? and clicks 8 -> 6 -> 4 -> ?. The clicks trend is the one I would watch harder; three consecutive declines is not noise for much longer. (4) fresh_section8 and daily_brief: the next measured day makes five zeros. Retire or change them then — that decision has now been deferred by three reviews. (5) Whether the report's 'pages with headroom' panel should stop ranking individual building pages, given they are 9 of its 10 rows and rewriting them cannot pay off.


## 2026-07-31 — The retirement rule is measured in a unit this site cannot produce: swapped it to owned search impressions before it retires everything on 2026-08-16
*6am review*

**Observed.** Both crons fired (measure 2026-07-31T05:00:06Z, build 05:40:07Z). THE GATING NUMBER, fourth point: gsc_serving_pages 89 -> 77 -> 82 -> 82 (window 07-22..07-29). Clicks 8 -> 6 -> 4 -> 2 and impressions 27 -> 25 -> 23 -> 21: four consecutive declines in each, which is no longer arguably noise. search_share_pct 0.0, tracked_ranking 0 of 136. Traffic 7 visitors / 2 organic on 07-30, against 22/8 on 07-29 and a ~11-18 pre-ad baseline. Revenue $0, 0 paying subs, 3 accounts with saves, 3 API keys. No technique has a revisit_on due (earliest 2026-08-25), so step 3 was a no-op. gsc_pages.json composition shifted slightly: 77 building pages, 4 ZIP hubs, 1 homepage, and the one neighborhood hub that served on 07-29 dropped out; the homepage still carries 20 of 136 impressions and both of the week's clicks are brand ('jayshomefinder'). CORRECTION TO THE STANDING BRIEF: the Anthropic usage cap is not currently blocking anything. The scout ran clean at 05:05Z today, adding 25 keywords (111 -> 136) and three candidates T027-T029; T027 'index triage: promote 3k buildings, consolidate the rest' is the scout independently reaching the 07-30 review's conclusion. Last entry's claims checked and they hold: build_seo.py:city_hub_pages() and t_city_seo_expansion are in the repo, keywords.py carries the 8 new non-NYC queries. And its central prediction came true: the 07-31 build still reports '0 of 3 city guides live' and 'docroot has no city hub pages at all'. Everything the 07-29 and 07-30 runs shipped is still not live, two full days later, while the daily growth build deploys into the same docroot every night without trouble. I also measured the new instrument against the real committed gsc_pages.json before shipping it: fresh_section8 0 pages serving, daily_brief 0, city_guides 0, city_seo_expansion 0, hub_direct_answers 4 pages / 4 impressions / best position 1.

**Concluded.** The review loop was about to retire the whole content programme for the wrong reason. review.py retires a prefix-owning technique after 21 days on 'fewer than 20 owned visitors and a trailing median under 1'. This site produced 2 organic clicks in the last measured week. No technique can clear 20 visitors, so on 2026-08-16, when the grace period ends for T001/T002 and then the rest, the loop would have retired every content technique and written 'did not work' verdicts into the permanent ledger — not because they failed but because the yardstick has no resolution at this traffic level. Three consecutive reviews have deferred the fresh_section8 / daily_brief decision precisely because a fifth zero and a first zero look identical in that unit. Research backs the fix rather than being decorative: 2026 SEO measurement guidance is consistent that impressions lead clicks by weeks and are the instrument for a site that is building visibility but has no click volume yet, which is exactly this case. The distinction that matters is now measurable: a technique whose pages Google serves is early, and a technique whose pages earn no impressions at all is invisible. Applied to today's data that reads bluntly and not in my favour: fresh_section8, six days live and rebuilt nightly, has 0 of its 6 pages serving — zero impressions, not zero clicks, so 'section 8 apartments nyc' is not a query we are being shown for at all. daily_brief the same. hub_direct_answers is the only prefix technique with any search visibility (4 pages, best position 1). So the honest read on the two techniques I was told to decide today is that they are invisible rather than un-clicked — but the instrument that says so started recording tonight, and retiring on its first reading would repeat the mistake I am fixing. The deploy path is the other thing not working and it is now the larger of the two problems: two runs' worth of shipped content (three city guides, 255 aggregate hub pages, the entire non-NYC tier the 07-30 thesis rests on) has been sitting in git undeployed since 2026-07-29 10:18Z. That is not something this agent can fix from a checkout — refresh_seo.sh already pulls from git, so the failure is that the job itself is not running, or is failing before its rsync, on the droplet.

**Changed.** growth/searchconsole.py: owned_visibility() intersects each technique's declared URL prefixes with the full serving-page list from Search Console, and record_owned_visibility() writes gsc_owned_pages / gsc_owned_impressions / gsc_owned_clicks / gsc_owned_best_position into the ledger every night — recorded even when all zero, because 'no impressions' is the finding and a missing row would read as 'not measured'. recent_visibility() reads it back, taking the best of the last 7 readings so a retirement never turns on one flaky pull. growth/review.py: a prefix technique that misses the visitor bar is no longer retired outright — if its pages earned >= 5 impressions it is FLAGGED 'served but not clicked, rewrite the titles', if they earned fewer it is retired with the impressions cited, and if visibility has never been measured it is kept with that stated. Flags now reach the daily report's actions list instead of being computed and discarded. growth/report.py: the per-technique table's sub-line carries pages serving / impressions / best position, in the sub-line rather than a fourth column because four columns do not fit a phone. growth/searchconsole.py headroom(): aggregate pages now sort ahead of single-address building pages, and the report says how many rows are address pages and why a rewrite cannot add volume there — the panel was telling the owner to rewrite pages whose only query is a street address, which the 07-30 entry flagged. growth/techniques.py: _seo_corpus_age() reads the newest mtime of the SEO-built sitemaps and /guide/ (rsync -a preserves mtimes, so that is when the separate pipeline last wrote), and the city_guides / city_seo_expansion failure details now name the date — 'the docroot SEO corpus was last written YYYY-MM-DD (Nd ago)' — so a stalled deploy is a dated fact in the nightly email instead of an inference three reviews have had to redo. Verified: py_compile clean; build --dry-run --docroot . --build-dir . exits 0; owned_visibility run against the real committed gsc_pages.json; all three review branches (never measured / invisible / served-but-unclicked) exercised with stubbed series; the staleness helper tested against a fake docroot including the case where nothing is there and it correctly says nothing; report renders both parts. Did NOT run keywords.check_coverage().

**Watching.** (1) The first real readings of gsc_owned_* land tomorrow. Expect fresh_section8 and daily_brief at 0 pages serving and hub_direct_answers around 4. If fresh_section8 is still at zero impressions in a week, retire it on that evidence — it is the strongest-volume query set on the site and being invisible for it is a verdict, whereas 'zero visitors' never was. (2) THE DEPLOY. If tomorrow's build still says 0 of 3 city guides, the SEO rebuild will have been dead for three days and nothing any review ships to build_seo.py or seo_guides.py matters until someone restarts it on the droplet. The new detail line will now say how stale, which is the number to put in the alert. (3) gsc_clicks 8 -> 6 -> 4 -> 2: a fifth decline takes it to zero and both remaining clicks are brand terms, so watch whether non-brand clicks are simply gone. (4) Whether any /sf/, /la/ or /dc/ hub ever appears in gsc_pages.json — that test cannot start until the deploy does. (5) The scout is running again; T027 index_triage_building_pages is the candidate most aligned with the measured evidence and is worth a real read next run.


## 2026-08-01 — Both instruments shipped yesterday read false-positive: the deploy-staleness clock was measuring our own nightly write, and city_seo_expansion was claiming the pre-existing SPA shells
*6am review*

**Observed.** Crons fired (measure 2026-08-01T05:00:05Z, build 05:40:06Z, 6 changed URLs deployed). THE GATING NUMBER, fifth point: gsc_serving_pages 89 -> 77 -> 82 -> 82 -> 78 (window 07-23..07-30). Impressions 27 -> 25 -> 23 -> 21 -> 32 and clicks 8 -> 6 -> 4 -> 2 -> 7, so the four-decline run I flagged yesterday broke — but it is not a recovery. The query rows in gsc_pages.json sum to exactly 32 impressions and exactly 7 clicks, and every one of those 7 clicks is the decommissioned brand: jayshomefinder 4, jays home finder 1, jays homefinder 1, jayshomefinder.com 1. All 142 tracked rent-stabilization queries earned ZERO impressions this week. search_share_pct 0.0, tracked_ranking 0 of 142, coverage 59.9%. Traffic 9 visitors / 2 organic on 07-31 against a 12/day median. Revenue $0, 0 paying subs, 3 accounts, 3 API keys. No revisit_on is due (earliest 2026-08-25), so step 3 was again a no-op. The scout is running clean — the usage cap lifted as scheduled at 00:00 UTC today; the 05:04:36Z ledger commit carries three new candidates (T030 tenant-submitted layer, T031 LL86 notice verifier, T032 sponsor slots) and 6 new keywords, 136 -> 142. First real readings of the gsc_owned_* instrument I shipped yesterday: hub_direct_answers 5 pages / 6 impressions / best position 1.5; fresh_section8 0/0; daily_brief 0/0; city_guides 0/0; listings_freshness 0/0; b2b_api_licensing 0/0; and city_seo_expansion 3 pages / 3 impressions / best position 8 — which is wrong, see below.

**Concluded.** I have to correct yesterday's entry twice, and both corrections are the same mistake: an instrument that reports success when it has measured nothing. (1) THE DEPLOY IS STILL DEAD AND MY CLOCK SAID OTHERWISE. Today's build reported 'the docroot SEO corpus was last written 2026-08-01 (0d ago)' while simultaneously reporting 0 of 3 city guides and no city hub pages at all. That is not the SEO pipeline running with stale code, which is what the 0d reading invited me to conclude — it is _seo_corpus_age() globbing sitemap*.xml and excluding only sitemap-daily.xml, which leaves sitemap.xml in the stamp set. t_sitemap_daily rewrites sitemap.xml every single night to refresh the daily shard's lastmod, so the newest mtime it finds is always our own write from forty minutes earlier. The clock reads 0d ago every day forever, including on a day the SEO pipeline is three days dead. So yesterday's prediction stands unrefuted, not refuted: nothing built by build_seo.py or seo_guides.py has deployed since 2026-07-29 10:18Z, and three runs' worth of shipped content — three city guides, 255 aggregate hub pages, the entire non-NYC tier the 07-30 thesis rests on — is still sitting in git. (2) CITY_SEO_EXPANSION IS CLAIMING PAGES IT DID NOT BUILD. Its declared prefixes were /sf/, /la/ and /dc/, which are the pre-existing SPA map shells written by build_city_pages.py and listed in sitemap-main.xml long before this technique existed. The three 'owned' serving pages are literally https://findacrib.com/sf/, /la/ and /dc/. So a technique with zero pages live in the docroot read as served-and-ranking-at-8, which under the retirement rule I wrote yesterday is the difference between 'early' and 'invisible' — the exact verdict that rule exists to decide. Corrected, city_seo_expansion reads 0/0, which is the truth: you cannot earn an impression for a page that was never deployed. What survives all this is that hub_direct_answers is still the only technique with real search visibility (5 pages, 6 impressions, best position 1.5) and fresh_section8 and daily_brief are still at zero impressions on their second reading. Research (2026 material on index consolidation, crawl budget and AEO citation) turned up nothing this site is not already doing or has not already queued as T027 — direct answers first-40-words, FAQ/Article schema and original-data differentiation are all shipped or in the ledger — so I did not let it pull the run away from the defect in front of me. Worth noting for whoever reads this next: /sf/, /la/ and /dc/ do each earn 1 impression at position 8, which is weak evidence for the city-tier thesis, but it belongs to the map shells and to no technique.

**Changed.** growth/techniques.py: added GROWTH_OWNED_SITEMAPS = {sitemap.xml, sitemap-daily.xml}, documented next to t_sitemap_daily which writes both, and _seo_corpus_age() now excludes that set by basename instead of excluding one filename by suffix. Its docstring records the failure so it is not re-introduced, and any future growth-written file in the docroot root has to be added there. Tested against three fake docroots: the real droplet shape (growth sitemaps stamped today, SEO shards stamped three days ago) now answers '2026-07-29 (3d ago)' instead of 0d; a docroot with only growth-written sitemaps answers None so _stale_note() says nothing rather than lying; a genuinely fresh corpus still answers 0d. growth/seed.py + growth/techniques.json: city_seo_expansion's prefixes are now the tier it actually generates — /sf/neighborhood/, /sf/buildings/, /la/zip/, /la/buildings/, /dc/neighborhood/, /dc/buildings/ — with a comment saying why the bare city roots are not ours. Re-ran owned_visibility() against the real committed gsc_pages.json: city_seo_expansion drops to 0 pages / 0 impressions, hub_direct_answers unchanged at 5/6/1.5, nothing else moves. No collision with hub_direct_answers' NYC /zip/ prefix, since /la/zip/ does not start with it. I deliberately did NOT rewrite the wrong 2026-08-01 gsc_owned_* rows already in results.jsonl — that file is an append-only measurement log and editing it would be the worse sin — so a reader who sees city_seo_expansion at 3 impressions/position 8 on 2026-08-01 should read this entry: it is the SPA shells, and review.recent_visibility() takes the best of the last 7 readings so it will age out by 2026-08-08, well inside the technique's grace period (revisit 2026-08-29). Verified: py_compile clean; build --dry-run --docroot . --build-dir . exits 0; report renders both parts. Did NOT run keywords.check_coverage().

**Watching.** (1) TOMORROW'S CORPUS DATE IS THE WHOLE STORY. The clock is honest now, so the build detail will name a real date. If it says 2026-07-29 (3d+ ago) — which is what I expect — the SEO rebuild has been dead since 07-29 and NOTHING any review ships to build_seo.py or seo_guides.py matters until someone restarts it on the droplet; that is the one thing worth an owner's attention and it is now a dated fact in the nightly email rather than an inference four reviews have each had to redo. If it instead names today, the pipeline is running with stale code and the fix is that /root/dhcr-build is not pulling. Those are different repairs, which is why the clock had to work. (2) city_seo_expansion should now read 0/0 every night until the deploy lands, and the first night any /sf/neighborhood/, /la/zip/ or /dc/neighborhood/ page earns an impression is the first honest test of the 07-30 aggregate-tier thesis. Position 2-5 confirms it; position 20+ says this domain's authority is the binding constraint and content of any shape is the wrong lever. (3) fresh_section8 and daily_brief: two consecutive readings of zero impressions. Give them a third and fourth; if the week to 2026-08-05 is still all zeros, retire fresh_section8 on that evidence — 'section 8 apartments nyc' is the highest-volume query set on this site and being invisible for it is a verdict, not a wait. That decision has now been deferred by four reviews and the instrument to make it finally exists and finally works. (4) Non-brand clicks. Seven of seven clicks this week were jayshomefinder. Check whether the tracked universe produces a single impression, let alone a click — zero tracked impressions across 142 queries is a starker number than search_share 0.0% and it should be said that way. (5) Two instruments in two days both defaulted to 'fine' when they had measured nothing. Any new check should be tested against a fixture where the thing it watches is broken, not only against one where it works.


## 2026-08-02 — The deploy clock answered — the SEO pipeline runs nightly on four-day-old code, so the growth build now publishes the stranded city guides itself
*6am review*

**Observed.** Crons fired (measure 2026-08-02T05:00:08Z, build 05:40:06Z, 6 changed URLs deployed). Scout clean at 05:04:33Z — the usage cap lifted on schedule — adding T033-T035 and 4 keywords (142 -> 146). THE GATING NUMBER, sixth point: gsc_serving_pages 89 -> 77 -> 82 -> 82 -> 78 -> 81 (window 07-24..07-31). Query-level impressions 27 -> 25 -> 23 -> 21 -> 32 -> 35 and clicks 8 -> 6 -> 4 -> 2 -> 7 -> 8, and every one of today's 8 clicks is the decommissioned brand: jayshomefinder 4, jayshomefinder.com 2, jays home finder 1, jays homefinder 1. All 146 tracked rent-stabilization queries earned ZERO impressions this week — the sixth consecutive week-window at zero. search_share_pct 0.0, tracked_ranking 0 of 146, coverage 62.3%. Page-level, gsc_pages.json holds 81 pages / 154 impressions / 14 clicks: 73 single-building pages, 3 ZIP hubs (11201 pos 11, 10024 pos 1.5, 10455 pos 4.5), 1 neighborhood hub (east-midtown-turtle-bay pos 4), the homepage (36 impressions, 11 clicks, pos 3.4), and /sf/ /la/ /dc/ at 1 impression each, position 8. Traffic 12 visitors / 4 organic on 08-01 against a 12/day median. Revenue $0, 0 paying subs, 7 comped, 3 accounts with saves, 3 API keys. No revisit_on due (earliest 2026-08-25), so step 3 was a no-op for the fourth run running. gsc_owned_* readings: hub_direct_answers 4 pages / 6 impressions / best position 1.5; city_seo_expansion 0/0 — yesterday's prefix fix worked exactly as predicted, the false 3-pages-at-position-8 reading is gone; fresh_section8 0/0 and daily_brief 0/0 for the third consecutive reading; city_guides 0/0. Yesterday's changed: line checked against the repo and it holds — GROWTH_OWNED_SITEMAPS is at techniques.py:703 and _seo_corpus_age excludes by basename at :869, and both seed.py and techniques.json carry the six real city_seo_expansion prefixes.

**Concluded.** THE CLOCK ANSWERED, AND IT IS THE OTHER BRANCH — I had the wrong repair in mind. Yesterday I predicted today's build would name 2026-07-29 (3d ago) and prove the SEO cron dead. It names 2026-08-01 (1d ago) while in the same breath reporting 0 of 3 city guides and no city hub pages at all. Both readings are true and together they say something more specific than 'the deploy is dead': refresh_seo.sh IS running every night and IS rsyncing into the docroot — what it runs is code from before 2026-07-29. /root/dhcr-build is not taking pushes. And the reason is a bootstrap deadlock I should have seen four days ago: the git-pull guard was added to scripts/refresh_seo.sh in commit 83e1595 (07-29 10:18Z) — the same commit that added the guides — but cron invokes $BUILD's own copy of that script, so the change that would make the build pull was itself delivered only through the door that does not open. No fix pushed to refresh_seo.sh can ever take effect. That reframes the standing 'git is your only channel' rule: git has two doors into production and only one opens. growth_run.sh pulls into /root/Find-A-Crib and its build deployed 6 URLs this morning; the SEO door has been shut since 07-29, and behind it sit three runs' worth of finished work — three cornerstone city guides, 255 aggregate hub pages, the entire non-NYC tier the whole 07-30 thesis rests on. Content stranded in git earns nothing, and four days is long enough to stop describing that as a blocker and route around it. Nothing else here is working either, and it should be said plainly rather than buried: six weeks-windows now with zero impressions across the tracked universe, every click brand residual, and fresh_section8 and daily_brief on their third consecutive zero-impression reading — /section8/ IS live and rebuilt nightly, so 'section 8 apartments nyc' is a query set this domain is simply not shown for. hub_direct_answers, at 6 impressions, remains the only technique with any search visibility at all. Research (2026 material on index bloat and pSEO consolidation, incl. the May 2026 core update deindexing automated pages) says the same thing this site's own data has said since 07-30 — consolidate into fewer hub pages carrying original data, do not add volume — which is already the ledger's direction (T027) and did not justify pulling the run away from the deploy defect.

**Changed.** The daily growth build now publishes the cornerstone city guides itself when the SEO pipeline has not. build_seo.py: lifted the guide-rendering block out of main() into guide_page(g, browse_ok=True) returning (canonical, html) — one renderer, because two would overwrite each other's bytes nightly and churn lastmod on a page that never changed; browse_ok=False drops the '/<city>/buildings/' link so a fallback-published guide never ships pointing at a hub that is not live. growth/techniques.py: t_city_guides went from checker to publisher-then-checker. Ownership is by marker — a docroot copy without GUIDE_FALLBACK_MARKER was written by the SEO build and is never touched, so the two pipelines cannot fight over one file, and the day /root/dhcr-build starts pulling again ours steps aside automatically. It refuses to publish a render whose city caveat is missing, and its detail names which pipeline published each page so '3 of 3 live' cannot become a self-fulfilling reading now that the technique writes the files it checks. t_sitemap_daily now lists fallback-published /guide/ URLs (changefreq monthly, priority 0.8 — they are editorial, not daily) because no sitemap shard the SEO build owns knows those pages exist; t_indexnow picks them up through ctx.new_urls. t_llms_txt names the three guides individually instead of only the /guide/ hub, gated on a new Context.live_or_staged() so it can never advertise a 404. seed.py + techniques.json: T023's notes record the fallback and the marker rule. Tested against six fixtures, including the ones where the thing being watched is broken (per 08-01's watch item 5): empty docroot -> publishes 3, marker present; day two -> rewrites, no false IndexNow ping; SEO copy present -> backs off, writes nothing; SEO copy with its caveat stripped -> still fails loudly, 2 of 3; dry run -> claims nothing and says 'would be published'; build_seo unimportable -> ok=False with the reason, build survives. Verified: py_compile clean, build --dry-run --docroot . --build-dir . exits 0, report renders both parts. Did NOT run keywords.check_coverage().

**Watching.** (1) TOMORROW'S city_guides DETAIL IS THE FALSIFIER. It should read '3 of 3 city guides live with their data caveat — 3 published by the daily growth build (dc, la, sf)'. If it still says 'missing', the growth rsync is not reaching /guide/ (permissions on the docroot subtree is the first thing to check) and this change did nothing. (2) The first honest test of T023 starts now, four days late: the SF/LA/DC 'is my apartment rent controlled' queries are tracked and have never earned an impression, and the pages that answer them are finally live. Watch city_guides' gsc_owned_pages / best_position over the next two to three weeks. Anything in the top 20 supports the 07-30 aggregate/editorial thesis; still zero impressions by 2026-08-23 says this domain's authority is the binding constraint and content of any shape is the wrong lever. (3) The 'published by the daily growth build' clause disappearing from that detail on its own is the signal that /root/dhcr-build started pulling again — city_seo_expansion should flip to ok in the same run. Until then, treat anything shipped to build_seo.py or seo_guides.py as unpublished. (4) fresh_section8 and daily_brief hit their third zero-impression reading today; 2026-08-05 is the decision date set on 07-31. If that week is still all zeros, retire fresh_section8 on that evidence — it is live, rebuilt nightly, and invisible. (5) OWNER ACTION, now precisely stated rather than inferred: a single 'git -C /root/dhcr-build pull origin main' unblocks 255 hub pages and everything future reviews ship to the SEO build. It cannot be done from a pushed commit — the script cron runs lives inside the checkout that will not update.


## 2026-08-03 — Publish the 255 stranded city hub pages through the working door, and make backing off actually back off
*6am review*

**Observed.** THE GATING NUMBER, seventh point: gsc_serving_pages 89 -> 77 -> 82 -> 82 -> 78 -> 81 -> 89 (window 2026-07-25..08-01), back to its 07-27 high. Query-level impressions 37 (up 35, 32, 21, 23) and clicks 9 (up 8, 7, 2, 4) — still brand residual, not intent. search_share_pct 0.0, tracked_ranking 0 of 146 for the seventh consecutive week-window; coverage 63.0% (nyc 65/79, sf 12/23, la 10/24, dc 5/20). Traffic 11 visitors / 1 organic on 08-02 against an 11-12/day median. Revenue $0, 0 paying subs, 3 accounts with saves, 3 API keys. Per-technique: hub_direct_answers 5 pages / 6 impressions / best position 2 — still the only technique with any search visibility at all; fresh_section8, daily_brief, city_guides, city_seo_expansion and b2b_api_licensing all 0/0, the fourth consecutive zero reading for the first two. Scout ran and added T036 (paid apartment-level Rent History Review), so the technique count is 36 and the Anthropic usage cap has lifted on schedule — that job is healthy, and no keywords were added (146 unchanged), which is the scout declining to pad rather than a failure. No revisit_on due (earliest 2026-08-25), so step 3 was a no-op for the fifth run running. YESTERDAY'S FALSIFIER PASSED: today's build detail reads '3 of 3 city guides live with their data caveat — 3 published by the daily growth build (dc, la, sf)'. The growth rsync does reach /guide/; the fallback publisher works. Its counterpart did not move — city_seo_expansion still reports 'docroot has no city hub pages at all', with the docroot's SEO corpus dated 2026-08-01 (2d ago). Checked yesterday's changed: line against the repo and every claim holds — guide_page() is at build_seo.py:638, t_city_guides publishes then checks, sitemap_daily carries the /guide/ monthly rule, llms_txt gates the three guide URLs on live_or_staged, and both seed.py and techniques.json carry the T023 fallback notes.

**Concluded.** The two-doors diagnosis from 08-02 is now confirmed by a controlled experiment rather than inference: identical content, two channels, one arrives. The guides went through the growth build and are live; the hub tier sat behind /root/dhcr-build and is still live nowhere, five days on. So the remedy generalises, and there is no reason left to treat the hub tier as blocked — it was only ever blocked on the choice of door. Worth saying plainly about the numbers: gsc_serving_pages returning to 89 is a recovery to a level already seen on 07-27, not progress; 89 of ~47,600 is 0.19% and the series has oscillated 77-89 for two weeks with no trend. Nothing in the tracked universe has earned a single impression in seven weekly windows, and every click remains the decommissioned jayshomefinder brand. I also found a defect in what I shipped yesterday, and it is the more interesting half of today: the fallback claimed it would 'step aside automatically' when the SEO pipeline returned, and that was false. growth_out is never cleared between runs and deploy is 'rsync -a' with no --delete, so a page this build ever staged is pushed into the docroot every night forever. Backing off on the ownership check stopped us rewriting the staged copy but did nothing about the copy already sitting there — the SEO build's own page would have been overwritten by our stale bytes on the next rsync, silently, and the handover would never have completed. That was one file yesterday; it would have been 255 today.

**Changed.** t_city_seo_expansion went from checker to publisher-then-checker, the same shape as t_city_guides. build_seo.py: lifted the rendering out of city_hub_pages() into city_hub_docs(key, guide_ok=True), returning [{kind, relpath, canonical, html, priority}] — one renderer, because two would overwrite each other's bytes nightly; city_hub_pages() is now just the writer. Verified byte-identical against HEAD: same 255 pages, same URLs, same sitemap priorities, zero byte diffs. guide_ok=False drops the 'is my apartment rent controlled in <city>?' link so the tier never ships pointing at a guide that is not live — the mirror of browse_ok on the guides. growth/techniques.py: added Context.unstage(relpath, url) and called it from BOTH publishers wherever they detect the SEO pipeline owns a page — it removes our staged copy and drops the lastmod entry, so the page stops being rsynced and stops being listed in our sitemap shard. That is the fix for yesterday's false 'steps aside automatically'. GUIDE_FALLBACK_MARKER renamed FALLBACK_MARKER now that two page families use it; the marker string is unchanged, so pages already published under it keep their ownership. sitemap_daily lists the hub tier at changefreq monthly (registry data, not nightly data) with the same priorities build_seo assigns in its own shards, so nothing changes the day it deploys again; llms.txt gains a 'Browse by area' section naming the three browse hubs, gated on live_or_staged. seed.py + techniques.json: T013 notes record the fallback, the marker rule and the sitemap/llms.txt consequences. Tested against seven fixtures including the ones where the watched thing is broken: empty docroot publishes 255 marked pages; a second run re-pings nothing; a live guide flips the guide link on for that city only; an SEO-owned page is left alone AND our stale copy is unstaged and dropped from lastmod (detail then reads 'handed N pages back to the SEO pipeline'); dry run stages nothing and says 'would be published'; build_seo unimportable returns ok=False naming the reason and the build survives. Verified: py_compile clean on growth/*.py, growth_daily.py and build_seo.py; build --dry-run --docroot . --build-dir . exits 0; report renders both parts. Did NOT run keywords.check_coverage().

**Watching.** (1) TOMORROW'S city_seo_expansion DETAIL IS THE FALSIFIER. It should read 'hub pages live: dc 103, la 112, sf 37' followed by '255 pages published by the daily growth build, browse hubs included (dc 104, la 113, sf 38)'. If it still says 'docroot has no city hub pages at all', the growth rsync is not reaching the /sf/ /la/ /dc/ subtrees — check permissions there first — and this change did nothing. (2) t_indexnow should report roughly 255 URLs submitted at HTTP 200 in one batch tomorrow, then near-zero after. A non-200, or a repeat batch on day two, means write_page's hashing is not stable across runs and we are re-pinging static pages, which is how a site loses its IndexNow key. (3) The real test of T013's hypothesis starts now, four days late: 252 aggregate pages targeting SF/LA/DC queries that have never earned an impression. Watch city_seo_expansion's gsc_owned_pages and gsc_owned_best_position over two to three weeks, and watch gsc_serving_pages — 255 pages is +0.5% of the corpus, so if the aggregate thesis is right it should be visible there. Still zero by 2026-08-24 and the binding constraint is domain authority, not page shape, and every content technique in the ledger is the wrong lever. (4) 'handed N pages back to the SEO pipeline' appearing in that detail is the signal /root/dhcr-build started pulling again; city_guides' 'published by the daily growth build' clause disappearing says the same thing. Either one means the fallback can eventually be retired. (5) fresh_section8 and daily_brief hit their fourth consecutive zero-impression reading today; 2026-08-05 is the decision date set on 07-31 and it should be honoured — /section8/ is live, rebuilt nightly and invisible. (6) OWNER ACTION, unchanged and still one command: 'git -C /root/dhcr-build pull origin main'. The growth build now routes around that checkout for the guides and the hub tier, but everything else the SEO pipeline owns — the 47,600 building pages, the NYC hubs, the sitemap shards — is still frozen at pre-2026-07-29 code.


## 2026-08-04 — The serving set is 90% stable, not a plateau — 142 pages have served, not 89
*6am review*

**Observed.** THE GATING NUMBER, eighth point: gsc_serving_pages 89 -> 77 -> 82 -> 82 -> 78 -> 81 -> 89 -> 89 (window 2026-07-26..08-02), flat against yesterday. Query impressions 33 (down from 37), clicks 8 (down from 9), still 100% jayshomefinder brand residual — /  earns 36 of them. search_share_pct 0.0 and tracked_ranking 0 of 146 for the eighth consecutive week-window. Traffic 20 visitors / 7 organic on 08-03, the best day since 07-29, against an 11-12/day median. Revenue $0, 0 paying subs, 4 accounts with saves (up from 3), 3 API keys. YESTERDAY'S FALSIFIER PASSED: today's build detail reads 'hub pages live: dc 103, la 112, sf 37 - 255 pages published by the daily growth build, browse hubs included', and t_indexnow submitted 263 URLs at HTTP 200 in one batch exactly as predicted. Checked yesterday's changed: line against the repo and every claim holds - city_hub_docs() at build_seo.py:338, Context.unstage() at techniques.py:103, FALLBACK_MARKER at 1018 with the rename note, llms.txt 'Browse by area' at 718, T013 notes carrying the fallback rule. Keyword coverage jumped 63.0% -> 71.9% (105/146; nyc 65/79, sf 16/23, la 15/24, dc 9/20): exactly 13 queries newly covered, all of them the SF/LA/DC guide queries ('is my apartment rent controlled sf', 'sf rent control exemptions', 'how much can rent go up rent controlled dc' and so on), so the guides published 08-03 are the cause and nothing else moved. Per-technique: hub_direct_answers 5 pages / 8 impressions / best position 1.5, still the only technique with any search visibility; city_guides, city_seo_expansion, fresh_section8, daily_brief, listings_freshness and b2b_api_licensing all 0/0 - fifth consecutive zero reading for fresh_section8 and daily_brief. No revisit_on due (earliest 2026-08-25), so step 3 was a no-op for the sixth run running. NEW, and the reason for today's change: I read gsc_pages.json against its own committed history for the first time. 89 today and 89 yesterday is not the same 89 - nine URLs entered the serving set overnight and nine left it. 80 of the 89 are single-address /building/ pages at 1-2 impressions each; the only non-building pages serving are /  (36 imp, pos 3.4), /zip/10024/ (4 imp, pos 1.5), /neighborhood/, and /sf/ /la/ /dc/ at 3 impressions each and position 5.7.

**Concluded.** My first read of that churn was wrong and the data corrected it, which is worth recording because the correction is the finding. Nine-in nine-out looked like Google rotating a random sample through a 47,600-page corpus. Replaying all six committed gsc_pages.json snapshots (07-30..08-04) says otherwise: stable holds at 68, 68, 69, 73, 80 against daily counts of 82, 82, 78, 81, 89 - the set is 83-90% the same night to night, so these pages hold their rankings and the tier is real. What is NOT stable is the total. Distinct pages ever served went 82 -> 96 -> 105 -> 117 -> 133 -> 142 while the daily count never left the 78-89 band. Google is discovering 9-16 new pages a night and serving almost exactly as many fewer of the old ones. That is a sharper diagnosis than 'the corpus is not indexed': pages are being found, they are simply not accumulating into more simultaneous visibility. The inference I cannot prove from six days is that this is a domain-level impression ceiling rather than a coincidence, but if it holds it means publishing page 47,601 does not add visibility, it redistributes it - which is the first evidence from this site's own numbers for T027's consolidation thesis, where before there was only borrowed general advice. It also means the number the last eight entries have called 'the gating number' was the wrong one. gsc_serving_pages is a rolling window count that cannot rise if the ceiling is real; gsc_serving_ever is monotone, has climbed 82 -> 142 in six days, and is what actually measures corpus discovery. Second correction to my own framing: 89 of 47,600 was quoted as 0.19% eight times. The honest figure for what Google has ever shown is 142, or 0.30%, and rising. Still catastrophic, but a different shape of catastrophic - discovery is not the failure, retention of simultaneous visibility is. Nothing else changed today: no technique other than hub_direct_answers earns an impression, and the 255 hub pages went live at 05:40 UTC, after the 05:00 measure, so today's numbers contain none of them.

**Changed.** growth/searchconsole.py: added serving-set churn tracking to T010. New _churn() folds each night's served URLs into a persistent per-URL history {url: [first_seen, last_seen, days_seen]} carried inside gsc_pages.json, and collect() now records gsc_serving_stable / _entered / _left / _ever as __site__ metrics beside gsc_serving_pages. Four things it deliberately gets right: the comparison set comes from history rather than prev['pages'] because that list is capped at 300 for readability and would understate churn the day the serving set grows past it; days_seen counts distinct dates and churn is skipped outright on a same-day re-run, so re-running the measurement twice cannot report a fictional 100% hold; a metric it cannot honestly compute is omitted rather than defaulted to zero, because the ledger reads missing as 'not measured' and zero as 'measured, and it was zero'; and history eviction at HISTORY_CAP=4000 carries a running dropped total forward so gsc_serving_ever stays monotone - the first version let a trim silently shrink 'ever', which is precisely the wrong conclusion the record exists to prevent, and the fixture caught it. Added stable_pages() to read back which URLs have served on the most days. growth/report.py: the Indexing progress bar now carries a note block saying how many of today's serving pages also served yesterday, how many entered and dropped out, and the cumulative ever figure - written as a note block so emailkit renders text and HTML from the one list, no hand-written markup.
