Backfilled from the six gsc_pages.json snapshots already committed in git (07-30..08-04): 142 URLs of real history into gsc_pages.json and 21 ledger rows, all marked backfilled in their meta, so tomorrow's review reads a six-day series instead of starting blind. Tested against eight fixtures including the ones where the watched thing is broken: cold start omits the churn metrics; day two counts one held, one entered, one left; a same-day re-run reports None rather than a fake plateau and does not inflate days_seen; total rotation reads stable 0; the cap evicts least-recently-served, reports how many, and 'ever' does not go down across the eviction; a legacy snapshot with no history key does not raise; an empty serving set does not crash. Verified: py_compile clean, build --dry-run --docroot . --build-dir . exits 0, report renders both parts and the new line reads identically in each. Did NOT run keywords.check_coverage(). NOT changed, and deliberately: /sf/ /la/ /dc/ are the three best-ranking non-homepage URLs on the site (position 5.7) and their only crawlable internal links to the hub tier and the city guides sit in a .seo-nav block that a mobile media query sets to display:none - see the watch list, I am not shipping a blind UX change to the map app from here.

**Watching.** (1) THE FALSIFIER FOR TODAY IS gsc_serving_ever. It should read roughly 150-160 tomorrow and keep climbing 9-16 a night while gsc_serving_pages stays in the 78-95 band. If ever flattens too, discovery has stopped and the problem is crawling, not visibility budget - a different and worse diagnosis. If gsc_serving_pages breaks above 100 and holds, the ceiling inference is wrong and consolidation is not urgent. (2) The 255 hub pages went live at 05:40 UTC today, after the measure, so tomorrow is their first measured day. Expect coverage to jump again by roughly 11 - the uncovered 'list' queries whose targets just went live are /sf/buildings/, /la/buildings/, /dc/buildings/, /sf/neighborhood/mission/, /dc/neighborhood/columbia-heights/, /dc/neighborhood/dupont-circle/, /la/zip/90019/, /la/zip/90026/. If coverage does NOT move, the rsync reached the docroot but coverage is scoring them uncovered, and the scorer is wrong. Impressions are a two-to-three-week question, not a tomorrow question: watch city_seo_expansion gsc_owned_pages through 2026-08-24. (3) fresh_section8 and daily_brief hit their fifth consecutive zero-impression reading today and 2026-08-05 is the decision date set on 07-31. Honour it tomorrow. Both are live, rebuilt nightly and invisible; if the impression-ceiling inference holds they are also actively costing the pages that do serve. (4) OWNER ACTION, unchanged for six days and still one command: 'git -C /root/dhcr-build pull origin main'. The growth build now routes around that checkout for the guides and the hub tier, but the 47,600 building pages, the NYC hubs and the sitemap shards are still frozen at pre-2026-07-29 code. (5) NEW OWNER ACTION, needs a browser and cannot be verified from this checkout: sf/index.html:608, and the same line in la/ and dc/, hide .seo-nav on mobile. That block holds the only contextual links from those three pages to /<city>/buildings/ and the city guide, and mobile is the indexing-primary rendering. I did not just delete the rule because the map is 100vh under a sticky header, so touch-dragging pans the map instead of scrolling the page and an un-hidden nav may be unreachable rather than merely invisible - which is probably why it was hidden. The fix wants a real device, and app files in this repo have no cron that deploys them anyway (refresh_seo.sh ships /root/dhcr-build/seo/, growth ships growth_out; nothing ships sf/index.html), so shipping it blind from here would be unpublished AND untested.


## 2026-08-05 — /section8/ has never been linked from anywhere — it is an orphan, not a failure. Ship the link and audit it nightly
*6am review*

**Observed.** Both crons fired (measure 2026-08-05T05:00:07Z, build 05:40:11Z, 10 changed URLs / 1 new, deployed; IndexNow 11 URLs HTTP 200). YESTERDAY'S FALSIFIER FIRED AND FAILED ITS PREDICTION. gsc_serving_ever went 82 -> 96 -> 105 -> 117 -> 133 -> 142 -> 142: flat, against a predicted 150-160. gsc_serving_entered went 14, 10, 12, 16, 9 -> 0; left 5; stable 84 of 84, so 100% of today's serving set is pages that have served before. gsc_serving_pages 89 -> 84. The window moved 07-26..08-02 to 07-27..08-03, so the one genuinely new day in it contributed not a single URL Google had not already served. Impressions 33 -> 29, clicks 8 -> 8, still 100% jayshomefinder brand residual. search_share_pct 0.0 and tracked_ranking 0 of 146 for the ninth consecutive week-window. Traffic 19 visitors / 3 organic / 1 AI on 08-04. Revenue $0, 0 paying subs, 4 accounts. Keyword coverage jumped 71.9% -> 82.2% (105 -> 120 of 146; nyc 66/79, sf 20/23, la 20/24, dc 14/20) -- yesterday predicted roughly +11 from the 255 hub pages going live at 05:40 after the measure, actual +15, so watch item (2) passes and the rsync did reach the docroot. Per-technique: hub_direct_answers 5 pages / 8 impressions / best position 1.5, still the only technique on the site with any search visibility; fresh_section8, daily_brief, city_guides, city_seo_expansion, listings_freshness and b2b_api_licensing all 0/0 -- the SIXTH consecutive zero for fresh_section8 and daily_brief, which is the decision date set on 07-31. Yesterday's changed: line checked against the repo and every claim holds: _churn() at searchconsole.py:246, HISTORY_CAP=4000 at 243, the four gsc_serving_* metrics recorded at 128-144, stable_pages() at 318, the churn note at report.py:141-143. THE FINDING, and the reason for today's change: I broke gsc_pages.json's 142-URL history down by section for the first time. 131 /building/, 6 /zip/, 1 /neighborhood/, /sf/, /la/, /dc/ and the homepage. Zero /section8/. Zero /brief/. Zero /guide/. Then I grepped the whole corpus for who links to them: nothing on findacrib.com links to /section8/ or /brief/ at all. index.html's .seo-nav links /buildings/, the five boroughs and /sf/ /la/ /dc/; sf/index.html links /sf/buildings/ and the SF guide. Every single non-building URL that has ever served is one of those linked targets. Separately: no scout-sourced ledger or keyword addition on 08-04 or 08-05 after four straight days of them (T030-T032 on 08-01, T033-T035 on 08-02, T036 on 08-03), keywords flat at 146 -- state.json is gitignored so the job's own error record is invisible from here and I am not calling it a failure. No revisit_on due (earliest 2026-08-25).

**Concluded.** /section8/ is an orphan, and I have been reading its zeros as a verdict on its content. Ten days live, rebuilt nightly from the only dataset on this site that genuinely changes daily, listed in sitemap-daily.xml with an honest lastmod, submitted to IndexNow every night, and it has never earned one impression. That is not 'too early' and it is not thin content: no page anywhere on this site links to it. 2026 indexing guidance is consistent that Google treats a URL reachable only from a sitemap as unimportant and may never index it however often it is submitted -- and this site is its own control group. The only non-building URLs that have ever served are the homepage and the exact set the app shells link to: /sf/, /la/, /dc/ (added to the homepage nav in the 07-30 import, first served 08-01, and held 5 of 5 days since), six ZIP hubs and one neighborhood hub from the interlinked SEO corpus. Linked pages serve; sitemap-only pages do not. That is the cleanest signal this site has produced in ten days and it applies to /brief/ and /guide/ identically. It also revises yesterday's central inference. Yesterday I concluded the constraint was on simultaneous visibility rather than discovery, and predicted gsc_serving_ever would keep climbing 9-16 a night. It went flat with zero entering, which is one data point and I am not calling discovery dead on it -- but the two stories fit: Google discovered 60 URLs in six days by crawling links inside the SEO corpus, has now stopped finding new ones there, and has never had a path into the growth build's own sections at all. On the decision that was due today: KEEP fresh_section8 and daily_brief, do not retire them. Six zero readings are real, but retiring on them would blame the content for a link-graph fault -- exactly the class of error the 07-31 entry existed to fix, one unit further up. Recorded in both ledger notes with a new decision date of 2026-08-26: 21 days after they have a verified inbound link, zero impressions convicts the content and they go. The thing I cannot verify from a checkout is whether index.html even deploys -- the SEO corpus in the docroot was last written 2026-08-01 (4d ago) and this project has twice concluded something shipped when it had not -- which is why the fix ships with an auditor rather than on its own.

**Changed.** Three edits, one fix and one instrument. (1) index.html: the homepage .seo-nav paragraph now links /section8/ and /brief/ in prose that is accurate about what they are ('rebuilt daily from the overnight AffordableHousing.com feed', 'what came on and off the market'). That block is where /sf/ /la/ /dc/ got their crawl path and it is the only inbound link available to me -- the pages that serve are all owned by the SEO pipeline, whose checkout has not taken a push since before 07-29, and the FALLBACK_MARKER doctrine is explicit that the growth build never edits a page it did not write. (2) growth/techniques.py: new technique T037 crawl_paths, registered in REGISTRY and ORDER between sitemap_daily and indexnow so a run about to submit an orphan says so in the same log. It reads every active technique's declared URL prefixes out of the ledger -- not a list in the code, so a technique added tomorrow is audited the night it goes live -- and checks the live docroot for a crawlable inbound link into each. Four things it deliberately gets right: a page inside the prefix it is being scored for does not count, because a family whose pages only link to each other is precisely the orphan case and self-links would report it healthy at the moment it is broken; section existence is tested with os.path.isdir, because glob('sf/buildings*') matches sf/buildings.min.json and the first version read three sections as built that were not; a prefix with nothing published under it is reported 'not built yet, not audited' rather than orphaned, and that list is printed last so the orphan names are not pushed off a phone-width line; and it samples one capped, sorted set of pages per generated family rather than reading 47,600 files, because a family shares one template. It publishes nothing -- zero blast radius on the docroot -- and it is the answer to 'did the app shell deploy', which is a question this project has needed twice. Eight fixtures, all passing, including the ones where the watched thing is broken: orphaned section detected and named with its slug; the same tree with the homepage link added reads 5 of 5; family self-links only still reads orphaned; a data file does not make a section read as built; an empty docroot says so rather than claiming health; the single-inbound-source line names the source; and two runs on an unchanged tree return identical dicts. (3) growth/seed.py: T037's record with the hypothesis and the evidence, and ledger notes on T001/T002 recording today's decision, its reasoning and the 2026-08-26 falsifier date. Verified: py_compile clean, build --dry-run --docroot . --build-dir . exits 0, report renders both parts with T037 in each and 20 balanced tables. Did NOT run keywords.check_coverage(). NOT changed and deliberately: daily_brief still mints a dated URL a day. I drafted consolidating it -- one canonical /brief/ instead of 365 near-duplicates a year -- and dropped it, because on today's diagnosis its zeros are the orphan fault too, and gutting a 10-day-old technique on a hypothesis I am about to test properly is the same mistake twice. Also noted, not fixed: t_daily_brief writes ledger state (brief_prev) even under --dry-run, so a dry run on the droplet would tell the next real run there was no movement. Small, real, and not worth touching the live brief path on the same day as this change.

**Watching.** (1) THE FALSIFIER IS T037's OWN OUTPUT, in tomorrow's last_run.json. If crawl_paths reports ok and '/section8/ <- home shell', the app shell deploys and the clock on the content verdict has started -- then watch fresh_section8's gsc_owned_impressions through 2026-08-26 and retire it if it is still zero. If it reports 'ORPHANED, reachable only from sitemap-daily.xml: /section8/', then index.html does NOT deploy from this repo, the fix is stranded exactly like the guides were, and that is a bigger finding than the link itself: it would mean every app-shell change any session has made since 07-30 is also stranded, and the next review's job is to publish the link through the growth build the way _publish_stranded_guides does. Do not conclude the link 'did not work' from an orphan reading -- read it as a deploy failure. (2) Impressions are a two-to-three-week question, not a tomorrow question. Expect /section8/ to appear in gsc_pages.json history no sooner than a week after the link goes live, and only if the crawl path is real. (3) gsc_serving_ever: 142 with 0 entering is one reading. If it is still 142 tomorrow and the day after, discovery has genuinely stalled and the diagnosis moves from 'no path into the growth sections' to 'crawling has slowed across the whole site', which is worse and needs a different answer. If it resumes climbing, yesterday's ceiling inference stands and today's orphan finding is a separate, additive problem. (4) The scout added nothing on 08-04 or 08-05 after four straight days. If a third day passes with no candidate and no keyword, read the report's 'Research jobs blocked' section for the actual ledger error and say which it is -- a spend cap is not a bug, but silence for three days is worth one look. (5) OWNER ACTION, unchanged for seven days: 'git -C /root/dhcr-build pull origin main'. The SEO corpus in the docroot was last written 2026-08-01. The growth build routes around it for the guides and the 255 hub pages, but the 47,600 building pages, the NYC hub tier and the sitemap shards are still frozen at pre-2026-07-29 code -- which is also why the homepage link had to go in the app shell rather than the hub templates.


## 2026-08-06 — The orphan fix could not deploy: index.html is not on any cron's path — moved the crawl path into build_seo.py's hub tier
*6am review*

**Observed.** Both droplet crons fired (last_run.json: build 05:40Z, measure 05:00Z). gsc_serving_pages is flat, not climbing: 89, 77, 82, 82, 78, 81, 89, 89, 84, 82 over 2026-07-27..08-06 — ~0.17% of 47,600 pages. But gsc_serving_ever climbs every day: 82, 96, 105, 117, 133, 142, 142, 151, while gsc_serving_stable sits at 68-84. So ~9 pages/day enter the serving set and ~9 leave it; the set is churning, not growing. Impressions 21-37/day, clicks 2-9, search_share_pct 0.0 for the 10th straight day, 0/146 tracked queries ranking. Visitors 7-22/day ex-ad-flight. paying_subs 0, mrr_usd 0.0. Keyword coverage 120/146 (82.2%). Crucially, today's crawl_paths audit (T037, shipped yesterday, first run today) returned ok:false — '/brief/ (daily_brief), /section8/ (fresh_section8)' still ORPHANED. No technique is due for revisit (earliest 2026-08-25).

**Concluded.** Yesterday's entry claimed it fixed the orphans by adding links to the homepage .seo-nav. The links ARE in the repo's index.html (verified in the diff of 362250c) — but that fix cannot reach production, and today's audit proves it. Neither cron deploys the root index.html: growth_daily.py cmd_deploy rsyncs only growth_out/, and scripts/refresh_seo.sh rsyncs only build_seo.py's seo/. The app shell is a hand-copied static file (README: 'no build step; serve the directory statically'). So the correct diagnosis was shipped to the wrong file. Second finding: build_seo.py — the 47.6k-page corpus that is the only part of this site Google actually crawls — contains zero references to /section8/ or /brief/ (grep: no matches). The reference was one-way, /section8/<borough>/ links out to /borough/<slug>/ and nothing linked back; current indexing guidance is that a hub whose spokes never reinforce it, and any URL with no internal link, is what gets dropped. That matches the churn signal exactly. Third: the SEO pipeline itself is stale — the docroot's SEO corpus was last written 2026-08-01, 5 days ago — so today's fix lands only when refresh_seo.sh next runs, which is the main risk to it. Not a cron failure to chase yet, but if the corpus is still 08-01 in a few days that is the real blocker, ahead of anything content-related.

**Changed.** build_seo.py: added VOUCHER_XLINK, a crawl-path block linking /section8/ and /brief/, and inserted it into the NYC hub tier — the /buildings/ master hub (priority 0.9), the 5 borough hubs, the 198 neighborhood hubs and the 165 ZIP hubs. Deliberately NOT on the 47k building pages: adding a boilerplate block to thin leaf pages would worsen the duplication that is likely causing the churn. It quotes no counts, because the voucher feed is rebuilt by the growth build hours later from a file build_seo.py never reads, so any number here would be unsubstantiable; the destination page carries the live figures. Verified by running the full build: 47,854 pages generated, block present in seo/borough/brooklyn/, seo/buildings/ and seo/zip/11221/. Confirmed the growth audit actually detects the single-quoted href form by running _crawl_link_audit against a synthetic docroot (both prefixes resolved to 'borough hub'). growth/techniques.py: t_crawl_paths' orphan branch now appends _stale_note(), so 'still orphaned' distinguishes 'the fix is wrong' from 'the fix has not deployed' — the exact ambiguity that cost this cycle. README.md: documented which paths a git push does and does not deploy. Left the index.html links in place; they are correct and will land whenever the app is next deployed by hand.

**Watching.** Whether the SEO corpus staleness clears — if _stale_note still says 2026-08-01 tomorrow, refresh_seo.sh has not run for 6 days and that is the top priority, above content. Once it does deploy, crawl_paths should flip to ok:true and name the inbound source families; if it still reports /section8/ or /brief/ orphaned AFTER a fresh corpus write, the link detection or the placement is wrong, not the deploy. Then the real test: gsc_serving_ever should keep climbing AND gsc_serving_pages should start to follow it instead of churning flat at ~82 — that gap closing is the signal this worked. Give it 10-14 days; /section8/ earning its first impression at all is the earliest possible evidence.


## 2026-08-07 — The corpus pipeline has been dead 6 days — routed the /section8/ and /brief/ crawl path through the growth build, the only deploy path that works
*6am review*

**Observed.** Both droplet crons fired (last_run.json: build 05:40:21Z, measure 05:00:06Z). GSC window 2026-07-29..08-05: 29 impressions, 10 clicks (the highest click count yet), 80 serving pages, search_share_pct 0.0 for the 11th straight day, 0 of 146 tracked queries ranking. gsc_serving_pages is flat-to-down: 89, 77, 82, 82, 78, 81, 89, 89, 84, 82, 80 across 07-27..08-07 — 0.17% of ~47,600 published pages. gsc_serving_ever climbed again to 163 (82, 96, 105, 117, 133, 142, 142, 151, 163), +12 today, with 13 entered and 15 left overnight; gsc_serving_stable fell for the third day, 84 -> 73 -> 67. Visitors 7-22/day ex-ad-flight (08-06: 14 visitors, 7 organic, 0 AI). paying_subs 0, mrr_usd 0.0. Keyword coverage 120/146 (82.2%). Of the 80 pages that serve: 72 building pages, 3 ZIP hubs, one neighborhood hub, the homepage (33 impressions, position 3.5) and /sf/ /la/ /dc/ (3 impressions each, position 5.7). hub_direct_answers owns 4 serving pages, 8 impressions, best position 1.5 — the only technique with a non-zero owned metric. fresh_section8 and daily_brief remain at 0 owned impressions after 33 days; city_seo_expansion 0 after 33; city_guides 0 after 10. t_crawl_paths returned ok:false again: 10 of 12 published sections have an inbound internal link, /section8/ (fresh_section8) and /brief/ (daily_brief) still ORPHANED — and the staleness note added yesterday reads 'the docroot SEO corpus was last written 2026-08-01 (6d ago)', unchanged from yesterday's 5d. No technique is due for revisit; the earliest is 2026-08-25. Verified yesterday's changed: line against the repo — commit bdb5b47 does contain all three claims (VOUCHER_XLINK at four insertion points in build_seo.py's NYC hub tier, _stale_note appended to t_crawl_paths' orphan branch, README deploy-path section); the stale note is visibly working in today's output. Correcting yesterday's concern about the scout: it is not silent. T038 (org_seat_licenses_legal_aid) carries source scout:2026-08-06, so the API usage cap lifted on schedule and the scout is producing.

**Concluded.** The one number that matters did not move, and the reason is now unambiguous: scripts/refresh_seo.sh has not completed since 2026-08-01. Everything downstream follows from that. The 47.6k-page corpus in the docroot — the only part of this site Google materially crawls, holding all 72 serving building pages, the 3 serving ZIP hubs, the 368 NYC hub pages and the VOUCHER_XLINK block shipped yesterday — is frozen at six-day-old code. _seo_corpus_age is trustworthy here: build_seo.py's write() rewrites its sitemap stamps unconditionally every run, so rsync -a would copy a fresh mtime across on any completed run; a stamp stuck at 08-01 means the pipeline genuinely has not finished, not that nothing changed. I cannot restart it from Anthropic's cloud and no code change in this repo will make an unrun cron run. The deeper failure is a pattern, not a bug: three consecutive reviews have now diagnosed the orphan correctly and shipped the fix into a file no working cron deploys — 08-05 into index.html (no cron copies it), 08-06 into build_seo.py's NYC tier (refresh_seo.sh copies it, and refresh_seo.sh is dead). Being right about the fix three times is worth nothing if it never reaches production. The growth build is the only path from this repo into the docroot that demonstrably works: it ran today at 05:40Z, published 255 city hub pages and 3 guides, and it imports build_seo.city_hub_docs from a fresh git checkout every night. So the fix belongs there, and that is a general lesson worth keeping — prefer the deploy path you can observe working over the one that owns the code. Honest weakness of today's change: the three browse hubs it links from have themselves earned 0 impressions in the 4 days since they were published, so this buys a real internal link from an unproven page. It is still strictly better than a link on a page that does not deploy at all. Also still true and still not working: 0 revenue, 0 ranking tracked queries, and gsc_serving_stable falling three days running while gsc_serving_ever climbs — Google keeps finding new pages and keeps dropping old ones, which is the signature of a corpus it does not think is worth holding.

**Changed.** Two edits, one purpose — put the crawl path into /section8/ and /brief/ on a page that will actually deploy tonight. (1) build_seo.py: added CITY_VOUCHER_XLINK and inserted it into city_hub_docs()'s browse hub, so /sf/buildings/, /la/buildings/ and /dc/buildings/ each link to /section8/ and /brief/. That function is the one build_seo entry point the daily growth build imports and rsyncs itself (_publish_stranded_city_hubs), so this reaches production on the next 05:40 UTC run whether or not refresh_seo.sh ever recovers. It is a second string rather than a reuse of VOUCHER_XLINK because the voucher feed is New York City only: the block names the city, and closes with 'There is no equivalent feed for <city> yet' so a San Francisco reader is not led to expect SF voucher coverage that does not exist. Browse hubs only, not the 252 place pages beneath them — the browse hub already carries an 'Other cities' section so a cross-city block is contextual there, and repeating a New York block on every SF neighborhood page is exactly the boilerplate-on-thin-pages duplication that plausibly drives the serving-set churn. (2) growth/techniques.py: t_fresh_section8's hub now links to /brief/. Every dated brief already linked to /section8/; the reference had only ever run one way. On its own that link is worth nothing — two orphans linking to each other is the orphan-cluster case — but with /section8/ now reachable from the browse hubs it carries the path one hop further into /brief/. Deliberately NOT added to t_crawl_paths' source families, because crediting an orphan-to-orphan link would make the audit report both sections healthy on the day they are not. Verified: py_compile clean on growth/*.py, growth_daily.py and build_seo.py; build --dry-run --docroot . --build-dir . exits 0; city_hub_docs renders for all three cities with both hrefs present on the browse hub and zero place pages touched, and the city name substituted correctly (San Francisco / Los Angeles / Washington, DC); a real build into a scratchpad confirmed the /brief/ link on the rendered /section8/ page, and the ledger files it touched were restored from git. Ran _crawl_link_audit against a synthetic docroot built from the new city_hub_docs: /section8/ and /brief/ both resolve to ['dc browse hub', 'la browse hub', 'sf browse hub'] where they previously resolved to nothing. Did NOT run keywords.check_coverage().

**Watching.** (1) THE FALSIFIER IS TOMORROW'S crawl_paths LINE. If it reads ok:true with /section8/ and /brief/ resolving to the city browse hubs, the growth build's deploy path works and the orphan is finally closed in production — then the clock starts on the content question, and the next number to watch is fresh_section8 and daily_brief earning a first non-zero gsc_owned_impressions, which is a 10-14 day question, not a tomorrow question. If it STILL reads ORPHANED, do not conclude the copy is wrong: it would mean the growth build's own rsync is not landing either, which would be a catastrophic finding since that is the only door left, and the next review's first job is to check whether last_run.json still reports deployed:true. (2) The staleness note. If it still says 2026-08-01 in another two days that is 8 days of a dead corpus pipeline and it outranks every content idea in the ledger; the owner action is unchanged and now urgent: get scripts/refresh_seo.sh running again on the droplet (check whether the Zumper scrape that precedes it in the chain is aborting under set -euo pipefail). (3) gsc_serving_stable, 84 -> 73 -> 67 over three days while gsc_serving_ever climbs 151 -> 163. If stable keeps falling while ever keeps climbing, the churn is getting worse and the diagnosis moves from 'these two sections are orphans' to 'Google is actively shedding this corpus', which needs a different answer — most likely T027 index triage: promote a few thousand strong pages and consolidate the rest, which the current research consensus supports (a small excellent set that earns impressions, then scale the pattern). (4) The 255 city hub pages hit 0 impressions on their 5th day today. If they are still at 0 owned impressions by 2026-08-17 then the tier today's link hangs off is itself not being crawled, and the link needs a stronger home.


## 2026-08-08 — The ORPHANED report was a false alarm — the audit reads the docroot before its own rsync
*6am review*

**Observed.** Both droplet crons fired (last_run.json: build 2026-08-08T05:40:19Z, deployed:true, 1 new / 10 changed URLs; measure 05:00:07Z; searchconsole 05:00:05Z). GSC window 2026-07-30..08-06: 20 impressions, 6 clicks, 80 serving pages, search_share_pct 0.0 for the 12th straight day, 0 of 146 tracked queries ranking, keyword coverage 120/146 (82.2%). gsc_serving_pages across 07-27..08-08: 89, 77, 82, 82, 78, 81, 89, 89, 84, 82, 80, 80 — flat for twelve days at 0.17% of ~47,600 published pages. gsc_serving_ever +9 to 172 (9 entered, 9 left overnight); gsc_serving_stable 67 -> 71, its first rise after three straight falls. Visitors 08-07: 17, of which 0 organic and 0 AI; ex-ad-flight median ~14/day. paying_subs 0, mrr_usd 0.0. Per technique after 34 days: fresh_section8, daily_brief, city_seo_expansion, listings_freshness and b2b_api_licensing are all still at 0 owned pages and 0 owned impressions; city_guides 0 after 11 days; hub_direct_answers remains the only technique with anything at all — 3 owned pages, 6 impressions, best position 1.3. NEW THIS RUN, from growth/gsc_pages.json (tracked, so any future review can redo this): of the 80 serving pages, 73 are single-address building pages, 3 are ZIP hubs, and the remaining 4 are the homepage and the /sf/ /la/ /dc/ shells; 123 impressions across the window; median position 8.0. Only four distinct queries clear Search Console's per-page threshold, and three of them are the decommissioned previous brand — 'jayshomefinder' 14 impressions, 'jays home finder' 6, 'jayshomefinder.com' 4 — while the fourth is a bare street address, '228 e 3rd st ny', at position 29.5. t_crawl_paths reported ok:false with /section8/ and /brief/ ORPHANED again, staleness note at 2026-08-01 (7d). I checked the 08-07 fix instead of believing that line: build_seo.city_hub_docs renders sf 38 / la 113 / dc 104 docs and every one of the three browse hubs carries href='/section8/' and href='/brief/', and today's build published exactly those counts (dc 104, la 113, sf 38). Commit 266448f contains both edits it claimed, including the /brief/ link on the built /section8/ hub. No technique is due for revisit; the earliest is 2026-08-25.

**Concluded.** Today's ORPHANED line was a false alarm, and a structural one rather than a slip: cmd_build runs every technique first and rsyncs growth_out at the very end, so any check that reads the docroot is scoring the PREVIOUS run's deploy. The links to /section8/ and /brief/ were staged and rsynced by the same 05:40 run that announced both sections orphaned. That makes four consecutive cycles spent on the orphan question, and the underlying cause has been different every time — wrong file (08-05), dead pipeline (08-06 and 08-07), blind instrument (08-08) — while the output looked identical each morning: one line saying ORPHANED. When three different faults produce the same sentence, the sentence is the defect. So today's work went into the instrument rather than into a fifth link. Second: the corpus pipeline is still dead at 7 days, which means every build_seo.py edit — including the VOUCHER_XLINK block on the NYC hub tier from 08-06 — is still only in git, and the 47.6k pages Google actually crawls are frozen at 08-01 code. The practical consequence for a review running in the cloud is worth stating plainly, because I nearly repeated the mistake: any idea whose implementation lives in build_seo.py is unshippable this week, so generating more of them is wasted effort. Third, and the most useful new thing I learned: this corpus's demonstrated ranking ability is for queries with no volume. Median position 8.0 across 80 serving pages, one visible query that is a literal street address, three that belong to a business that no longer exists at this domain — against 0 of 146 category queries in the top 10. Meanwhile hub_direct_answers holds best position 1.3 but appears on only 3 of 368 hub pages. The binding constraint is indexing, not page quality and not page count, which is also what this week's search reading says post-March-2026: consolidate, noindex thin variants, 'the winners are not the ones with the most pages'. That makes T027 index triage the right next big move and makes publishing any new page family a mistake right now. I considered and rejected three: address-level pages for DC/SF/LA (adds thin pages to a corpus Google is already shedding — 9 pages entered and 9 left the serving set overnight); an honest re-crawl nudge for the NYC hub tier via lastmod (those pages genuinely have not changed since 08-01, so any bump would be a fabrication, and Google re-crawls from lastmod); and a new guide answering one of the 26 uncovered tracked queries such as 'good cause eviction nyc' or 'how to tell if my building was deregulated' (auto-authoring legal claims in a cron run risks precisely the accuracy this site sells — if that guide is written it should be written by a human, or by a run whose only job is that guide).

**Changed.** Three edits, all in the growth build's own deploy path, all aimed at the same thing: stop the review loop losing a cycle a day to an ambiguous line. (1) growth/techniques.py — t_crawl_paths now audits the staging dir as well as the docroot, via _crawl_link_audit(root, prefixes) (renamed parameter; it takes either tree). A prefix with no live inbound link but one on a page this run staged now reports 'awaiting this run's rsync, linked from a page staged minutes ago and not yet in the docroot: /section8/ (fresh_section8) <- sf browse hub' instead of ORPHANED. It never counts a staged link as healthy — ok stays False, because a link that exists only in growth_out is not a link Google can follow — and it is skipped in a dry run, which stages nothing this run but may hold a previous run's growth_out. To keep 'wait one night' distinct from 'the last working deploy path is dead', the first date a prefix is seen staged-but-not-live is remembered in state (crawl_staged_since); past STAGED_GRACE_DAYS=1 the wording escalates to 'STAGED BUT NOT DEPLOYED, the growth build's own rsync is not reaching the docroot', which would be the most serious finding this audit can produce. The genuinely-orphaned branch is unchanged and keeps the corpus staleness note. (2) growth_daily.py — cmd_build now records seo_corpus {written, days_old} as a first-class field in the git-tracked last_run.json, instead of leaving it embedded in a sentence inside three techniques' detail strings. That is the one number a cloud review cannot otherwise see: I have spent part of three runs reverse-engineering it out of prose. (3) growth/report.py — a callout, high in the email just under the Goals block, when that field is 2+ days old (tone bad at 3+): 'SEO corpus frozen for 7 days ... scripts/refresh_seo.sh runs nightly ... needs a hand on the droplet; nothing in the repo can restart it.' Built as an emailkit callout block so the HTML and text parts come from the same list; rendered both and read them. Also updated README's deploy-path section: the two paths are not equally reliable, check build.seo_corpus before assuming a build_seo.py edit shipped, and the build's run-then-rsync order is why a docroot check scores yesterday's deploy. Verified: py_compile clean on growth/*.py, growth_daily.py and build_seo.py; build --dry-run --docroot . --build-dir . exits 0; t_crawl_paths exercised against synthetic docroot/staging pairs for all four states (live-linked -> ok True; staged today -> 'awaiting this run's rsync'; staged 3 days ago -> 'STAGED BUT NOT DEPLOYED'; nothing anywhere -> ORPHANED) plus a dry run, which correctly ignored staging and wrote no state; then end-to-end against a real build into a scratchpad with a docroot rigged to look like the previous deploy, where the pending branch named its source families correctly. Rendered the report with a stale corpus record and read the callout in both the HTML and the plain-text part. Restored the ledger files and last_run.json that the scratchpad build touched — note for future runs: a non-dry build overwrites growth/last_run.json, which is the droplet's ground truth, so check git status before committing. Did NOT run keywords.check_coverage(). Added no keywords: the 26 uncovered queries are a content roadmap, and adding more unanswered queries to it measures nothing new.

**Watching.** (1) Tomorrow's crawl_paths line is the direct test of today's change AND of yesterday's. If it reads ok:true with /section8/ and /brief/ resolving to the city browse hubs, both are confirmed and the orphan question is finally closed after four cycles — move on to content. If it reads 'awaiting this run's rsync' the instrument is working but something re-staged the fix, and if it reads 'STAGED BUT NOT DEPLOYED' after 2026-08-09 then the growth build's rsync itself is failing, which is the last door from this repo into the docroot and outranks everything else. If it still reads a bare ORPHANED, today's change did not fire — check that ctx.out exists on the droplet (it is build_dir/growth_out, and build_dir is the repo checkout). (2) The new callout must appear in tomorrow's 05:00 ET email, since the 05:40 UTC build writes seo_corpus first. If build.seo_corpus is null in last_run.json, _seo_corpus_age found no sitemap stamps in the docroot, which would itself be a finding. (3) The corpus staleness clock: 7 days today. This is the owner action and it has not moved since 08-01 — get scripts/refresh_seo.sh running (check whether the listings scrape ahead of it in the chain is aborting under set -euo pipefail). Every build_seo.py change pushed since 08-01 is waiting on it. (4) gsc_serving_stable rose 67 -> 71 today after three falls while gsc_serving_ever went to 172. One day is not a trend; if stable keeps rising the churn is easing on its own, and if it resumes falling then T027 index triage should be promoted from candidate to the next substantial build, on the growth-build side if the corpus pipeline is still dead. (5) fresh_section8 and daily_brief earning a first non-zero owned impression is the 10-14 day test of the crawl-path work, not a tomorrow test — do not judge it before 2026-08-21.


## 2026-08-09 — No droplet run this morning for the first time in the record — and the browse hubs, the one tier we can still deploy, had no extractable answer
*6am review*

**Observed.** NO LEDGER COMMIT FOR 2026-08-09. The droplet's growth_run.sh has pushed a 'growth: ledger <date>' commit twice a day (05:00-05:04Z measure, 05:40Z build) every day from at least 2026-08-03 through 2026-08-08; at 10:04Z today origin/main's newest commit is 2930432 at 05:36Z, an operator commit, and there is no ledger commit at all. So both of today's crons are ~5h overdue as observed from git, and every number below is yesterday's. The operator pushed 8 commits between 2026-08-08T23:49 and 2026-08-09T01:36 EDT (04:13-05:36Z), overlapping the 05:00Z measure window; growth_run.sh pulls with --rebase --autostash and falls through to the local copy on failure, so a failed pull alone would not stop a run, but a push race would leave the commit sitting unpushed on the droplet. Yesterday's numbers (last_run.json, build 2026-08-08T05:40:19Z deployed:true): GSC window 07-30..08-06, 20 impressions, 6 clicks, 80 serving pages, search_share_pct 0.0 for the 12th straight day, 0 of 146 tracked queries ranking, coverage 120/146 (82.2%). gsc_serving_pages 07-27..08-08: 89, 77, 82, 82, 78, 81, 89, 89, 84, 82, 80, 80 — 0.17% of ~47,600 pages, flat for twelve days. gsc_serving_ever 172, gsc_serving_stable 71. Visitors 08-07: 17, 0 organic, 0 AI. paying_subs 0, mrr_usd 0.0. From gsc_pages.json: of the 80 serving pages, 73 are NYC building pages (manhattan 31, brooklyn 27, bronx 13, queens 2), 3 are NYC ZIP hubs, and the last 4 are the homepage (23 impressions, position 3.8) and the /sf/ /la/ /dc/ shells (3 impressions each). ZERO pages from the 255-page SF/LA/DC hub tier serve, six days after it was published, and zero from /section8/, /brief/ or /guide/. Checked yesterday's changed: line against the repo — commit 963757b does contain all three claims (staging-aware _crawl_link_audit with crawl_staged_since state, seo_corpus as a first-class field in last_run.json, the stale-corpus callout in report.py) and the field is present in the tracked file, reading null only because I overwrote it with a scratchpad build and restored it. No technique is due for revisit; the earliest is 2026-08-25, so step 3 was a genuine no-op today. NEW: t_hub_direct_answers globs only neighborhood/*/*, borough/* and zip/* — the NYC tier. Its daily green 'answer block on 368 of 368 hub pages' has never looked at the 255 SF/LA/DC hub pages, and build_seo.city_hub_docs gives every place page an answer_block and an FAQ but gives the three city browse hubs neither.

**Concluded.** Two things. First, on the missing run: one absent morning is not yet a dead cron, and the standing instruction is not to cry cron-failure until it is several days stale — so I am recording it, not escalating it. But it is the first gap in the record and it is not the invisible-gitignored-file false alarm of 2026-07-26: last_run.json is tracked, growth_run.sh commits it explicitly, and the file in the repo still says 2026-08-08. The benign reading is that the run happened and the push lost a race with the operator's 05:36Z push; growth_run.sh's push has no retry, so an unpushed ledger commit would sit on the droplet and arrive with tomorrow's. The malign reading is that nothing ran. Tomorrow's commit distinguishes them cleanly: two ledger commits or one. Second, and the reason today's change is where it is: the tier we can actually deploy earns nothing, and the highest-priority page in it was the one page missing the only pattern on this site with a measured win. hub_direct_answers holds best position 1.3 — every other technique is at 0 owned impressions after 34 days — and the current search reading is consistent about why (a direct answer at the top, question-style headings, and structured data that matches the visible text; ~65-71% of pages cited by ChatGPT and Google AI Mode carry structured data). Meanwhile the crawl-budget consensus for large corpora says the same thing from the other side: Google will not crawl 47,600 pages when the first hundred it sees are thin, and the ratio that matters is indexed-to-published, which here is 0.17%. I am not adding pages to a corpus Google is already shedding. So the move is to make the pages we already publish worth citing, starting at the top of the tier. Honest weakness, stated plainly: the three browse hubs have earned 0 impressions in 6 days, so this improves a page nothing currently reads, and it does not touch the 47.6k-page corpus where all 80 serving pages live. It is a bet that the tier is thin, not invisible. Also unchanged and still not working: 0 revenue, 0 ranking tracked queries, and the SEO corpus pipeline, which was 7 days stale yesterday and which I cannot check today because no build ran. Still blocked and not worked around: AdSense has no slot id, and api@findacrib.com may not forward. Neither scout nor outreach failed today because neither ran.

**Changed.** Two edits, both on the growth build's rsync — the only deploy path observed working — and both aimed at the one pattern with a measured position. (1) build_seo.py: the three city browse hubs (/sf/buildings/, /la/buildings/, /dc/buildings/) now carry a direct-answer block, a three-question FAQ and FAQPage JSON-LD, matching what every place page beneath them already had. New CITY_BROWSE_COPY (per-city source phrase, legal rule, caveat), _place_word(), _city_browse_answer() and _city_browse_faq(); the browse hub's jsonld goes from [crumb] to [crumb, faq_jsonld]. Per-city rather than templated because the authority differs: SF is an owner-reported inventory anonymized to the block, LA is a derived likely-RSO proxy for a list LAHD holds, DC is a registration record. Every claim is scoped to the index rather than the city — 'This index covers 4,271 properties across 103 neighborhoods' — because total_recs sums only places clearing MIN_CITY_HUB, and 'Washington DC has 4,271' would overstate what the data supports; the count of places left off is stated in both the lead and the FAQ. The third FAQ is per-city for accuracy, not variety: median build year with the certificate-of-occupancy caveat for SF and LA, median reported rent for DC, where coverage turns on registration and a pre-1976 build and a build-year frame would be wrong. The count sentence moved out of the lead into the answer block rather than being duplicated, and the lead's 'N more had fewer than 5' was reworded since it no longer has an antecedent. Every FAQ answer is visible text as well as JSON-LD — the 2026-07-26 hidden-FAQ lesson. (2) growth/techniques.py: t_hub_direct_answers now audits the SF/LA/DC tier too, via _answer_hub_families() (patterns are lists; glob has no brace expansion), so its daily line covers 626 pages instead of 368 — its green light previously covered 59% of the hub pages this site publishes and would have stayed green through a regression in the only tier we can deploy. It reads the staging dir when the docroot copy is behind, exactly as t_crawl_paths does, so tonight's run does not report the tier broken by its own fix: staged-but-not-live is named as 'awaiting this run's rsync' and still keeps ok False, because a block only in growth_out is not one an answer engine can extract. No second staleness escalator — these pages ride the same rsync as /section8/ and /brief/, which t_crawl_paths already escalates on. Verified: py_compile clean on growth/*.py, growth_daily.py and build_seo.py; build --dry-run --docroot . --build-dir . exits 0; all three browse hubs render with answer blocks of 60/64/63 words inside answer_block's 45-80 assertion, three FAQ pairs each, FAQPage JSON-LD carrying 3 mainEntity items and matching the visible text; t_hub_direct_answers exercised against synthetic trees in all four states (staged-not-live -> 'awaiting this run's rsync', ok False; dry run -> ignores staging; after rsync -> ok True; a place page losing its block -> named as missing); a real non-dry build into a scratchpad published 255 hub pages and the post-rsync audit read 'answer block on 255 of 255 (dc hub 103/103, la hub 112/112, sf hub 37/37, city browse hub 3/3)'. That build overwrote growth/last_run.json — the droplet's ground truth — and it was restored from git along with the other ledger files, as yesterday's entry warned to check. Added no keywords and retired nothing. Did NOT run keywords.check_coverage().

**Watching.** (1) FIRST THING TOMORROW: count the 'growth: ledger' commits. Two for 2026-08-09/10 means today's run happened and only its push was lost — benign, and the 08-09 measurements will arrive with it. One, or none, means the crons have missed two days and that outranks everything in this file; the owner action is to check cron and the droplet itself, and the second-day-missing case is the point at which this stops being a note and becomes the finding. (2) The direct test of today's change is tomorrow's hub_direct_answers line. 'answer block on 623 of 626 ... city browse hub 0/3 — 3 awaiting this run's rsync' is the expected first night and is correct, not a failure. The night after it must read 626 of 626. If it reads a bare 'missing e.g. sf/buildings/index.html' with no pending note, the staging read did not fire — check that ctx.out exists on the droplet. (3) build.seo_corpus in last_run.json, which I could not read today. It was 7 days stale on 08-08. If tomorrow reads 9+ days, scripts/refresh_seo.sh has been dead for over a week and every build_seo.py edit since 08-01 — including today's browse-hub work, which reaches production through the growth build's own rsync rather than that pipeline, and the operator's 067883d Dataset markup, which does not — is still waiting on it. (4) The bet today's change makes is falsifiable but slow: do /sf/buildings/, /la/buildings/ or /dc/buildings/ earn a first non-zero impression? They have had 0 for 6 days. Judge it no earlier than 2026-08-23, and judge the tier by gsc_serving_pages crossing 90, not by any single page. (5) If the browse hubs are still at 0 on 2026-08-23 while the NYC building pages keep holding all 73 serving slots, then the SF/LA/DC tier is invisible rather than thin, the answer is discovery not quality, and T027 index triage moves from candidate to the next substantial build.


## 2026-08-10 — Two days of total droplet silence — and the git-conflict theory is disproven, so the cause is upstream of this repo. Made silence diagnosable
*6am review*

**Observed.** NO DROPLET ACTIVITY OF ANY KIND FOR TWO DAYS. The last commits from the engine are e934d11 'growth: ledger 2026-08-08' at 05:40:19Z, d244314 at 05:04:39Z, and 60e8d7d 're-rentals: daily sweep 2026-08-08' at 06:49:09Z. At 10:05Z today origin/main carries no ledger commit for 2026-08-09 or 2026-08-10 and no re-rentals sweep for either day; today's 05:40Z build is 4.5h overdue and yesterday's never arrived. That is three independent crons silent together. Every number below is therefore 2026-08-08's and is unchanged from yesterday's entry: GSC window 07-30..08-06, 20 impressions, 6 clicks, 80 serving pages (0.17% of ~47,600 published), search_share_pct 0.0 for the 14th straight day, 0 of 146 tracked queries ranking, coverage 120/146 (82.2%), gsc_serving_ever 172, gsc_serving_stable 71. Visitors 08-07: 17, of which 0 organic and 0 AI. paying_subs 0, mrr_usd 0.0. Per technique: hub_direct_answers still the only one with anything — 3 owned pages, 6 impressions, best position 1.3 — while fresh_section8, daily_brief, city_seo_expansion, city_guides, listings_freshness and b2b_api_licensing are all 0 owned pages and 0 owned impressions. No technique is due for revisit; the earliest is 2026-08-25, so step 3 was a genuine no-op. Checked yesterday's changed: line against the repo — commit 915bda5 contains all three claims (CITY_BROWSE_COPY, _place_word, _city_browse_answer and _city_browse_faq at build_seo.py:413-482; _answer_hub_families at growth/techniques.py:917 and its use at :972). All present, and none of it is in production, because no build has run since it was pushed. Reviewed the operator's 11 commits from 08-08 and 08-09: none touches growth_run.sh, growth_daily.py or the growth/ package's run path, though 067883d does rewrite all 700 lines of growth/techniques.json.

**Concluded.** Yesterday's entry set the test precisely — two ledger commits means the 08-09 run happened and only its push was lost, one or none means the crons have missed two days and it outranks everything in this file. The answer is none. So the benign lost-push-race reading is dead, this is now several days stale by any standard, and I escalated it by push notification rather than only writing it here. I then tried to name the cause and, importantly, RULED OUT the most attractive theory rather than shipping it as fact. The theory: 067883d rewrote every line of growth/techniques.json, the file the droplet writes and commits nightly, so a `git pull --rebase --autostash` on the droplet would hit an autostash-pop conflict and leave '<<<<<<<' markers that ledger.load_techniques() refuses to parse — wedging every later run. I reproduced that scenario end-to-end in a scratch repo and it is real but it is NOT what happened here, and the reproduction is worth recording twice over. First, the genuinely alarming half: `git pull --rebase --autostash` EXITS 0 when the autostash pop conflicts. The old script's `|| echo` could never have caught it, and the wedge is permanent — nothing later in the day clears it. Second, the half that clears the droplet: under the old script that scenario still produces a ledger commit, because `git add -A <path>` on an unmerged path resolves it and stages the marker text, so the run commits and pushes a corrupted techniques.json. We see no commit at all, and origin's techniques.json parses cleanly (status read all 38 records). Any invocation reaching line 17 rewrites last_run.json and results.jsonl and would have committed. No commit for two mornings therefore means the script never got that far, on either day, for both crons — and a third, unrelated cron went quiet at the same hour. That triangulates upstream of this repo entirely: the host, the cron daemon, or a full disk, which is the single cause that would take out three unrelated jobs at once and stay invisible in git. Nothing I can push fixes it; it is an owner action and it is the only thing that matters today. Consequence worth stating plainly: everything pushed since 08-07 is undeployed — the /section8/ and /brief/ crawl path, yesterday's browse-hub answer blocks and FAQ, and the operator's Dataset markup — and build.seo_corpus, already 7 days stale when last read on 08-08, cannot be read at all now. Research (step 4) turned up nothing new: the 2026 consensus is the same one the last three entries acted on — indexed-to-published ratio is the metric, Google will not crawl 47,600 pages when the first hundred are thin, consolidate rather than publish more — which reinforces T027 index triage and argues against any new page family. I deliberately did not spend the run on a content idea: a technique that cannot deploy is worth nothing, and the binding constraint today is that the engine is not running.

**Changed.** Hardened the one channel this loop has, so the next outage is diagnosable instead of costing cycles to guess at. Three days in a row have now been spent partly on 'what does silence mean' — 08-08 on an ambiguous ORPHANED line, 08-09 on a missing morning read as a possible push race, today on two. (1) growth_run.sh now appends a start and a finish record to growth/cron_heartbeat.jsonl around every invocation and commits that file in the same add list as the ledger, whether or not the run succeeded. The finish record carries the exit code, the pull's outcome, HEAD, and — on failure only — the last two non-empty lines of output, which is the one thing the droplet's logs never reach a cloud review. Failure only, and capped at 300 chars, because this file goes to a public repo and a successful run is already summarised in last_run.json. The invariant is the point: EVERY INVOCATION LEAVES A COMMIT, so no commit for a morning now means the script did not run, and can never again mean 'it ran but stayed quiet'. Written in sed/tr rather than python, because a heartbeat whose job is to report that python did not start cannot need python to be written. Trimmed to the last 200 records, ~50 days at two crons a day. (2) The same script now detects a conflicted checkout independently of the pull's exit code — `git ls-files -u` plus the .git/rebase-merge and rebase-apply directories, since the pull exits 0 on an autostash-pop conflict — aborts any rebase and resets the conflicted paths to origin/main, recording pull:recovered. Resetting ledger files to origin is safe: the droplet is their only writer and pushes them every run, so origin is its own last-known-good copy, and the run is about to regenerate them. (3) The push now retries three times with a rebase between attempts. It had none, which is what made the lost-race reading of 08-09 plausible in the first place. (4) growth_daily.py grows cron_liveness() and cmd_status prints it FIRST, above the ledger, with the last six records: it names 'NO RUN FOR N DAYS', a start with no finish as 'died mid-flight', a non-zero rc with the captured output, a recovered pull, and a missing file. (5) Created growth/cron_heartbeat.jsonl with one honest bootstrap record, so a future review can never read its absence as ambiguous — the trap this repo's gitignored files have already sprung once. (6) README documents all of it, including that the pull exits 0 on a stash-pop conflict. Verified: bash -n clean; py_compile clean on growth/*.py and growth_daily.py; build --dry-run --docroot . --build-dir . exits 0 and dirtied no ledger file (git status checked). End-to-end in a scratch repo with a bare remote: a run whose python exits 1 still commits and pushes, with the traceback in the heartbeat note; the operator-rewrite-plus-uncommitted-write conflict is detected, recovered, marked pull:recovered, and the NEXT run is not wedged; and the old script replayed against the identical conflict was confirmed to commit and push a techniques.json containing a conflict marker, which is the evidence that ruled the theory out above. cron_liveness exercised against all seven states. Added no keywords, retired nothing, no revisit was due. Did NOT run keywords.check_coverage(). HONEST WEAKNESS, stated plainly: this change reaches production only when the droplet pulls again, which is exactly what is not happening. It prevents the next outage from being a mystery; it does not fix today's. Today's fix is the notification.

**Watching.** (1) FIRST THING TOMORROW, before anything else: run `python3 growth_daily.py status` and read the cron line. If it shows droplet start/finish records, the outage is over and the heartbeat is working — then read the finish records for rc and pull, because a run can now report its own failure. If it still shows only the bootstrap record, either the droplet is still down or it is running an older growth_run.sh that predates the pull; distinguish those by whether ANY commit arrived from the engine. (2) If the outage is over, the first build back will publish everything stacked up since 08-07 at once: expect a large new/changed URL count, and check t_crawl_paths for /section8/ and /brief/ resolving to the city browse hubs and t_hub_direct_answers reading 626 of 626 — those are the still-unrun tests from 08-08 and 08-09, and the browse-hub bet cannot be judged before 2026-08-23. (3) build.seo_corpus, unreadable since 08-08 when it was 7 days stale. If it reads 10+ on the first run back, scripts/refresh_seo.sh has been dead a fortnight and every build_seo.py edit since 08-01 is still waiting on it. (4) If a run comes back reporting pull:recovered, the operator's pushes and the droplet's ledger writes are genuinely colliding and the real fix is to stop the droplet committing a file the operator hand-edits — worth doing then, not on speculation now. (5) If the crons are still silent on 2026-08-11, that is four days, the measurement record has a four-day hole, and no growth work in this repo means anything until someone touches the droplet: say so and do not ship a content change to make the run feel productive.


## 2026-08-11 — The crons are back — and the 08-09 browse-hub work was never going to deploy: the hands-off rule strands every improvement to a page the SEO pipeline already published
*6am review*

**Observed.** DROPLET IS ALIVE. cron_heartbeat.jsonl — shipped yesterday and paying off on day one — carries 2026-08-11T05:00:01Z start / 05:04:57Z finish rc=0 pull=ok for measure, and 05:40:01Z / 05:40:22Z rc=0 pull=ok for the build. Two clean runs, no pull:recovered. The 08-09 and 08-10 measurements arrived backfilled, so the record has no hole: visitors 19 (08-09) and 17 (08-10), organic 6 and 2, AI 0 and 0. GSC window 08-02..08-09: 15 impressions, 4 clicks, gsc_serving_pages 75 — down from 86 (08-10), 88 (08-09) and the 89 peak on 07-27 and 08-03/04 — against ~47,600 published, so 0.16%. That dip is soft: the window's last two days are always partial in GSC. gsc_serving_ever, which cannot decay, keeps climbing 172 -> 182 -> 186 -> 194, so pages are still earning first impressions; gsc_serving_stable 67. search_share_pct 0.0 for the 15th straight day, 0 of 146 tracked queries in the top 10, coverage 124/146 (84.9%). paying_subs 0, mrr_usd 0.0. Per technique, hub_direct_answers is STILL the only one with any GSC presence at all: 3 owned pages, 5 impressions, best position 1.3. fresh_section8, daily_brief, city_seo_expansion, city_guides, listings_freshness, b2b_api_licensing: 0 owned pages, 0 owned impressions. account_lifecycle sent 3 emails on 08-10. Nothing is due for revisit until 2026-08-25, so step 3 was a genuine no-op. Checked yesterday's changed: line against the repo — commit 3e4aee7 contains every claim (cron_heartbeat append and commit in growth_run.sh, git ls-files -u conflict detection, push retry, cron_liveness() at growth_daily.py:201, the bootstrap record). All present and all working. build.seo_corpus reads written 2026-08-08, 3 days old. And hub_direct_answers came back ok:false — 'city browse hub 0/3, missing dc/la/sf buildings/index.html', with NO 'awaiting this run's rsync' note.

**Concluded.** The 08-09 entry predicted that exact string and named the wrong cause. It said a bare 'missing e.g. sf/buildings/index.html' with no pending note would mean the staging read did not fire, and to go check ctx.out on the droplet. That is wrong, and I am recording the correction rather than acting on it: ctx.out is fine. The real cause is a design defect in _publish_stranded_city_hubs, and it is worse than a missing note because it is silent and permanent. Ownership between the two pipelines was decided by FALLBACK_MARKER alone: a docroot page WITHOUT our marker was written by the SEO pipeline, so the growth build never touched it again. That conflates two different claims — 'that pipeline published this page once' and 'that pipeline's copy is what the committed code renders'. The gap between them ate the 08-09 work whole. The SEO pipeline deployed the three city browse hubs on 08-08. On 08-09 a review added the answer block and FAQ to build_seo.city_hub_docs(). From that night on, every growth build imported that renderer, produced the improved page, compared it to the docroot, saw no marker, said hands-off, and threw the render away — while refresh_seo.sh, the only other path to the docroot, has not written since 08-08. So the pages were not waiting to be published; they were being discarded nightly, and would have been discarded indefinitely. Nothing in the daily report could have said so, because from the outside the page was live and only the answer-block audit was red. This matters more than a three-page bug because of what those three pages are: direct-answer blocks are the ONLY thing on this site with a measured search position (1.3, and the sole technique with any impressions at all), and the browse hubs are the top of the entire SF/LA/DC tier. It also means the strand generalises — any future improvement to a build_seo-rendered page that the SEO pipeline has already deployed is silently discarded, which is precisely the class of change these reviews keep making. Research (step 4) turned up nothing that survives the rules and nothing new: the 2026 consensus is the one the last four entries already acted on — thin pages are a liability under scaled-content-abuse, Google will not crawl 47,600 pages when the first hundred are thin, consolidate rather than publish more (reinforces T027, still a candidate), and on the AI side the weighting is answer-first formatting and FAQ schema ahead of backlinks. That last point is not a new idea, it is a direct endorsement of the work that was stuck, so I fixed the pipe rather than proposing another technique. Separately and honestly: refresh_seo.sh has now missed 08-09, 08-10 (explained by the outage) and 08-11 04:10 (NOT explained — the 05:00 and 05:40 crons ran fine today). One unexplained miss is not yet a finding, but that pipeline is the only one with no heartbeat reaching this repo, and it publishes 47,599 of the site's ~47,600 pages.

**Changed.** Replaced the marker-only ownership test with a content test, in growth/techniques.py. New _seo_page_is_current(live, ours, docroot): a page the SEO pipeline owns is left alone when its bytes equal what the committed renderer produces — the handover is real and there is nothing to do either way — and is taken over only when the bytes differ AND that pipeline has demonstrably missed a night (_seo_corpus_age >= SEO_STALE_DAYS = 2, the same threshold report.py uses to call it stalled). Applied at both call sites: _publish_stranded_city_hubs and _publish_stranded_guides. The conservatism is the point — a pipeline still deploying daily wins every disagreement, so the two never fight over one file, and when a stalled one catches up it rsyncs the exact bytes we render, the comparison goes equal, and the next run hands the page back on its own via the existing unstage() path. Byte comparison is sound because build_seo.page() embeds no date (BUILD_DATE appears only in sitemaps) and city_hub_pages writes d['html'] verbatim, so the render is deterministic — I checked both rather than assuming. In _publish_stranded_guides the render now happens before the ownership decision, and a render failure while a live SEO copy exists still reports 'seo' rather than a spurious 'render failed', so t_city_guides does not go red over a page that is serving fine. Added a 'refreshed' counter, distinct from the stranded-page count, and surfaced it: the detail line now says '... because the SEO pipeline is behind this checkout — N of them were live but stale, still serving the SEO pipeline's older render of the same page'. 'Published because it was missing' and 'republished because it was out of date' are different failures and a future review should not have to reconstruct which. Verified: py_compile clean on growth/*.py, growth_daily.py and build_seo.py; build --dry-run --docroot . --build-dir . exits 0 and dirtied no ledger file (git status checked, growth/state.json and growth_out removed after testing). build_seo.py runs end-to-end in a scratch dir: 47,599 pages, exit 0 — so that pipeline's silence is NOT a crash in the code we push. Five-state test against the real sf render: same bytes + fresh corpus, same bytes + stale corpus, and differing bytes + FRESH corpus all correctly hand off; differing bytes + stale corpus republishes; a marker-carrying page stays ours regardless. Then a production-shaped test — all 255 city hub pages seeded as SEO-owned, only the three browse hubs in their pre-08-09 shape, corpus stamped 3 days old — republishes exactly 3, hands 252 straight back, and flips t_hub_direct_answers from 'city browse hub 0/3' to '255 of 255 ... city browse hub 3/3', ok True. Added no keywords, retired nothing, no revisit was due. Did NOT run keywords.check_coverage().

**Watching.** (1) Tonight's city_seo_expansion detail is the direct test. Expect '3 pages published ... because the SEO pipeline is behind this checkout ... 3 of them were live but stale' and hub_direct_answers going green at 626 of 626 (or 623, the count moves with how many places clear MIN_CITY_HUB — judge the 'city browse hub 3/3' clause, not the total). If hub_direct_answers is still 0/3 tomorrow, my content-difference reading is wrong and the next thing to check is whether the droplet's docroot copy differs from our render for some reason other than the answer block. (2) If instead it reports MORE than 3 refreshed, the droplet's /root/dhcr-build is running materially older code than this checkout and the divergence is bigger than one commit — worth naming, not panicking about. (3) build.seo_corpus. It reads 3d today. If it reads 0d tomorrow, refresh_seo.sh recovered on its own and today's 04:10 miss was outage tail; the growth build will then hand all three hubs back within a night and that handover appearing in the detail is the proof the fix is not a takeover. If it reads 4d or more, that pipeline has missed a clean morning while the other two crons ran, and it is the next thing to fix — it has no heartbeat, it publishes 47,599 of ~47,600 pages, and giving it the same start/finish record growth_run.sh now has is the obvious next build. (4) gsc_serving_pages 75 today. Do not read the dip as a regression yet — the window's tail is partial and gsc_serving_ever rose to 194. Read it again on 08-13 against 86/88; if it is still in the 70s with serving_ever flat, that IS a real decline. (5) The browse-hub bet still cannot be judged before 2026-08-23, and now genuinely starts tonight rather than on 08-09 — it has been stranded for two of the days it was supposedly being tested, so give it to 2026-08-25 before deciding, and judge the tier by gsc_serving_pages crossing 90, not by any single page. (6) The generalisation is the thing to carry forward: any page rendered by build_seo.py that the SEO pipeline has already deployed was, until today, immune to improvement from this loop. If a future review edits build_seo.py and the change does not show up live, this is the first place to look.


## 2026-08-12 — The serving set stopped growing for the first time — and the pipeline that publishes 47,599 of 47,600 pages still cannot say why it is frozen. Gave it a voice
*6am review*

**Observed.** Both droplet crons green: cron_heartbeat.jsonl has 2026-08-12T05:00:01Z/05:04:59Z rc=0 pull=ok (measure) and 05:40:01Z/05:40:19Z rc=0 pull=ok (build). GSC window 08-03..08-10: 15 impressions, 4 clicks, gsc_serving_pages 65 — down from 75 (08-11), 86, 88 and the 89 peak held on 07-27 and 08-03/04 — so 0.14% of ~47,600 published. THE NEW FACT IS THE CHURN, NOT THE LEVEL: gsc_serving_ever is 194, exactly yesterday's 194, the first flat day in its history (172 -> 182 -> 186 -> 194 -> 194), and gsc_pages.json says gsc_serving_entered 0, gsc_serving_left 10, gsc_serving_stable 65 of 65. Zero pages earned a first impression in an eight-day window. Yesterday I said to re-read the dip on 08-13 and call it real only if serving_ever went flat; it went flat a day early, so the dip is not just a partial GSC tail. search_share_pct 0.0 for the 16th straight day, 0 of 146 tracked queries in the top 10, coverage 124/146 (84.9%). Visitors 08-11: 16, organic 3, AI 0. paying_subs 0, mrr_usd 0.0. WHAT THE 65 SERVING PAGES ACTUALLY ARE, from gsc_pages.json (129 impressions, 10 clicks total): 58 NYC building pages (manhattan 24 pages/48 imp, brooklyn 24/32, bronx 10/13, queens 1/2), the homepage (20 imp, 8 clicks), /dc/ /la/ /sf/ at 4 imp each, and 2 zip hubs at 1 imp each. Zero from /section8/, zero from /brief/, zero from the 255 SF/LA/DC hub pages, zero from the 3 browse hubs, zero from the 3 city guides, and 2 of ~368 NYC neighborhood/borough/zip hubs. And discovered_untracked is brutal: 'jays home finder' 8 imp/2 clicks, 'jayshomefinder' 4 imp, 'jayshomefinder.com' 2 imp/2 clicks, '180 thompson st unit le new york, ny 10012' 1 imp at position 36. That is 14 of 15 query-level impressions and 4 of 4 clicks going to a decommissioned brand that used to hold this domain. In eight days the site earned ZERO impressions from rent-stabilization intent. build.seo_corpus: written 2026-08-08, 4 days old. Checked yesterday's changed: line against the repo — commit 23aa664 contains every claim (_seo_page_is_current at growth/techniques.py:1087, applied at both _publish_stranded_guides and _publish_stranded_city_hubs, the 'refreshed' counter and its detail clause) and it WORKED on its first night: city_seo_expansion reports '3 pages published by the daily growth build, browse hubs included (dc 1, la 1, sf 1) ... 3 of them were live but stale'. No revisit is due until 2026-08-25, so step 3 was a genuine no-op.

**Concluded.** Two things, and the second is the one I could act on. (1) The serving set is now shrinking and nothing is entering it. 0 entered / 10 left is a different animal from a low number: 89 -> 65 with serving_ever pinned means Google is not merely ignoring the 47,600 pages, it is retiring the handful it had. Combined with the page list — the serving set is essentially NYC building pages plus four index pages, and every single thing this loop has published in three weeks (/section8/, /brief/, 255 city hub pages, 3 browse hubs, 3 guides) has never earned one impression — the honest reading is that this is a crawl-demand problem, not a content-quality problem, and it is the case T027 index triage was written for. I did not start T027 today, deliberately: it is a large consolidation build and starting it while the pipeline that would deploy it is frozen would repeat the exact mistake of 2026-08-09, when a review improved pages that were then discarded nightly. (2) I CORRECTED MY OWN PREDICTION FROM YESTERDAY, twice over. Yesterday I wrote that if hub_direct_answers still read 'city browse hub 0/3' today, my content-difference reading was wrong. It still reads 0/3 — and my reading was right; the prediction was wrong for an unrelated reason I had not looked for. hub_direct_answers sat at position 3 in techniques.ORDER, ahead of BOTH techniques that publish the pages it audits. It reads the docroot and falls back to ctx.out for pages the run staged, but nothing had been staged when it ran, so its fallback could never fire and it was reporting last night's docroot as though it were tonight's result. That is why the 08-09 entry predicted '3 awaiting this run's rsync', got a flat 'missing', and sent the 08-11 review chasing a staging bug in ctx.out that did not exist. Two reviews burned on a list ordering. (3) The deeper problem behind the frozen corpus is that refresh_seo.sh publishes 47,599 of ~47,600 pages and reports NOTHING about itself. All any review has ever had is an mtime — 'last written 2026-08-08 (4d ago)' — and that one fact is equally consistent with a cron that never fired, a build_seo.py that crashed, and an rsync that failed. Those are three different owner actions, four mornings have now been spent unable to choose between them, and the daily report's own callout ends with 'needs a hand on the droplet' without saying which hand. That is fixable from git, so I fixed it. Research (step 4) again produced nothing new that survives the rules: the 2026 consensus is the one the last five entries acted on — crawl demand is popularity and importance rather than page count, thin programmatic pages are a liability, consolidate instead of publishing more, and on the AI side put a direct answer at the top and use FAQ schema. The one figure I had not seen is that unlinked brand mentions correlate with AI citations far more strongly than backlinks, which is real but not something a solo operator can act on without buying mentions, so I discarded it.

**Changed.** Gave the SEO pipeline a voice, and fixed the ordering bug that made two reviews wrong. (1) scripts/refresh_seo.sh now writes .seo-build-status.json into the DOCROOT at start and again on exit via an EXIT trap, carrying the step that was in flight (startup/pull/build/count/deploy/indexnow/done), the exit code, the commit it built from, the URLs that changed, and the number of pages the build produced. The docroot is the only place both pipelines can see and the growth build's push is the only channel that reaches this cloud review, so that is the route: refresh_seo.sh runs from /root/dhcr-build and can never commit anything. STRUCTURED FIELDS ONLY, never captured output — growth_run.sh's heartbeat may carry two lines of a traceback because it writes into a repo it controls, but this one writes into a web-served docroot from a directory holding indexnow.key, and a step name plus an exit code is the whole diagnosis anyway. Every write is best-effort so a heartbeat can never be the reason the night's rebuild fails. (2) growth/techniques.py gains _seo_pipeline_status(), which whitelists the fields (this record is copied into a public repo) and derives a one-line state: 'FAILED rc=N in step X', 'STARTED BUT NEVER FINISHED', 'NO RUN FOR N DAYS', or 'ran clean' — plus one the mtime can never catch, a build that completes and rsyncs a TRUNCATED corpus, which looks freshly written from outside; a clean run reporting under 1,000 pages against the real ~47,600 is called a break. (3) growth_daily.py folds it into last_run.json as build.seo_pipeline and cmd_status prints it under the cron line. (4) report.py's 'SEO corpus frozen' callout now names the failing step, because the step IS the fix. (5) techniques.ORDER moves hub_direct_answers after city_guides and city_seo_expansion, where an audit belongs. Verified: py_compile clean on growth/*.py, growth_daily.py and build_seo.py; bash -n clean; build --dry-run --docroot . --build-dir . exits 0 and left the tree clean (git status checked). The shell heartbeat was run end-to-end in a scratch tree through four states — clean run (rc=0, step=done, counts right), a build that exits 3 (rc=3, step=build), missing changed_urls.txt and a missing seo/ dir (rc=0, counts 0, no stderr noise), and an UNWRITABLE docroot (rc=0, the run completes — the heartbeat cannot break the pipeline). _seo_pipeline_status was tested against 13 inputs including a killed run, a failed rsync, a truncated corpus, a stale-AND-crashed record, an unparseable timestamp, absent/corrupt/non-dict files, and a hostile file carrying an extra 'secret' field and a 500-char head — the unknown field is dropped and the string is truncated to 64. The ORDER change was A/B'd against a production-shaped scratch docroot (all 255 city hub pages seeded as SEO-owned, the 3 browse hubs in their pre-08-09 shape, corpus stamped 4 days old, same run both ways): old order says 'city browse hub 0/3 — missing e.g. dc/buildings/index.html', new order says 'city browse hub 0/3 — 3 awaiting this run's rsync, the block is in the page this run staged but not yet in the docroot', and correctly stays ok:False either way. README documents both. Added no keywords, retired nothing, no revisit was due, did NOT run keywords.check_coverage(). HONEST LIMITATION, stated plainly: refresh_seo.sh pulls itself, so the new version only runs the night AFTER the droplet next pulls — expect the first status record on 2026-08-14, not tomorrow. And if that cron is not firing at all, it never pulls and never self-reports; the absence then becomes the evidence, which is why the README now gives the decision rule rather than leaving it to be re-derived.

**Watching.** (1) build.seo_pipeline in last_run.json. Absent on 08-13 and 08-14 means only that the droplet has not pulled the new script yet — do not read it as a failure. From 08-15 on, still absent AND seo_corpus still frozen is positive evidence that the 04:10 cron is not firing at all, which is the one thing the file cannot report from the inside and is precisely what it narrows to; say so and escalate, because at that point every build_seo.py change since 08-01 has been dead in git for two weeks. If it IS present, read the state line and stop guessing: it names the step. (2) hub_direct_answers tonight. It should now read 'city browse hub 3/3' and go ok:True, because the 08-11 fix already rsynced the three answer-block hubs on 08-12 and the audit now runs after the publishers. If it reads 0/3 with the new 'awaiting this run's rsync' clause, the pages are being staged and re-staged and the rsync is not landing them, which is a different and worse bug than the one I fixed. If it reads 0/3 with a flat 'missing' again, the ORDER change did not deploy. (3) gsc_serving_ever. Flat at 194 today for the first time. Two flat days in a row with gsc_serving_pages still in the 60s makes this a confirmed discovery collapse, not a wobble, and at that point T027 index triage stops being a candidate and becomes the next substantial build — provided the deploy path is alive, which is condition (1). If serving_ever resumes climbing, today's zero was a window artefact and the level matters less than I thought. (4) The browse-hub bet from 08-09 genuinely started on 08-12, not 08-09 — it was stranded for the first two days. Do not judge it before 2026-08-25, and judge the tier by gsc_serving_pages crossing 90, not by any single page. (5) Carry this forward for whoever reads it next: 14 of 15 query impressions are 'jayshomefinder', a brand that has nothing to do with this site. When someone next reports impressions or clicks as progress, subtract that first — the real rent-stabilization number for 08-03..08-10 is one impression, at position 36.


## 2026-08-13 — 94.9% of building pages had two or fewer inbound internal links — the corpus was a star, not a graph. Made every neighborhood a ring
*6am review*
