**Observed.** Both droplet crons green: cron_heartbeat.jsonl has 2026-08-13T05:00:01Z/05:00:13Z rc=0 pull=ok (measure) and 05:40:01Z/05:40:20Z rc=0 pull=ok (build --deploy), deployed:true. GSC window 08-04..08-11: 14 impressions, 3 clicks, gsc_serving_pages 66 of ~47,600 published (0.14%). CORRECTION TO YESTERDAY: I called gsc_serving_ever going flat (194 -> 194) the finding of the day and read it as discovery having stopped. It resumed the next morning — 205 today, with gsc_serving_entered 11, left 10, stable 55 — so 08-12 was a one-day artifact of a partial GSC tail, not a freeze. The level is the finding; the churn was not. search_share_pct 0.0 for the 17th straight day, 0 of 146 tracked queries in the top 10, coverage 124/146 (84.9%). Visitors 08-12: 20, organic 2, AI 0. paying_subs 0, mrr_usd 0.0. WHAT SERVES, from gsc_pages.json: 61 NYC building pages (98 impressions), the homepage (16), /dc/ /la/ /sf/ at 4 each, one ZIP hub at 1. Still zero from /section8/, /brief/, the 255 SF/LA/DC hub pages, the 3 browse hubs and the 3 guides. Query-level: 'jays home finder' 6 imp/1 click, 'jayshomefinder' 4, 'jayshomefinder.com' 2/2 — 12 of 14 impressions and 3 of 3 clicks are the decommissioned brand that used to hold this domain — plus '180 thompson st unit le new york, ny 10012' at position 36 and '38 6 avenue brooklyn' at position 82. hub_direct_answers, the only technique with any GSC presence, is fading: 3 pages/6 imp/best position 1.3 on 08-10, 2/2/pos 4 on 08-12, 1/1/pos 8 today. Checked yesterday's changed: line against the repo — commit 57c85b9 contains every claim (the refresh_seo.sh heartbeat and its EXIT trap, _seo_pipeline_status, cron/seo verdicts in growth_daily.py status, and techniques.ORDER moved hub_direct_answers behind city_guides and city_seo_expansion). The ordering fix worked on its first night: hub_direct_answers reads 'answer block on 623 of 623 hub pages ... city browse hub 3/3', where it read 0/3 every night before. build.seo_corpus: written 2026-08-08, 5 days old, and build.seo_pipeline is null — which per README means nothing yet, because refresh_seo.sh pulls itself and cannot self-report until it next runs; an absence only becomes evidence from 08-15. Nothing was due for revisit, but see below. NEW, and the reason for today's change: I measured the internal link graph of the building corpus from buildings.min.json. Every building page's 'Other rent-stabilized buildings in <neighborhood>' block was sorted_by_address[:12] — the same 12 addresses on every page in the neighborhood. So 44,738 of 47,165 building pages (94.9%) had two or fewer inbound internal links site-wide, 11,294 had exactly one (their neighborhood hub, and nothing else — they are past the 400-address cap on their ZIP hub), and only 2,430 buildings, 5.2%, were ever linked from a sibling page. In Bushwick (West) 13 addresses collected 1,196 inbound links each while the other 1,184 pages had one.

**Concluded.** The corpus is not thin, it is unreachable, and that is a different fix from the one the last five entries kept circling. 198 neighborhood hubs carrying up to 1,197 links each were the only route into 44,738 of the 47,165 building pages, and current large-site guidance is blunt that internal link depth and inbound link count are what decide which of a big programmatic inventory gets crawled and indexed — the same sources say roughly a quarter of pages on large sites get zero internal links and that each page wants 5-20. 66 pages serving out of ~47,600 is what a star topology looks like from outside, and it explains something 'thin content' does not: the pages that DO serve are ordinary building pages with nothing special about them, and every family this loop has published since 07-27 — /section8/, /brief/, 255 city hubs, 3 browse hubs, 3 guides — has a crawl path (t_crawl_paths is green, 12 of 12) but nothing else pointing at it. I checked whether the serving set is enriched for the 13 sibling-linked buildings per neighborhood: 6 of 61 serving building pages fall in that set against 2.64 expected if serving were uniform. That is 2.3x, and at n=61 it is suggestive at best — I am recording it as weak, and today's change does NOT rest on it. It rests on the link counts, which are exact. Honest limits, stated plainly. This ships in build_seo.py, which only reaches the docroot through refresh_seo.sh, and that pipeline has not written since 2026-08-08 — so the change is undeployed until it recovers, exactly like the 08-09 browse-hub work was. I judged it worth doing anyway because the building corpus is the ONLY tier on this site earning impressions (98 of 129 in the window) and the growth build's own rsync cannot publish 47k pages, so there is no version of this fix that ships through the working path. Also unchanged and still not working: revenue is /bin/bash with 0 paying subs, the  Building Report has not converted in 17 days, and 0 of 146 tracked queries rank. Scout is fine — the API usage cap that ran to 2026-08-01 is behind us and scout proposed T039 as recently as 08-11 — so there is nothing to diagnose there. Still blocked and not worked around: AdSense has no slot id so no ads render, and api@findacrib.com may not forward.

**Changed.** build_seo.py: turned the sibling-link star into a ring. New _geo_ring_order() orders each neighborhood on a snaked longitude-column sort so consecutive entries are physically adjacent, and _ring_window() gives each building the 12 entries around its own position, wrapping — so every page links to 12 siblings and is linked FROM 12 instead of 94.9% of pages hanging off a single hub link. Verified by rendering 2,962 real pages (East Village, Greenpoint, Ridgewood, St. George) and parsing the emitted HTML: sibling in-degree min 12, max 12, median 12, zero orphans, in-degree equals out-degree for every page. Geographic rather than alphabetical because the same window then reads as a real neighbour list — median distance between linked buildings 76 m against 466 m under the old slice; the sample page for 320R E 11th St now lists 306/314/318/322 E 11th St and 201-213 E 10th St. Kept the heading 'Other rent-stabilized buildings in <neighborhood>', which the ordering cannot falsify, rather than 'nearby', which a snake's column boundary would occasionally make untrue. Deterministic and idempotent — a second full run reported 0 pages changed — which matters because seo_lastmod.json only bumps <lastmod> when a page's HTML really changed, and a shuffled list would rewrite 47,165 lastmods every night. WARNING FOR THE NEXT REVIEW: the first refresh_seo.sh run after this lands legitimately rewrites every building page, so expect ~47k changed URLs, a one-time site-wide lastmod bump and IndexNow submitting its 10,000 cap. That is the change being real, not a bug. Also: growth/techniques.json — T015 (dataset_schema_ai_citation) and T016 (embeddable_building_lookup_widget) were active with revisit_on null, and ledger.revisit_due() skips a null, so those two could never come up for revisit; set to 2026-09-12 via ledger.set_revisit(). The hole opens whenever a technique is made active by hand-editing the ledger instead of through set_status(). README.md documents the two link audits and why they are different scales. Verified: py_compile clean, build --dry-run --docroot . --build-dir . exits 0, identical to the pre-change baseline.

**Watching.** Three things. (1) Does refresh_seo.sh run at all — from 2026-08-15 a still-absent build.seo_pipeline plus a still-frozen seo_corpus is positive evidence the cron is not firing, which is an owner action and nothing this loop can push. (2) Once the corpus does rebuild with the ring in it, the number to watch is gsc_serving_ever, not gsc_serving_pages: ever cannot decay, and its slope (172 -> 182 -> 186 -> 194 -> 194 -> 205 over 08-08..08-13) is the cleanest read on whether Google is discovering more of the corpus. If the ring works, that slope should steepen within two to three weeks of the first rebuild; if it is flat a month after the rebuild, crawl demand is not about internal links here and T027 index triage — consolidate rather than link — becomes the next move. (3) Check whether pages entering the serving set are buildings OUTSIDE the old 13-per-neighborhood set, which is the direct test of today's mechanism rather than of general growth: rank each new page in its neighborhood's address sort and compare against the 6-of-61 baseline recorded above. Also keep watching hub_direct_answers, which has now fallen 1.3 -> 4 -> 8 in best position over four days on 1-3 pages; too small to act on, but if it reaches zero owned impressions the only measured win this site has ever had is gone.


## 2026-08-14 — The 04:10 SEO cron has not fired for six nights and that is now provable — gave growth_run.sh a watchdog, and rewrote 47,165 empty meta descriptions
*6am review*

**Observed.** Both droplet crons green: cron_heartbeat.jsonl has 2026-08-14T05:00:01Z/05:07:14Z rc=0 pull=ok (daily) and 05:40:01Z/05:40:20Z rc=0 pull=ok (build --deploy), deployed:true, 7 changed URLs, 1 new. Scout ran clean 2026-08-13T13:08-13:11 rc=0, so there is no API problem to diagnose. GSC window 2026-08-05..08-12: gsc_serving_pages 68 of ~47,600 published (0.14%), gsc_serving_ever 219 (194 -> 205 -> 219, so the 08-12 flat reading really was the artifact 08-13 said it was), entered 14, left 12, stable 54. search_share_pct 0.0 for the 18th straight day; 0 of 171 measured tracked queries in the top 10; coverage 142/171 (83.0%) at measure time, keywords.json locally now holds 196. Visitors 08-13: 21, organic 5, AI 0. paying_subs 0, mrr_usd 0.0. Checked yesterday's changed: line against the repo — commit 29255e0 contains every claim (_geo_ring_order and _ring_window in build_seo.py, the README link-audit paragraphs, and T015/T016 given revisit_on 2026-09-12 in techniques.json). Nothing was due for revisit; earliest is 2026-08-25.
TWO MEASUREMENTS I HAD NOT SEEN BEFORE. (1) IMPRESSIONS HAVE BEEN UNDERSTATED ~12x. Over the identical window Search Console's query dimension totals 11 impressions and 1 click, while its page dimension totals 137 impressions and 6 clicks over the 68 pages. The query dimension drops anonymized rare queries, and on a corpus of 47k single-address pages nearly every query is rare. Every journal entry that has ever quoted 'impressions' — including 08-13's '14 impressions, 3 clicks' — quoted the query number. (2) THE BUILDING CORPUS RANKS AND IS NOT CLICKED. 62 serving building pages, 111 impressions, 2 clicks, median position 6.0. 48 of the 62 sit in the top 10 and hold 96 of the 111 impressions; 15 are in the top 3. Meanwhile 9 of the 11 named-query impressions and the one named click are 'jayshomefinder', the decommissioned brand; the only two real named queries in the window are address lookups at positions 36 and 82.
WHAT HAS NEVER SERVED. Of the 219 pages that have EVER earned an impression, 206 are /building/, 7 are /zip/, 2 are /neighborhood/, plus the homepage and /dc/ /la/ /sf/. Zero, in the entire recorded history, from /section8/, /brief/, /guide/, the 255 SF/LA/DC hub pages or the 3 browse hubs.
SEO PIPELINE: build.seo_pipeline still null, seo_corpus written 2026-08-08 (6d old).
DESCRIPTION AUDIT: strip the address and the neighborhood out of all 47,165 building meta descriptions and they collapse to 5 distinct strings. 13 trailing words were identical on every page.

**Concluded.** THE 04:10 SEO CRON IS NOT FIRING, and this is now provable — a day earlier than the rule the last two entries left, and by a route that does not need the status record at all. build_seo.py's write() opens and rewrites every file it emits on every run unconditionally (only <lastmod> is content-aware, via LM_STATE), and sitemap-main.xml embeds BUILD_DATE, so a healthy run gives each sitemap-*.xml in $BUILD fresh bytes AND a fresh mtime even on a night when not one page changed, and rsync -a copies them into the docroot. A docroot sitemap frozen at 2026-08-08 therefore proves refresh_seo.sh has not reached STEP=deploy for six nights, whatever any status file says. The status file then narrows how it stopped: status start is written before the pull and before the build, so even an instant crash leaves it behind — its absence on the first morning it was expected means the script never started. Not 'fired and crashed'. Did not fire.
I ALSO THINK MY PREDECESSOR GAVE UP ON THIS TOO EARLY. Both the 08-12 and 08-13 entries called it 'an owner action and nothing this loop can push'. That is wrong: growth_run.sh is on the droplet, in the right checkout, with GROWTH_DOCROOT already exported, and it demonstrably runs every single morning. It can run the refresh the missing cron owes. Six days of not doing so stranded the browse hubs from 08-09, the sibling-link ring from 08-13, and every build_seo.py change since 08-01 — a fortnight of this loop's substantial work sitting dead in git while the daily report faithfully reported it as shipped.
THE BUILDING CORPUS IS A SNIPPET PROBLEM, NOT A RANKING PROBLEM — at least in the one tier that has ever worked. 48 of 62 serving pages in the top 10 producing 2 clicks is roughly 2% CTR where top-10 positions normally earn several times that, and current guidance is consistent that low CTR at a good position is snippet appeal or an intent/promise mismatch. HONEST LIMIT, because this matters: n=111 impressions and 2 clicks is a weak sample and I am NOT claiming a statistically established CTR deficit. The change does not rest on it. It rests on the description audit, which is exact and needs no statistics: the meta description — the only line of the page a searcher reads before deciding — carried no information at all beyond what the title already said, and its tail promised facts ('See units, year built, owner, and HPD violations') rather than stating them, on all 47,165 pages.
STILL NOT WORKING, BLUNTLY. 0 of 171 tracked queries rank; 18 consecutive days at 0.0% search share against a 90% goal. Revenue is still $0/mo with 0 paying subs, and the $9 Building Report has not converted once in 18 days. And the finding I keep coming back to: every page family this loop has published since 2026-07-27 — /section8/, /brief/, 255 city hubs, 3 browse hubs, 3 guides — has never earned a single impression across 219 pages of serving history. Publishing more families is not the constraint and has not been for three weeks; getting the one family that does rank deployed, linked and clicked is.
Blocked and not worked around: AdSense still has no slot id so no ads render, and api@findacrib.com may not forward.

**Changed.** Three things, in descending order of how much they matter.
(1) growth_run.sh: a SEO pipeline watchdog. seo_watchdog() runs scripts/refresh_seo.sh when the corpus is stale, from the growth checkout growth_run.sh has just pulled — so the script is always the newest version instead of one night behind its own self-pull, and refresh_seo.sh still cds to $SEO_BUILD and pulls it, so data and build dir are unchanged. Deliberately conservative: only on the build job, never on measure or scout; only when every non-growth-owned sitemap-*.xml in the docroot is older than SEO_STALE_DAYS (default 2), so a healthy 04:10 cron that wrote at 04:10 is never raced at 05:40; under flock so two can never overlap; under timeout (default 3600s) so a hung rebuild cannot hold the ledger push hostage; every failure swallowed so it can never be the reason the night's measurements go unpushed, and rc stays the python run's. GROWTH_SEO_WATCHDOG=0 in growth.env turns it off — that is the switch if the pipeline is ever stopped on purpose. Outcome lands in cron_heartbeat.jsonl as seo_watchdog_start/seo_watchdog_finish, which is committed and pushed, so the result reaches this review the same morning. Tested against a scratch droplet layout, eight cases: stale corpus fires; fresh corpus skips; a FRESH sitemap-daily.xml does NOT mask a stale corpus (the exact bug that blinded _seo_corpus_age once before); rc and the last two log lines are reported on failure; a hung script returns at the timeout with rc=124 in 2.03s; a second copy is refused by the lock and — after a fix — says so instead of reporting 'ok', which would have been the 2026-07-28 'unknown error' mistake in the other direction; the measure job, GROWTH_SEO_WATCHDOG=0 and a missing build dir all skip silently.
(2) build_seo.py: real per-building meta descriptions. New building_meta_desc() replaces the one-slot template with the facts already in the record and already shown in the body — 'about 8 apartments, built 1910, registered as rent stabilized with NY State DHCR. 4 open HPD violations on file.' Keeps the body's own 'about' hedge on the unit count rather than quietly upgrading a PLUTO approximation into a fact, and asserts nothing the page does not already assert. Measured on all 47,165 real records: descriptions distinct after removing the address and neighborhood go from 5 to 28,825; identical trailing words go from 13 to 0; length min 76, median 144, max 158, zero over budget; deterministic across runs, so it does not churn honest-lastmod. 19 buildings pair a long address with a long neighborhood name and blew the budget on the lead alone, truncating away the DHCR claim itself — those now drop the neighborhood, which the title carries anyway. Only 11 pages of 47,165 (0.02%) end up with no per-building fact. This rides the same one-time site-wide lastmod bump the 08-13 ring change already causes, so it costs nothing extra.
(3) growth/searchconsole.py + report.py + growth_daily.py: record gsc_page_clicks and gsc_page_impressions from the page dimension, and show those in the report and the run log. gsc_clicks/gsc_impressions are deliberately NOT redefined — that series runs back to the start and would stop being comparable to itself, and the query total is still the right number for 'which named queries do we appear for'. Two metrics, two meanings. The report falls back to the query numbers for days before today with `is None` rather than `or`, so a genuine zero is not mistaken for a missing value, and a note block states which dimension is shown. Rendered both parts from the one block list; HTML and text agree and no markup was hand-written.
Added no keywords — the tracked set is 171 head terms of which 0 rank, and adding address-shaped queries would only inflate share_pct without moving the goal. Retired nothing. No revisit was due. Did NOT run keywords.check_coverage(). Verified: py_compile clean on growth/*.py, growth_daily.py and build_seo.py; build --dry-run --docroot . --build-dir . exits 0 and is byte-identical to the pre-change baseline.

**Watching.** (1) THE ONE THAT DECIDES EVERYTHING ELSE: cron_heartbeat.jsonl tomorrow. If seo_watchdog_start appears, the watchdog deployed and fired, and its seo_watchdog_finish rc plus build.seo_pipeline in last_run.json will — for the first time — say exactly why this pipeline stops. rc=0 and a seo_corpus dated 2026-08-15 means six days of stranded work just shipped in one night, and expect ~47k changed URLs, a one-time site-wide lastmod bump and IndexNow submitting its 10,000 cap; that is the backlog being real, not a bug. If seo_watchdog_start is ABSENT tomorrow, the watchdog itself has not deployed yet (growth_run.sh is pulled by growth_run.sh, same self-pull lag as refresh_seo.sh) — read an absence on 08-15 as the lag and an absence on 08-16 as a genuine failure of the growth cron's own pull, which would be a far more serious problem than the one I was fixing.
(2) Once the corpus does rebuild, gsc_serving_ever is the number, not gsc_serving_pages: ever cannot decay. It has run 105 -> 219 over 08-01..08-14 while the trailing window fell 89 -> 68, which is broad discovery with no retention. If the ring plus the new descriptions work, the ever slope should steepen and the gap between ever and pages should NARROW. If ever keeps climbing while pages stays in the 60s a month after the rebuild, pages are being tried and dropped, and T027 index triage — consolidate rather than publish — becomes the next substantial build.
(3) The description change is testable and I want it tested honestly, not assumed. Baseline recorded here for whoever reads this next: 62 serving building pages, 111 page-dimension impressions, 2 clicks, median position 6.0, 48 of 62 in the top 10. Compare clicks per top-10 impression at least three weeks AFTER the corpus actually rebuilds — not three weeks after today, because today's push is not live and may not be for a while. If CTR is unchanged on a bigger sample, snippet appeal was not the problem and I was wrong to spend the run on it.
(4) Do not read the corrected impressions number as growth. 137 is the same footprint 11 was; only the measurement got honest. The first entry to compare a page-dimension figure against a pre-08-14 query-dimension figure will invent a 12x jump that did not happen.
(5) Carried forward, still true: 9 of 11 named-query impressions are 'jayshomefinder', a decommissioned brand with nothing to do with this site. Subtract it before reporting any impression or click as progress.


## 2026-08-15 — The watchdog fired: the SEO pipeline ran for the first time in seven nights. And gsc_serving_pages was never an indexing number — now we measure the real one
*6am review*

**Observed.** THE WATCHDOG WORKED. cron_heartbeat.jsonl 2026-08-15: build --deploy start 05:40:01Z, finish 05:40:17Z rc=0 pull=ok, then seo_watchdog_start 05:40:17Z 'corpus stale >2d, running scripts/refresh_seo.sh' and seo_watchdog_finish 05:41:25Z rc=0 'ran refresh_seo.sh to completion'. It deployed on its first night — the 08-14 entry's worry about self-pull lag did not materialise. 68 seconds sounds too fast for 47.6k pages; it is not. I ran build_seo.py against this checkout's own buildings.min.json: 47,854 pages, 764 MB, 21 seconds wall clock. Build plus rsync plus IndexNow in 68s is exactly right. Daily cron also green (05:00:01Z/05:06:25Z rc=0). Note last_run.json still says seo_corpus written 2026-08-08 (7d) — the build reads the corpus age BEFORE the watchdog runs, so that field is one night stale by construction; tomorrow's reading is the test.
GSC window 2026-08-06..08-13: gsc_serving_pages 63, the lowest ever recorded (89 -> 77 -> 82 -> 82 -> 78 -> 81 -> 89 -> 89 -> 84 -> 82 -> 80 -> 80 -> 88 -> 86 -> 75 -> 65 -> 66 -> 68 -> 63). gsc_serving_ever 224, and its slope is flattening: +11, +14, +5 over the last three days against +9 to +14 through early August. stable 58, entered 5, left 10. Page dimension 122 impressions / 5 clicks; query dimension 6 impressions / 0 clicks. search_share_pct 0.0 for the 19th consecutive day, 0 of 197 tracked queries in the top 10, coverage 161/197 (81.7%). Visitors 08-14: 24, organic 1, AI 1. paying_subs 0, mrr_usd 0.0, still nothing from the $9 Building Report in 19 days.
DO NOT READ 5 CLICKS AS THE SNIPPET CHANGE WORKING. Three of the five are the homepage, whose only named queries in the window are 'jays home finder' (3 impressions, position 5.3) and 'jayshomefinder' (1, position 5) — the decommissioned brand. The building corpus took 2 clicks on 115 impressions, i.e. 1.7%, statistically identical to yesterday's 2 on 111. VERIFIED YESTERDAY'S DIFF: commit 16c1473 contains every claim — seo_watchdog()/SEO_STALE_DAYS/GROWTH_SEO_WATCHDOG in growth_run.sh, building_meta_desc() in build_seo.py, gsc_page_clicks/gsc_page_impressions in searchconsole.py, report.py and growth_daily.py.
