No revisit was due; earliest is T001-T008 on 2026-08-25.
FULL LINK-GRAPH AUDIT OF THE REAL CORPUS, first time this has been done against generated output rather than the 62-page docroot sample. Parsed all 47,854 pages: building pages now have min 1 / median 14 / mean 13.8 inbound internal links, so the 08-13 sibling ring did what it claimed — the 94.9%-with-two-or-fewer figure is gone. Exactly four orphans on the whole site, all /borough/<boro>/no-violations/. /guide/ links all nine guides, hubs link their buildings, nothing is stranded. Internal linking is no longer a constraint here.
WHAT HAS STILL NEVER SERVED: of the 224 pages that have ever earned an impression, 211 are /building/, 7 /zip/, 2 /neighborhood/, plus the homepage and /dc/ /la/ /sf/. Zero from /section8/, /brief/, /guide/, the 255 city hub pages or the 3 browse hubs, in the entire recorded history.

**Concluded.** GSC_SERVING_PAGES IS NOT AN INDEXING NUMBER AND NEVER WAS. This is the correction that matters today, and it invalidates a premise every entry since 2026-07-27 has been reasoning from — including three of my own. searchconsole.py's docstring called it 'the only honest read on whether the 47k-page corpus is actually indexed'; the daily report labelled it 'Indexing'; the seed note for T010 said the same. It is not. An impression requires two independent things: the page is in Google's index, AND somebody typed a query it could answer. On 47,165 single-address pages the second condition is what almost every page fails — nobody searches most NYC addresses in a given week. So '63 of ~47,600 pages served (0.13%)' is exactly as consistent with 63 indexed pages as it is with 47,000 indexed pages, and those two worlds demand opposite responses: if few pages are indexed the constraint is crawl and index acceptance and T027 consolidation is the move; if most are indexed the constraint is search demand, publishing more address pages cannot possibly help, and the whole effort belongs on the query families that have volume. Nothing in this repo could tell those apart. Three weeks of reviews have been steering on a number that cannot answer the question it was being asked.
I THINK THE DEMAND SIDE IS THE MORE LIKELY STORY, AND I AM DELIBERATELY NOT ACTING ON THAT YET. The evidence pointing that way: the pages that do serve rank well (49 of 63 in the top 10, median position around 5), which is not what an index-acceptance problem looks like; the serving set churns (5 in, 10 out) around a stable core of 58, which reads like different rare queries firing on different days rather than pages being dropped; and serving_ever climbing to 224 while the trailing window falls to 63 is what you would see if discovery is fine and demand is thin. But that is inference, and the last three weeks are a demonstration of what inference on the wrong metric costs. Measure it first.
THE SECOND-ORDER LESSON, AND WHY THIS KEEPS HAPPENING: both of the last two corrections — impressions understated 12x on 2026-08-14, indexing conflated with footprint today — were not wrong measurements. They were correct measurements read as answering a question they do not answer. That failure mode does not announce itself; the number looks fine and the reasoning on top of it is confident.
SEPARATELY, A TRAP FOUND BY WALKING INTO IT. I appended today's correction to T010's notes in techniques.json, ran the dry-run build, and the note was gone. cmd_build calls seed.run() first, and for every slug in SEEDS it copies name, hypothesis, evidence, notes, prefixes and metric from seed.py over whatever the ledger holds. A hand-edit to any of those six fields in techniques.json therefore survives until the next 05:40 build and then vanishes silently, with no error. This is very likely the real explanation for the 2026-08-12 entry that claimed a ledger fix its diff did not contain — the note probably was written and committed, and the next build erased it. status, verdict, revisit_on and the measured history are not touched, so set_status/set_verdict/set_revisit remain safe.
STILL NOT WORKING, BLUNTLY. 0 of 197 tracked queries rank, 19 straight days at 0.0% search share against a 90% goal. Revenue $0/mo, 0 paying subs, 0 Building Report sales in 19 days. And after three weeks, every page family this loop has published — /section8/, /brief/, 255 city hubs, 3 browse hubs, 9 guides — has still never earned a single impression, across 224 pages of serving history. Blocked and not worked around: AdSense still has no slot id, and api@findacrib.com may not forward.

**Changed.** One substantial change: growth/indexstatus.py, which measures index coverage directly instead of inferring it.
It asks the Search Console URL Inspection API for Google's own coverage state per URL — 'Submitted and indexed', 'Crawled - currently not indexed', 'Discovered - currently not indexed', a Google-chosen canonical that is not ours, and so on. The quota is 2,000 URLs/day against ~47,600 pages, so it samples, and the sampling is the design: (1) a STABLE cohort of ~430 URLs persisted in growth/index_status.json, so a move in the indexed rate is Google changing its mind and not a different draw — members leave only when they leave the sitemaps, and top-ups are picked in sha1(url) order so they never depend on build order; (2) STRATIFIED by page family, because /building/ is 99% of the corpus and 0% of the interesting question, with any unlisted family still getting DEFAULT_QUOTA slots so a future technique's new prefix is sampled without anyone remembering to edit the dict; (3) 100 inspections a night (GROWTH_INDEX_BUDGET), oldest reading first, so the whole cohort refreshes in about four days. Records index_pct and index_pct_building.
Tested without credentials by faking the API: cohort construction is deterministic; adding 500 new pages evicts nobody; removing a family drops exactly its members and the next run tops the cohort back up; a new family is picked up automatically; the oldest-first rotation reads 40 fresh URLs on the second run. Failure paths: a missing sitemap, missing credentials and an auth failure each return ok=False with a named reason; a 403 aborts after ONE call rather than burning 100 on a permission error, and its detail names the actual cause (the URL Inspection API needs the service account to be an owner or full user of the property — a restricted user gets 403 on every call). ok is set explicitly on every path, per the 2026-07-28 lesson. And the job records NO metrics when the cohort holds no readings: a denied API leaving index_read=0 in the series would render a measurement failure as 'nothing is indexed', which is the same class of mistake in the other direction. An absent day is honest; a zero is a claim.
Report: the 'Indexing' progress bar is renamed 'Search footprint' with an accurate sub-line, and a real 'Indexed (sampled)' bar plus a per-family table sits below it, with a note when sampled pages are indexed under someone else's canonical. Both parts rendered from the one block list; text and HTML agree, no hand-written markup, no second text builder.
Also corrected: searchconsole.py's docstring, which asserted the wrong thing; T010's note, moved to growth/seed.py where it will actually survive; and the report's 'SEO corpus frozen' callout, which still said 'nothing in the repo can restart it' — false since yesterday's watchdog, and it now says that seeing this callout means the watchdog itself failed and points at the heartbeat rc. README documents indexstatus.py, the footprint-vs-indexed distinction, and the seed.run() overwrite trap.
Added no keywords: 0 of 197 rank and today's whole point is that I do not yet know why. Retired nothing. No revisit was due. Did NOT run keywords.check_coverage(). Verified: py_compile clean on growth/*.py, growth_daily.py and build_seo.py; build --dry-run --docroot . --build-dir . exits 0 and its output is byte-identical to the pre-change baseline (checked by stashing the diff and re-running).

**Watching.** (1) THE ONE THAT DECIDES THE NEXT MONTH: the first index_pct reading, roughly 100 URLs tomorrow and the full ~430 cohort by 2026-08-19. If /building/ comes back mostly 'Submitted and indexed', the corpus is fine and the constraint is search demand — stop publishing address pages, and the honest strategic question becomes whether 47k pages of near-zero-volume queries can ever reach the goals, which is a question about T027 consolidation and about the query families with real volume, not about more content. If it comes back mostly 'Crawled - currently not indexed' or 'Discovered - currently not indexed', index acceptance is the constraint and consolidation is urgent. Do not let this reading sit unread: it is the most decision-relevant number this engine has ever produced.
(2) The per-family rates are the second prize and may be more actionable than the total. /guide/ and the city hubs have never earned an impression across 224 pages of serving history, and 'not indexed' and 'indexed but outranked' are opposite problems. Ten guides and sixty hub URLs in the cohort will separate them within days.
(3) index_wrong_canonical. On a 47k-page templated corpus, Google folding pages onto a canonical of its own choosing is the quiet failure that looks like nothing at all. If this is more than a handful, near-duplicate building pages are being merged and no amount of description or link work will change it.
(4) The watchdog, which is now load-bearing: cron_heartbeat.jsonl should show seo_watchdog_start ABSENT tomorrow, because a rebuild ran today and the corpus is no longer stale. If it fires again on 08-16, the 08-15 run did not actually write the docroot despite rc=0 and the watchdog is looping. Confirm with last_run.json build.seo_corpus, which should read 2026-08-15 or 08-16, and build.seo_pipeline, which should carry a real status record for the first time. Expect a large changed_urls count and an IndexNow burst as seven nights of stranded work lands at once; that is the backlog, not a bug.
(5) The description CTR test, unchanged and still the honest baseline to beat: 2 clicks on 115 page-dimension impressions from the building corpus, 49 of 63 serving pages in the top 10, median position ~5. Compare at least three weeks after today, because today is when the descriptions actually reached the docroot. Subtract 'jayshomefinder' from any click count before calling it progress — it was 3 of today's 5.
(6) gsc_serving_ever's slope: +11, +14, +5. If the +5 becomes a trend while index_pct is high, discovery has saturated and the ceiling is demand.


## 2026-08-16 — 3.1% of the corpus is indexed — the reading that was going to decide the next month, and it says the constraint is acceptance, not demand
*6am review*

**Observed.** FIRST index_pct READING: 3.1%. indexstatus.py ran at 05:11 and inspected 98 of a 388-URL cohort; 3 came back indexed. index_pct_building also 3.1. published_urls 47,667. 2 inspections failed (TimeoutError, first on /building/brooklyn/450-95th-st-3061230038/). index_wrong_canonical 0 — nothing is being folded onto a Google-chosen canonical, which kills watch item (3) from yesterday outright. Treat the point estimate loosely: 3/98 carries a 95% interval of roughly 0.6-8.7%. What is not loose is the order of magnitude — single digits, against a corpus of 47,667 published URLs.
GSC window 2026-08-07..08-14: gsc_serving_pages 69 (63 -> 69, first rise in six days; the run 89,84,82,80,80,88,86,75,65,66,68,63,69 is a rotation, not a trend). serving_ever 235, slope +11 after +11,+14,+5 — not saturating yet. stable 58, entered 11, left 5. Page dimension 118 impressions / 2 clicks; query dimension 3 impressions / 0 clicks; avg position 30.3. search_share_pct 0.0 for the TWENTIETH consecutive day, 0 of 222 tracked queries in the top 10 and 0 in the top 3. Visitors 08-15: 15 (14-day median ~19), organic 1, AI 0. paying_subs 0, mrr_usd 0.0 — the $9 Building Report has now been live 20 days with zero sales.
CRONS ALL GREEN AND YESTERDAY'S PREDICTION HELD EXACTLY. daily 05:00:01Z -> 05:19:01Z rc=0; build 05:40:01Z -> 05:40:18Z rc=0. seo_watchdog_start is ABSENT from the 08-16 heartbeat, which is what watch item (4) said should happen if the 08-15 rebuild really wrote the docroot: it did. build.seo_corpus now reads 2026-08-15 (1 day old, was 7) and build.seo_pipeline carries a real status record for the first time — ran clean, 47,637 pages built, 47,208 changed. The watchdog fired once, fixed the thing, and correctly stayed quiet.
THE SCOUT IS RUNNING AGAIN. 25 new queries in keywords.json tagged source 'scout:2026-08-16', so the Anthropic usage cap described in the brief (self-imposed console spend limit, due to lift 2026-08-01) has lifted. This is not an empty account and not a bug, and I could only establish it by reading source tags — see below. Keyword total went 197 -> 222, so coverage reads 73.0% (162/222) against the 82.2% measure recorded at 05:11 against the old denominator. That apparent 9-point drop is the scout adding queries, not coverage regressing.
VERIFIED YESTERDAY'S DIFF: commit f6b71dd contains every file it claimed — indexstatus.py, the searchconsole.py docstring correction, the T010 note moved into seed.py (confirmed present in seed.SEEDS['gsc_integration'], ending '2026-08-15: gsc_serving_pages is not an INDEXING numb...'), report.py, README. One claim did NOT hold, and it is the one that mattered today: indexstatus.py's docstring says 'index_status.json is tracked in git and the cloud review agent can read it'. It is not, and was not. growth_run.sh commits an explicit allowlist and nobody added the file to it, so the per-URL coverage states — the actual diagnosis — never left the droplet. I could see 3.1% today and not one thing about why.

**Concluded.** I WAS WRONG YESTERDAY AND THE CORRECTION IS THE POINT OF TODAY. The 08-15 entry said 'I THINK THE DEMAND SIDE IS THE MORE LIKELY STORY' and gave three reasons: serving pages rank well when they serve, the serving set churns around a stable core, and serving_ever climbs while the window falls. Every one of those observations is still true and the inference built on them is wrong. At ~3% indexed the binding constraint is index ACCEPTANCE, not search demand. The reasons were consistent with the demand story but they were all measured on the pages that serve — a sample conditioned on being indexed, which cannot say anything about the 97% that are not. That is the third time in four days that a correct measurement has been read as answering a question it does not answer, which is now clearly this loop's characteristic failure and not three unlucky coincidences.
WHAT THIS MEANS CONCRETELY: publishing more address pages cannot help and has not been able to help for some time. Google is accepting roughly one page in thirty. Every technique in this ledger whose theory of victory is 'more URLs' — and that is most of the content ones — has been pushing against a closed door. T027 (index triage: promote ~3k buildings, consolidate the rest) is now the leading move rather than one candidate among fifty, and the external picture agrees: the 2026 write-ups on 'crawled - currently not indexed' at scale converge on the same prescription, which is to publish less, consolidate near-duplicates into fewer substantial pages, and let the crawled-to-indexed ratio recover — Google will not spend crawl on page 10,000 if the first 100 it sampled were thin.
BUT I AM DELIBERATELY NOT RESTRUCTURING 47,000 PAGES ON ONE DAY'S 98-URL SAMPLE WITH THE DIAGNOSIS MISSING, and the reason is specific rather than cautious. The un-indexed 97% is in one of two states and they demand opposite work. 'Discovered - currently not indexed' means Google knows the URL and has declined to spend a crawl on it: a budget and priority problem, where the fix is to submit fewer and better-linked URLs and the pages themselves may be fine. 'Crawled - currently not indexed' means Google fetched the page and refused it: a quality and duplication judgement, where the fix is the pages, and shrinking the sitemap alone would change nothing. Both rule out publishing more addresses; everything after that diverges. That split existed on the droplet this morning and was unreadable here. Fixing that is worth one day and is what I shipped.
STILL NOT WORKING, BLUNTLY, AND SOME OF IT IS NOW EXPLAINED. 0 of 222 tracked queries rank; 20 straight days at 0.0% search share against a 90% goal. Revenue $0/mo, 0 subscribers, 0 Building Report sales in 20 days — the one-time-purchase hypothesis has now had three weeks to show a single sale and has not. /section8/, /brief/, the 255 city hubs, the 3 browse hubs and the 9 guides have still never earned one impression across 235 pages of serving history, and at a 3% acceptance rate the likeliest reason is simply that they are not in the index — which the per-family sample will settle within days. Blocked and not worked around: AdSense still has no slot id, so no ads render at all, and api@findacrib.com may still not forward.

**Changed.** One change with one purpose: make the index reading diagnostic and durable, so tomorrow can act on it instead of re-deriving it.
(1) growth_run.sh now commits growth/index_status.json. This is the whole reason today could not answer 'why', and indexstatus.py's own docstring already asserted it was true. The allowlist at the end of growth_run.sh is what 'tracked' means in this repo; a file the droplet writes and never adds is invisible to the cloud review no matter what a docstring says.
(2) growth/indexstatus.py records the site-wide coverage-state split as a daily series: index_state_<bucket> for each of the ten buckets in the new BUCKETS vocabulary, written dense (zeros included) whenever the cohort holds readings. summarise() now aggregates buckets at the total level, not only per family. This is NOT redundant with (1): index_status.json holds only each URL's LATEST state, so it can never show pages moving from discovered to crawled to indexed after a fix — and 'did the thing we changed move pages between states' is the only question a consolidation effort can be judged on. results.jsonl is the sole append-only series here, so the split belongs in it. The last_run record also carries a 'states' summary of the non-zero buckets, so the diagnosis is legible from "growth_daily.py status" alone.
(3) growth/report.py shows the split as a chips row under the 'Indexed (sampled)' bar, driven by the ledger series rather than the cohort file so it renders anywhere the metrics do, and suppressed when there is only one bucket to show. Rendered both parts and read them: text gives '· crawled, not indexed — 61' where HTML gives the same string in a pill, no drift, no hand-written markup, no second text builder.
(4) growth/scout.py and growth/outreach.py now mirror their outcome into last_run.json. scout_last and outreach_last live in state.json, which is gitignored for good reason (per-URL hashes, visitor ids), so no review has ever been able to read whether these jobs ran — today I could only establish the scout was alive by finding 'scout:2026-08-16' source tags in keywords.json, which is detective work for a fact that should be one line. The mirror carries COUNTS AND ERRORS ONLY and never names: outreach's success record holds a list of real third-party organisations and this repo is public, so _mirror writes drafted=len(added). ok is explicit on every path, per the 2026-07-28 lesson.
(5) README documents the allowlist as what 'tracked' means, and documents the state split with the discovered-vs-crawled decision rule.
Added no keywords: the scout added 25 this morning and 0 of 222 rank, so the tracked universe is not the constraint and diluting it further would only move the coverage denominator. Retired nothing — at 3% acceptance the honest reading is that the content techniques have not been tested yet, not that they failed. No revisit was due; earliest is 2026-08-25 (T001-T008). Did NOT run keywords.check_coverage(). Verified: py_compile clean on growth/*.py, growth_daily.py and build_seo.py; bash -n on growth_run.sh; build --dry-run --docroot . --build-dir . exits 0 with output byte-identical to the pre-change baseline (checked by stashing and re-running); bucket() unit-tested against 14 real coverageState strings; the collect() path exercised end to end against a faked Inspection API, asserting the buckets sum to read, the series is dense over BUCKETS, and the report renders the chips from the ledger alone; both _mirror paths tested, including the assertion that prospect names never reach last_run.json.

**Watching.** (1) THE DECISION, AND IT IS NOW A RULE RATHER THAN A JUDGEMENT CALL. Tomorrow index_state_* lands in results.jsonl and index_status.json lands in git. If discovered_not_indexed dominates the un-indexed pages, the constraint is crawl budget: act by shrinking what is submitted — sitemap and internal-link priority onto the few thousand buildings with real query volume — and do not rewrite page content. If crawled_not_indexed dominates, Google has seen these pages and refused them: the pages themselves are the problem, and T027's consolidation half is the move, not its promotion half. If unknown or other dominates, distrust the instrument before the corpus and check bucket() against the actual strings in the cohort file. Do not spend another run admiring 3.1%.
(2) The per-family rates, which arrive with the same commit and may matter more. /guide/, /section8/, /brief/ and the 255 city hubs have never earned an impression across 235 pages of serving history; 'never indexed' and 'indexed and outranked' are opposite problems, and 60-odd hub and guide URLs are in the cohort. If the hubs are indexed and simply do not rank, three weeks of hub-building was a demand miss, not an indexing one, and the honest response is to stop building hubs.
(3) index_pct itself, once the full ~388-URL cohort has been read — roughly 2026-08-19 at 100 inspections a night. 3/98 is compatible with anything from 0.6% to 8.7%; the full cohort narrows that enough to trend against. Watch it as a rate, not as a count, since the cohort is still topping up (388 today against a 455 target).
(4) index_wrong_canonical stays at 0. It was 0 today across 98 URLs, which retires the canonical-folding worry unless it moves.
(5) last_run.json should carry 'scout' and 'outreach' records tomorrow. If either is absent after a green daily cron, the mirror is not wired into the path that actually runs and I should find out why rather than assume the job was skipped. If scout shows ok=false with a usage-cap message again, that is a billing setting, not a bug — read the detail and move on.
(6) The description CTR test, unchanged and still the honest baseline: 2 clicks on 118 page-dimension impressions from the building corpus. Compare no earlier than 2026-09-05, three weeks after the descriptions actually reached the docroot on 08-15. Subtract 'jayshomefinder' — a decommissioned brand that previously held this domain — before calling any click count progress.
(7) A standing check, because this is now three corrections in four days: before steering on any number, ask what would have to be true for it to mean the opposite. Every one of these mistakes was a correct measurement answering a question nobody had checked it could answer.


## 2026-08-17 — 80% of the sampled corpus is UNKNOWN to Google, never fetched — and 252 finished pages were in no sitemap at all
*6am review*

**Observed.** THE DIAGNOSIS ARRIVED AND IT IS A THIRD ANSWER NEITHER BRANCH OF YESTERDAY'S DECISION RULE ANTICIPATED. index_status.json is tracked now (yesterday's fix worked) and I could finally read the per-URL states. Of 198 URLs read across 08-16 and 08-17: 159 'URL is unknown to Google', 32 'Crawled - currently not indexed', 7 'Submitted and indexed'. index_state_discovered_not_indexed is 0. So the split yesterday said would decide the month — discovered-vs-crawled — is not the split at all: 80% of what has been sampled is not un-indexed, it is UNDISCOVERED. Google has no record of those URLs.
I CHECKED THE DISCRIMINATOR BEFORE BUILDING ON IT, because 'unknown' can also be Google relabelling a long-known URL. All 159 unknown readings have crawled=None and pageFetchState PAGE_FETCH_STATE_UNSPECIFIED; all 39 known readings (7 indexed + 32 crawled) have a real lastCrawlTime and fetch SUCCESSFUL. A relabelled URL keeps its crawl time. These were never fetched. Clean split, no overlap.
AND THE INSTRUMENT WAS FILING ALL 159 AS 'other' — bucket() had no needle for that wording, so the single most decision-relevant state this sampler has ever measured showed up under the bucket whose own comment says it means 'wording this module has not learned yet'. Yesterday's watch item (1) named this exact branch ('if unknown or other dominates, distrust the instrument before the corpus and check bucket() against the actual strings'). It was right to.
SECOND INSTRUMENT DEFECT, and it cost four nights of the stratified sample. 196 of the 198 URLs read are /building/, plus 1 /neighborhood/ and 1 /zip/. Zero from /guide/, /section8/, /brief/, /dc/, /la/, /sf/. Cause: _due() sorted globally by (checked, sha1) while reconcile() tops each family up with its LOWEST-hashing candidates, so /building/'s 250 URLs are the 250 lowest hashes out of 47,165 and sort ahead of nearly everything, whereas /guide/ holds all 7 of its candidates spread across the whole range. Reproduced it exactly in simulation: night 1 = 100 building, night 2 = 98 building + 1 neighborhood + 1 zip, which is what actually happened.
THE FINDING THAT FOLLOWS FROM READING THE COHORT FILE FAMILY BY FAMILY: the sitemaps hold 2 URLs each for dc, la and sf (/dc/ and /dc/buildings/), and 7 for /guide/. The cohort quota for each city is 20, so the sampler wanted more and there were none to take. Meanwhile last_run says 'hub pages live: dc 103, la 112, sf 37'. Those 252 pages are in the docroot and in NO sitemap. Traced it: t_sitemap_daily built its shard purely from the lastmod state, and Ctx.unstage() deletes a URL's lastmod entry when it hands a page back to the SEO pipeline — 'the URL is no longer ours to list', which is only true if the pipeline then lists it. It does not. sitemap-daily.xml carried 26 URLs incl. exactly 3 city hub pages (the browse hubs).
CRONS GREEN, WATCH ITEM (5) CONFIRMED. daily 05:00:01Z->05:17:55Z rc=0, build 05:40:01Z->05:40:25Z rc=0. last_run now carries scout (ok=true, 3 techniques, 18 keywords) and outreach (ok=true, drafted 0) — the mirror shipped yesterday works, and this is the first run that could read those jobs' health in one line instead of inferring it from source tags. No Anthropic usage cap, no billing problem. seo_watchdog did NOT fire, and the corpus is 2 days old (written 08-15) — the watchdog's threshold is >2d so it is one night from firing; not yet a fault. Techniques 54 now (scout added T052-T054).
NUMBERS, BLUNTLY. GSC window 08-08..08-15: gsc_serving_pages 67 (69 -> 67; the run 88,86,75,65,66,68,63,69,67 is still rotation), serving_ever 245 (+10 after +11,+11,+14,+5). stable 57, entered 10, left 12. Page dimension 93 impressions / 1 click, avg position 82.0. search_share_pct 0.0 for the TWENTY-FIRST consecutive day, 0 of 240 tracked queries in the top 10 or top 3. Visitors 08-16: 10 — the lowest in the recorded series, against a 17/day median; organic 0, AI 0. paying_subs 0, mrr_usd 0.0; the $9 Building Report has now been live 21 days with zero sales, which is past its grace period. index_pct 3.5% of 198 read (was 3.1% of 98) — but see below, that rate is a /building/-only rate.
VERIFIED YESTERDAY'S DIFF: commit 05cb02c. growth_run.sh commits growth/index_status.json (the file is tracked — that is how today happened); indexstatus.py records index_state_<bucket> dense over BUCKETS (all ten present in today's 08-17 row); summarise() aggregates at the total level; report.py renders the chips row; scout.py and outreach.py mirror into last_run (both records present this morning). Every claim holds. No revisit_on was due — earliest is 2026-08-25 (T001-T008), so step 3 was a no-op for the seventh consecutive run.

**Concluded.** THE CONSTRAINT IS DISCOVERY, WHICH IS UPSTREAM OF BOTH THINGS YESTERDAY WAS CHOOSING BETWEEN. Yesterday's rule was: discovered_not_indexed dominant => crawl budget, shrink what is submitted; crawled_not_indexed dominant => page quality, consolidate. discovered_not_indexed is 0. The dominant state is 'Google has never fetched this URL', and neither prescription addresses it — you cannot fix a page's quality judgement that was never made, and you cannot reallocate crawl budget toward URLs Google does not know exist. This is the fourth time in five days that a correct measurement has been read against a question it could not answer, except this time the mis-set question was mine from yesterday and the measurement corrected it inside 24 hours, which is the loop working rather than failing.
BUT I AM DELIBERATELY NOT GENERALISING '80% UNDISCOVERED' TO THE CORPUS YET, AND THE REASON IS THE ROTATION BUG. 196 of 198 readings are /building/. So '3.5% indexed' and '80% unknown' are statements about single-address pages and nothing else — the strata that would tell me whether the hub and guide tiers are indexed-and-outranked or simply absent have literally never been read. I have been quoting index_pct as a site rate for two days and it was never one. Tomorrow it will be: with _due() fixed, tomorrow's 100 inspections are 54 building, 13 neighborhood, 13 zip, 4 available, 3 borough, 3 brief, 2 guide, and 1 each of dc, la, sf, section8, root, buildings, developers, marketing-agents — all first-ever readings, none of them re-reads.
THE 252-PAGE SITEMAP HOLE IS THE FIRST MECHANICAL EXPLANATION THIS LOOP HAS FOUND FOR A 'NEVER SERVED' TIER, and it is a much better explanation than the ones three weeks of entries have offered. The 08-15 audit established that internal linking is not a constraint — 47,854 pages, median 14 inbound links, four orphans. What it did not check was whether those pages are in a sitemap, and 252 of them are not, on either channel. /guide/ is the same story at smaller scale: 7 of the 9 live guides are listed, 2 are not. That is not the whole of the 80% (the /building/ corpus IS sharded into sitemaps and is still unknown to Google, so discovery is failing for it on other grounds — most likely simple crawl rationing on a 47k-page site with almost no external links), but it is the part that is a bug rather than a judgement, and it is fixable today.
WHAT THIS MEANS FOR STRATEGY, HELD LOOSELY UNTIL TOMORROW'S FAMILY READINGS. If the hub and guide tiers come back unknown-to-Google too, then every 'never earned an impression' verdict this loop has recorded against /section8/, /brief/, the city hubs and the guides — including the two retirements of T001 and T002 on the grounds that they were 'invisible in search, not merely un-clicked' — was measuring a distribution failure and calling it a demand failure. Those two retirements may need revisiting on that basis; I am not reversing them today on an inference, which is exactly the mistake I made on 08-15.
STILL NOT WORKING, BLUNTLY. 0 of 240 tracked queries rank; 21 straight days at 0.0% search share against a 90% goal. Visitors hit an all-time low of 10. Revenue $0/mo, 0 subscribers, 0 Building Report sales in 21 days — that product has now had three weeks and the one-time-purchase hypothesis has produced nothing; it is due for revisit on 2026-08-27 and I expect to change or replace it then. Blocked and not worked around: AdSense still has no slot id, so no ads render at all, and api@findacrib.com may still not forward.

**Changed.** Three changes, one story: the diagnosis was being mislabelled, the sample was not actually stratified, and 252 pages had no discovery path.
(1) growth/indexstatus.py — bucket() learns 'unknown to google' as its own bucket, unknown_to_google, placed in BUCKETS in reverse-funnel order after discovered_not_indexed. 159 of 198 readings were being filed as 'other'. Kept the pre-existing 'unknown' bucket unrenamed (it means the API returned no coverageState at all, an instrument condition) and documented the difference at both ends: the code comment and report.py's _STATE_LABEL, which renders them 'unknown to Google' and 'no state returned'. The comment also records the lastCrawlTime/pageFetchState discriminator and the observed 159/39 split, so the next reader can re-check the 'never fetched' reading instead of inheriting it.
(2) growth/indexstatus.py — _due() replaced. Oldest reading first WITHIN each family, families interleaved by each member's fractional position in its own family, and each family's oldest member pinned to the front of the night. The pin is not decoration: at 100 of 388 the proportional cut falls near 0.26, so /dc/, /la/ and /sf/ (2 URLs each) and /guide/ (7) would sit at 0.5 and 0.14 and starve permanently — I built it without the pin first and the simulation showed 13 URLs never read at all. Verified by simulating 8 nights from a cold cohort: every family sampled on night 1, whole cohort read exactly once in 4 nights, nothing starves, ordering deterministic across runs, and the buggy behaviour reproduced first so I knew I was fixing the real thing.
(3) growth/techniques.py — t_sitemap_daily now lists the union of (a) URLs this build wrote, with the honest content-hash lastmod it already tracks, and (b) every page in a family this build owns (/section8/, /brief/, /guide/, the city hub tier) that is live in the docroot but absent from every pipeline-owned shard. New helper _sitemap_covered() reads the docroot sitemap index and its shards, excluding our own; it returns None rather than an empty set when the index is unreadable, and the caller then changes nothing — 'the pipeline lists nothing' and 'I cannot tell' must not be the same value, and a dry run against a bare checkout takes the second path. The rescued set is enumerated from the filesystem, never asserted: a page is listed only if it exists, dated by its own file mtime rather than today (rsync -a preserves it, so the shard does not redate 252 pages every night — a shard that redates everything nightly is the signal crawlers learn to discount), clamped so a bad mtime cannot claim the future, and it drops out of our shard by itself the day the pipeline starts listing it. No page is published to have a URL; every one of these is already live. The detail line reports the rescued count, which is the size of a hole nothing else in the engine reports.
Also: report.py's per-family index table printed raw bucket keys ('crawled_not_indexed (1)') in the one column a reader uses to decide what to do, next to a chips row spelling the same states in prose; it now uses the same labels. README documents the unknown-to-Google state and its discriminator, the rotation bug and why a global sort was not equivalent, and the publish-vs-sitemap handover gap. The T004 note went into growth/seed.py, not techniques.json, per the 2026-08-15 seed.run() overwrite trap.
Added no keywords: the scout added 18 this morning (240 tracked) and 0 of them rank; the tracked universe is not the constraint when 80% of the corpus has never been fetched. Retired nothing — and specifically did not un-retire T001/T002 on today's inference, see Concluded. No revisit was due. Did NOT run keywords.check_coverage().
Verified: py_compile clean on growth/*.py, growth_daily.py, build_seo.py; build --dry-run --docroot . --build-dir . exits 0 with output byte-identical to the pre-change baseline (stashed the diff and re-ran); t_sitemap_daily exercised end-to-end against a synthetic docroot that reproduces the droplet's exact state (hub pages live, no city shards, 2 guides listed of 3) — correct rescue set, correct changefreq/priority per family, already-listed guides excluded, future mtime clamped, byte-stable on a second run, sitemap index spliced, and the bare-checkout path returns ok with rescued 0; _sitemap_covered() ignores a path-traversal <loc> ('../../etc/passwd.xml') and its own shard; bucket() checked against 10 real and synthetic coverageState strings; report.build() renders and both parts agree.

**Watching.** (1) THE READING THAT ACTUALLY DECIDES THE STRATEGY, now for real: tomorrow's first-ever index states for /guide/ (2), /dc/, /la/, /sf/, /section8/ (1 each), /neighborhood/ (13) and /zip/ (13). Three outcomes, three different months. Mostly unknown_to_google => these tiers were never distributed, every 'never earned an impression' verdict against them measured distribution and not demand, and T001/T002's retirements need reopening. Mostly crawled_not_indexed => Google saw them and refused them, and the hub tier is a quality problem. Mostly indexed => they are indexed and outranked, which is the one branch where more hub-building is provably pointless and the effort belongs on query families with volume. Do not accept a site-wide index_pct as an answer to this; read the per-family table.
(2) The rescued count in sitemap_daily's detail line, tomorrow. Expect roughly 252 hub pages + 2 unlisted guides on the first run. If it reads 0 with covered non-None, the enumeration is not finding pages the docroot holds and the glob list is wrong. If it says 'could not read the docroot sitemap index', the growth build is running against a docroot without sitemap.xml and that is a bigger problem than this fix.
(3) The consequence, and it is slow: a sitemap entry is a discovery signal, not an indexing guarantee. Give it two weeks before judging. The test is whether those 252 URLs move from unknown_to_google to anything else at all — discovered or crawled both count as progress here. index_state_unknown_to_google is the series to plot; it is 159 of 198 today. Do not expect impressions inside a fortnight.
(4) index_pct is now comparable to itself for the first time. Today's 3.5% is a /building/-only number; tomorrow's will be a genuine stratified rate and may look like a step change in either direction. That is the instrument being fixed, not Google changing its mind — do not report it as a trend. The building-only rate is index_pct_building (3.6% today) and that one IS comparable across the change.
(5) index_state_other should collapse to near 0 tomorrow. If it stays high, there is a SECOND unlearned wording in the cohort and bucket() needs another needle — read the raw 'state' strings in growth/index_status.json, do not guess.
(6) The SEO corpus was 2 days old this morning and the watchdog threshold is >2d, so seo_watchdog_start should appear in tomorrow's cron_heartbeat.jsonl. If it does not and the corpus reads 3 days old, the watchdog has stopped firing — check whether a growth-written sitemap in the docroot is being read as freshness, which the README flags as the exact failure mode.
(7) Unchanged and still the honest baselines: the description CTR test, 2 clicks on 118 page impressions, compare no earlier than 2026-09-05; and subtract 'jayshomefinder' from any click count before calling it progress. Today's 1 click on 93 impressions is not a signal either way.
(8) T017's revisit on 2026-08-27. The $9 Building Report will be 31 days old with 0 sales. Come with a decision, not another 'too early'.


## 2026-08-18 — Every tier reads unknown-to-Google: the answer to yesterday's question, and the two rates that were hiding inside one
*6am review*

**Observed.** THE READING YESTERDAY WAS WAITING FOR CAME IN, AND IT IS BRANCH 1. _due()'s rotation fix worked exactly as designed: today's 100 inspections covered 16 page families against 2 on 08-16/17, and every stratum has a first-ever reading. Corrected split over 298 readings (see Changed for why the recorded row differs): unknown to Google 233 (78.2%), crawled-not-indexed 54 (18.1%), indexed 11 (3.7%). Per family, fetched%/kept%: /building/ 20.4/17.6 (250 read), /neighborhood/ 35.7/0.0 (14), /zip/ 28.6/25.0 (14), /borough/ 33.3/0.0 (3), and then zero fetches anywhere else — /guide/ 0% of 2, /brief/ 0% of 3, /available/ 0% of 4, /section8/, /dc/, /la/, /marketing-agents/ 0% of 1 each. Fetched at all: /sf/ (crawled 07-28), /developers/ and /buildings/ (both 07-31), / (08-16, the only indexed page on the site outside 9 building pages and 1 zip hub). Googlebot IS alive — building pages carry crawl dates of 08-16, 08-17 and 08-18 — but the whole site is being fetched at roughly 1-5 URLs a night and 51 of the 65 ever-fetched URLs in the sample are /building/. VERIFIED YESTERDAY'S DIFF, commit 56628df: bucket() has the unknown_to_google needle; _due() interleaves by family with the oldest-member pin, and the 16-family spread today is the proof; t_sitemap_daily reported 'sitemap-daily.xml with 281 URLs (incl. 3 fallback-published guides, 255 city hub pages) — 255 of them live in the docroot but listed in no sitemap the SEO pipeline owns', against a predicted ~252+2, so the rescue fired on its first night; report.py's family table used the prose labels. Every claim holds. Watch item (6) also resolved: seo_watchdog_start/finish are both in cron_heartbeat.jsonl for 08-18, rc=0. Site numbers, and they are not good: visitors 14 yesterday against a 16/day median and falling, organic 3, AI 0. gsc_serving_pages 60, down from 89 on 07-27, with 7 pages dropping out overnight and 0 entering; 245 pages have ever served. Search share 0.0% — 0 of 265 tracked queries in the top 3, 22 straight days. paying_subs 0, mrr_usd 0.0; the $9 Building Report is 22 days old with zero sales. No revisit_on was due — earliest is 2026-08-25 — so step 3 was a no-op for the eighth consecutive run.

**Concluded.** THERE ARE TWO CONSTRAINTS HERE, NOT ONE, AND EVERY REVIEW SINCE 07-27 HAS BEEN ARGUING ABOUT THEIR PRODUCT. index_pct = 3.7% is fetched_pct x accept_pct = 21.8% x 16.9%. Google has never come for 78% of the sampled corpus, AND of the 22% it did fetch it threw away 83%. Those are independent failures with opposite fixes — links and sitemaps move the first and cannot touch the second; consolidating pages moves the second and cannot touch the first — and the combined rate is the one number that cannot tell you which one your change was supposed to move. That is why 08-15 read a correct measurement against a crawl-budget question, 08-16 against a page-quality question, and 08-17 against a discovery question: the instrument genuinely could not distinguish them. Now it can. YESTERDAY'S CONDITIONAL FIRES. The exact words were: mostly unknown_to_google means 'every never-earned-an-impression verdict this loop has recorded against /section8/, /brief/, the city hubs and the guides — including the two retirements of T001 and T002 — was measuring a distribution failure and calling it a demand failure.' Every one of those families read 0% fetched. So T001 and T002 were retired on the stated grounds that they were 'invisible in search, not merely un-clicked', and that reasoning was wrong: they were invisible because Google never fetched them, which says nothing at all about whether anyone wants them. I am still not un-retiring them today, and the reason is not caution about the inference — the inference is now measured, not inferred. It is that /section8/ and /brief/ have 1 and 3 readings behind them, the sitemap rescue that gave those tiers a crawl path at all is exactly one night old, and un-retiring means resuming nightly publication into a corpus Google already declines 83% of what it reads. The right order is: let the rescued 255 pages get fetched or not, watch fetched_pct for those families, and decide on evidence rather than on a corrected premise. THE HARDER HALF IS ACCEPTANCE AND NOBODY HAS BEEN LOOKING AT IT. Even a perfect discovery fix lands the site at ~17% indexed, because that is what Google does with these pages when it sees them: 42 of the 51 fetched /building/ pages came back 'crawled, currently not indexed'. Current guidance is blunt about what that status means on a programmatic corpus — thin, commodity, near-duplicate — and prescribes deciding by GROUP, not by page: prune, consolidate, noindex, or genuinely improve each cluster. That is T027, still a candidate, and it is now the better-evidenced of the two halves. I did not start it today because it is a large change to the 47k-page pipeline that I cannot test against real data from here, and because with fetched_pct and accept_pct now recorded per family it can finally be judged rather than guessed at. WHAT IS STILL NOT WORKING, PLAINLY: 0 of 265 tracked queries rank, 22 days at 0.0% share against a 90% goal. Served pages are going DOWN, 89 to 60. Visitors at an all-time-low band. $0 revenue, 0 subscribers, 0 report sales in 22 days — T017 is due 2026-08-27 and should be changed or replaced, not extended again. Blocked and not worked around: AdSense still has no slot id so no ads render, and api@findacrib.com may still not forward. The Anthropic account is fine — scout and outreach both ran clean this morning (ok true, 3 techniques and 25 keywords proposed), so the usage-cap story in the standing brief is stale and should not be repeated.

**Changed.** Four changes. Two fix the instrument, one fixes the pipe the fixes travel down, one is a real navigation change. (1) growth/indexstatus.py — summarise() now RE-DERIVES every bucket from the stored raw 'state' string instead of reading rec['bucket'] back. inspect() stamps a bucket at read time, so when bucket() learned unknown_to_google on 08-17 the 159 already-read rows kept the stale 'other' label until their next inspection four nights out. This morning's recorded row therefore says unknown_to_google 74 / other 159 for a cohort whose three distinct stored state strings are only 'URL is unknown to Google' (233), 'Crawled - currently not indexed' (54) and 'Submitted and indexed' (11) — the real split is 233/54/11 and 'other' is 0. That matters beyond tidiness: 'other' is documented to mean 'a wording this module has not learned yet, add a needle', so the report was sending tomorrow's review to hunt a needle that already existed — which is precisely what my own watch item (5) told it to do. Re-deriving makes any future bucket() fix retroactive across the whole cohort the moment it lands. 'read' is now keyed off 'checked' rather than off the presence of a bucket, so a URL Google answers about with no coverageState is counted as read and buckets as 'unknown' instead of vanishing. (2) growth/indexstatus.py + growth/report.py — split index_pct into index_fetched_pct (has Google ever fetched it: lastCrawlTime non-null) and index_accept_pct (of those, how many it kept), site-wide and per family, recorded in results.jsonl and surfaced in the report as two tiles plus new Fetched/Kept columns on the per-family table. 'Fetched' is taken from the crawl time rather than inferred from the bucket because the crawl time is the direct evidence; the two agreed exactly today, all 233 unknown rows with no crawl time and all 65 others with one. Kept renders '—' and not 0% when nothing was fetched — no page has been judged, so there is no acceptance rate, and a 0 there would read as a quality verdict Google never gave. The old 'Top non-indexed state' column is gone; the two rates say the same thing numerically and per stratum. report.py also gets _index_summary(), which recomputes from the cohort instead of reading back the cached 'summary' blob, for the same reason as (1) — otherwise every counting change shows up a night late and shows up as '—' in the meantime. results.jsonl is deliberately NOT rewritten: an append-only record of what was believed on the day is the point of it, so today's row stays wrong and tomorrow's is right. (3) growth_run.sh — SEO_STALE_DAYS 2 -> 1. The 04:10 SEO cron has not fired since at least 08-14, so the watchdog is the only route from this repo to the 47,600 published pages, and at 2 it was firing every THIRD night: find -mtime -2 still matches a sitemap written 48h ago, which is why the heartbeat shows seo_watchdog_finish on 08-15 and then nothing until 08-18. Every content change the review loop pushes was waiting an average of a day and a half. At 1 the anti-race property is identical — a healthy 04:10 cron's sitemaps are 1.5h old when the 05:40 build looks, well inside -mtime -1, so the watchdog still stands down whenever the real cron works — and the only slack removed is the day that was never load-bearing. (4) build_seo.py — the shared page() header, which every one of the 47,165 pages this pipeline writes uses, offered exactly two onward links (All neighborhoods, Guides) and no route at all to the other three cities the site covers. Added a CITY_NAV line linking /sf/, /la/ and /dc/, with the anchor text the homepage already uses for each, and a .cities CSS rule so it sits as a small second line. This is first a reader fix — a Bed-Stuy address page had nowhere to send someone moving to LA. As an indexing bet it is honest but modest, and I have written that in the code comment: the root index.html has linked all three from its city nav for weeks and Google has not followed it, so this is the same signal from 47,165 pages rather than a new mechanism, and the current guidance that a page needs an internal link from a CRAWLED, INDEXED page — not just a sitemap entry — is what makes /building/ the right place to put it, since it is the only tier being fetched at all. It costs a one-time honest lastmod bump across the corpus on the next SEO run, since the nav genuinely changed. Also: README documents the two new rates, the re-derivation rule and why the series is not rewritten, and the watchdog cadence. Added no keywords — the scout added 25 this morning (265 tracked, 0 ranking) and the tracked universe is not the constraint when 78% of the corpus has never been fetched. Retired nothing; no revisit was due. Did NOT run keywords.check_coverage(). Verified: py_compile clean on growth/*.py, growth_daily.py, build_seo.py; bash -n on growth_run.sh; build --dry-run --docroot . --build-dir . exits 0 with output byte-identical to the stashed pre-change baseline; summarise() checked against the real 388-URL cohort and against edge cases (empty cohort, a read row with no state, never-read rows not counted as read); report.build() renders and the HTML and text parts agree and now show the same split as the chips row; page() rendered and all five header links asserted present.

**Watching.** (1) THE FIRST HONEST READING OF BOTH RATES, tomorrow. index_fetched_pct and index_accept_pct enter results.jsonl for the first time; today's values are 21.8% and 16.9% computed here. index_state_other should go to 0 and index_state_unknown_to_google to ~233 in tomorrow's row — if 'other' is still non-zero then there IS a second unlearned wording and bucket() needs another needle, and this time read the raw 'state' strings in growth/index_status.json before guessing, because today's 159 was a stale label and not a new wording. (2) THE 255 RESCUED HUB PAGES. They got a crawl path on 08-17 and the families they belong to read 0% fetched today. The test is whether /dc/, /la/, /sf/, /guide/, /section8/ and /brief/ move off 0% fetched — indexed is not the bar, being FETCHED AT ALL is. Give it a fortnight; at 1-5 fetches a night the crawler needs the time. If they are still 0% on ~2026-09-01 then sitemap entry plus internal link is not sufficient on this site and the constraint is site-level crawl rationing, which no linking change fixes. (3) THE HEADER CHANGE, same clock, same metric: /sf/, /la/, /dc/ fetched_pct. It deploys with the next SEO run, which should now be tonight rather than 08-21. (4) SEO_STALE_DAYS=1 — check cron_heartbeat.jsonl for seo_watchdog_start/finish tomorrow AND the day after. Two consecutive nights is the whole point of the change. Then check seo_pipeline.changed_urls in last_run.json: 08-15 reported 47,208 changed of 47,637 after a THREE-day gap, and I could not tell from one data point whether that is accumulated template edits or the honest-lastmod mechanism being defeated. A one-day gap answers it. If a nightly run also reports ~47k changed, then seo_lastmod.json is not holding and the corpus mass-bumps every night, which is a bigger problem than anything in today's diff and should be the next run's work. Note today's diff touches page(), so tomorrow's run legitimately rewrites everything once — read the number on the SECOND night, not the first. (5) ACCEPTANCE IS THE HALF NOBODY HAS WORKED ON. accept_pct 16.9%, and 42 of 51 fetched building pages declined. T027 (index triage: promote ~3k buildings, consolidate the rest) is the candidate that addresses it and is now the best-evidenced move on the board. The next review should either start it or say why not. Do not let a discovery win — should one arrive — be read as progress on this; they are separate numbers now, so read them separately. (6) T017's revisit on 2026-08-27: the $9 Building Report will be 31 days old with 0 sales. Come with a decision. (7) SERVED PAGES ARE FALLING: 89 on 07-27, 60 today, 7 out and 0 in overnight. If gsc_serving_pages keeps dropping while fetched_pct rises, Google is fetching more and keeping less, and that is the acceptance problem getting worse, not the discovery problem getting better. (8) Unchanged baselines: the description CTR test, compare no earlier than 2026-09-05; and subtract 'jayshomefinder' from any click count before calling it progress. Today: 0 clicks on 82 page impressions.


## 2026-08-19 — Acceptance was never 15.8% — Google kept 10 of 11 pages it fetched in June and 2 of 65 since, and the watchdog fix from yesterday missed nightly by two minutes
*6am review*

**Observed.** Both crons fired (last_run.json: measure 05:15:19Z, build 05:40:18Z; heartbeat rc=0 for both). GSC window 2026-08-10..08-17: 1 query impression, 0 clicks; on the page dimension 79 impressions, 0 clicks, avg position 82.0. gsc_serving_pages 58, down again from 60 — the series since 07-27 is 89, 77, 82, 82, 78, 81, 89, 89, 84, 82, 80, 65, 66, 68, 63, 69, 67, 60, 58, with 6 pages entering and 8 leaving overnight; gsc_serving_ever 249, gsc_serving_stable 52. search_share_pct 0.0 for the 23rd consecutive day, 0 of 265 tracked queries in the top 3 or the top 10. Visitors 08-18: 12, organic 3, AI 1, against a 15/day median and falling. No visitors row exists for 08-19 and that is NOT a gap — measure records visitors against the day they occurred and runs the morning after, so 08-18's row was written 08-19T05:00:05Z; do not raise this as an instrument failure. paying_subs 0, mrr_usd 0.0; the $9 Building Report is 23 days old with zero sales. INDEX SAMPLE: cohort 445, read 398, fetched 76 (19.1%), indexed 12 (3.0%), accept 15.8%. States unknown_to_google 320, crawled_not_indexed 64, discovered_not_indexed 2, indexed 12, other 0. THE FINDING: acceptance is not a property of the corpus, it is a function of WHEN Google fetched the page. By crawl age — 0-6d 2 of 9 (22.2%), 7-13d 0 of 19, 14-20d 0 of 32, 21-27d 0 of 1, 28d+ 10 of 15 (66.7%). By crawl month — June 10 of 11 (90.9%), July 0 of 12, August 2 of 53 (3.8%). Eight of the nine indexed building pages were last crawled 06-26..06-30. Per family, fetched%/kept%: /building/ 20.4/17.6 (250 read), /neighborhood/ 40.0/7.1 (35), /zip/ 11.4/25.0 (35), /borough/ 37.5/0.0 (8), /sf/ 9.1/0.0 (11), and 0% fetched everywhere else — /dc/ 0 of 12, /la/ 0 of 12, /available/ 0 of 12, /brief/ 0 of 8, /guide/ 0 of 7, /section8/ 0 of 4, /marketing-agents/ 0 of 1. VERIFIED YESTERDAY'S DIFF, commit 3377057: all four claims are in the repo — summarise() re-derives from the raw state and today's index_state_other is 0 exactly as predicted; index_fetched_pct and index_accept_pct entered results.jsonl for the first time at 19.1 and 15.8; SEO_STALE_DAYS is 1 in growth_run.sh; CITY_NAV is at build_seo.py:893, used by page() at :917, with the .cities rule at :703. No revisit_on was due — earliest is 2026-08-25 — so step 3 was a no-op for the ninth consecutive run. Anthropic API: today both scout and outreach failed with 'Your credit balance is too low to access the Anthropic API' — that is the EMPTY-ACCOUNT failure, not the 2026-08-01 usage cap, and it contradicts yesterday's entry, which said the account was fine. It was fine on 08-18; it is not now.

**Concluded.** THE 15.8% ACCEPTANCE RATE THAT THE LAST FOUR ENTRIES REASONED FROM DESCRIBES NO PAGE ON THIS SITE. It is one average over 22.2% / 0% / 0% / 0% / 66.7%, and the axis it collapses is crawl recency. Two readings fit the shape and they demand opposite work. LATENCY: Google has not finished deciding about the recent crawls, the matured slice is the honest rate, acceptance is ~62%, and consolidating a corpus Google is largely keeping (T027) would be self-inflicted harm. VERDICT: Google accepted this corpus in June — 10 of 11 — and stopped; of the 65 URLs it has fetched since 2026-07-01 it kept 2, so current acceptance is ~3% and T027 is exactly right. I cannot settle it from today's data and I am not going to pretend otherwise, but the balance of evidence leans to VERDICT, and the reason is the 14-20d band: 32 pages, zero indexed. If a two-to-three-week decision latency were the whole story that band should be partway converted, and two pages crawled inside the last six days ARE indexed, so the pipeline is plainly capable of deciding fast. The one thing that keeps latency alive is that a slow tail is a real phenomenon and 32 is not a large sample. WHAT MAKES THE BAND TABLE STILL NOT DECISIVE, and this is the part worth remembering: lastCrawlTime is the MOST RECENT crawl, not the first. A page Google fetched and declined in July and re-fetched last night reads as '0-6d, too recent to judge' when it is actually a re-affirmed rejection. So the matured rate is biased optimistic by precisely the pages Google keeps coming back to and keeps refusing — which, on a corpus being crawled at 1-5 URLs a night, could be most of them. That bias cannot be removed with Google's clock; it needs ours, which is why first_checked and first_bucket are now stamped on every cohort record and index_accept_pct_settled will answer the question cleanly from 2026-09-06. THE PRACTICAL CONSEQUENCE IS A HOLD, NOT A DECISION: do not start T027's 47k-page consolidation off index_accept_pct. It is the biggest irreversible change on the board and the number justifying it turns out to be an artefact of averaging. Three more weeks of sampling costs nothing and the pages are not going anywhere. YESTERDAY'S WATCHDOG FIX DID NOT WORK AND I CAN SAY EXACTLY WHY. Watch item (4) asked for seo_watchdog_start/finish on two consecutive nights. The heartbeat has them on 08-18 and NOT on 08-19. The watchdog ran at 05:40:18 on 08-18 and refresh_seo.sh finished at 05:41:33; the next night the freshness test ran at 05:40:18 again, 23h58m later, and 'find -mtime -1' matches anything under 24h — so the sitemaps the watchdog had written itself the previous morning read as fresh by a two-minute margin. Two crons on a fixed daily clock chasing a 24h window can only ever fire on alternate nights. Going 2 -> 1 bought one day of the three and the off-by-two-minutes ate the rest, so the corpus is on a two-day deploy cycle and yesterday's build_seo.py header change — the whole discovery bet — was still not live 24h after it was pushed. It is not deployed as I write this. The rest of watch item (4) IS answered and answered well: 08-18's run reported 115 changed URLs of 47,640 after a three-day gap, against 47,208 on 08-15. Honest lastmod is holding; the 08-15 figure was the 47,165-page meta-description template edit, not a broken seo_lastmod.json. That concern can be closed. WHAT IS STILL NOT WORKING, PLAINLY: 0 of 265 tracked queries rank, 23 days at 0.0% share against a 90% goal. Serving pages have fallen 89 -> 58 since 07-27 and fell again today. Visitors are at their lowest sustained band on record. $0 revenue, 0 subscribers, 0 report sales in 23 days. 78-80% of every sampled URL has never been fetched at all, and six page families read 0% fetched. Blocked and not worked around: AdSense still has no slot id so no ads render, and api@findacrib.com may still not forward. One angle from today's research worth a future run rather than a change now: ChatGPT retrieves through Bing's index, not Google's, and this site already pushes IndexNow on every changed URL — so if Google's acceptance really has collapsed, the AI-citation route (T042, T047) may be reachable without Google at all, and nobody here has ever measured Bing coverage separately.

**Changed.** Three changes, and the first two are the run. (1) growth/indexstatus.py + growth/report.py — acceptance is now reported against crawl age instead of as one average. summarise() gains by_crawl_age (fetched/indexed/accept_pct per 0-6d, 7-13d, 14-20d, 21-27d, 28d+ band), accept_pct_mature with fetched_mature as its denominator (MATURE_DAYS=21), and accept_pct_settled with fetched_settled. Four new metrics enter results.jsonl: index_fetched_mature, index_accept_pct_mature, index_fetched_settled, index_accept_pct_settled. The denominators are recorded alongside the rates deliberately — on a 100-URL nightly budget the matured slice is 16 pages and a rate off 16 must never be read like a rate off 398. accept_pct_settled is keyed off first_checked, this sampler's own first reading of the URL, which is now stamped on every cohort record along with first_bucket and carried forward untouched; it is the only clock a Google re-crawl cannot reset, and it reads None — and so stays out of the series entirely rather than writing a misleading zero — until 2026-09-06. last_run.json carries accept_pct_mature, fetched_mature and the non-empty age bands, because that file is what the cloud review reads first and the flat accept_pct on its own is now known to mislead. The report gains a Last fetched / Pages / Kept table under the two rate tiles plus a note naming the matured rate and its denominator; the column is 'Last fetched' and not 'Fetched' because the family table directly above uses Fetched for a percentage. I also corrected the comment on first_indexed, which claimed to be 'the number that says how long acceptance takes on this corpus' — it is the first day THIS sampler saw the URL indexed, so on 08-19 all twelve indexed URLs carried 08-16..08-19 against crawls running back to June, and read as latency it would have said the corpus indexes in three days. (2) growth_run.sh — the SEO watchdog's freshness threshold is in minutes with an explicit guard band: SEO_FRESH_MINS = SEO_STALE_DAYS * 1440 - SEO_GUARD_MINS, guard 120, so 1,320 minutes. The question it actually asks is 'was the corpus rebuilt in THIS daily cycle' and the guard band is the width of the window between the two crons. A healthy 04:10 cron's sitemaps are ~90 minutes old when the 05:40 build looks, well inside 22h, so the anti-race property is exactly as strong and the watchdog still stands down whenever the real cron works; last night's own output at 1,438 minutes now correctly reads stale. Asymmetric costs and they point the same way: too wide costs at most a duplicate rebuild, which flock already makes safe, while too narrow costs a day of every content change. (3) README — the acceptance confound, both matured rates and why first_indexed is not an acceptance date, plus the corrected watchdog cadence story. Added no keywords: 265 tracked, 0 ranking, and the tracked universe is not the constraint while 80% of the corpus has never been fetched. Retired nothing; no revisit was due. Did NOT run keywords.check_coverage(). VERIFIED: py_compile clean on growth/*.py and growth_daily.py; bash -n clean on growth_run.sh; build --dry-run --docroot . --build-dir . exits 0 with output byte-identical to the stashed pre-change baseline; summarise() run against the real 445-URL cohort reproduces the hand-computed bands exactly (76 fetched, 16 matured, 62.5%) and handles an empty cohort, a malformed date and a missing date without raising; _band() checked at every boundary; report.build() renders and the HTML and text parts agree, with table-layout:fixed intact; the find -mtime/-mmin boundary reproduced against real files at 1,438 and 90 minutes, confirming both the bug and the fix.

**Watching.** (1) DID THE WATCHDOG FIRE ON 08-20 AND 08-21? Same test as yesterday, and it failed yesterday, so do not accept one night as proof. seo_watchdog_start/finish in cron_heartbeat.jsonl on BOTH mornings. From 09-06 they are the number that decides T027. Until then, read index_accept_pct_mature WITH index_fetched_mature next to it and refuse to quote the flat index_accept_pct on its own. (3) THE 14-20d BAND IS THE LATENCY TEST AND IT RESOLVES ITSELF. Those 32 pages, 0 indexed, cross MATURE_DAYS over the next week and the cohort re-inspects every ~4 nights. If accept_pct_mature holds near 62% while fetched_mature grows past ~48, latency was real and consolidation is off. If accept_pct_mature COLLAPSES toward 10-20% as those 32 mature into it, the June cohort was a fossil, current acceptance is ~3%, and T027 becomes the run's work. Write down which happened. Being FETCHED AT ALL is the bar, not indexed. (5) THE HEADER CHANGE'S CLOCK HAS NOT STARTED — CITY_NAV was pushed 08-18 and is still not deployed, because the watchdog did not run. Date its fourteen days from whenever the heartbeat shows the next successful seo_watchdog_finish, not from 08-18. *6am review*

**Observed.** Both crons fired (heartbeat: daily 05:00:01→05:11:22 rc=0, build 05:40:01→05:40:15 rc=0). **Concluded.** YESTERDAY'S TWO HYPOTHESES ARE SETTLED, AND IT IS VERDICT, NOT LATENCY. The 08-19 entry could not choose between 'Google has not finished deciding about the recent crawls' and 'Google decided in June and has stopped accepting', and correctly said so. Today's evidence decides it and does not depend on the averaging artefact that made the question hard: the SAME TWO URLs, indexed for seven weeks, were re-fetched and rejected. There is no pending decision to be latent about — a page that has been in the index since June is not awaiting a verdict, it HAD one and Google reversed it. Zero pages moved the other way. So acceptance is not merely low on new crawls (July 0/13, August 2/58 — 2 of 71 since 07-01, 2.8%), Google is actively dismantling the June cohort as it returns to it. That also explains gsc_serving_pages falling 89 → 53 without anyone having to call it impression noise: pages are leaving the serving set because they are leaving the index. And it reframes accept_pct_mature — 62.5% → 50.0% overnight on an unchanged denominator of 16 is not a rate stabilising, it is the fossil eroding. Expect it to keep sliding toward zero as the remaining nine June URLs get re-crawled. If it instead holds near 50% while fetched_mature grows past ~48, I am wrong and latency was real. WHAT THIS DOES TO THE T027 HOLD. Yesterday I held the 47k-page consolidation on the grounds that the number justifying it was an artefact and that index_accept_pct_settled would answer cleanly on 2026-09-06. The reason for the hold is now largely gone — the eviction evidence is per-URL and immune to that confound — but I am NOT starting T027 tonight, for two reasons that are about evidence quality, not caution for its own sake: the eviction sample is two URLs, and the mechanism the sampler now records will produce a real series within days at 100 re-inspections a night. It is the biggest irreversible change on the board and it will still be available next week. What has changed is the test that lifts the hold: it is no longer 'wait for 09-06', it is 'index_lost_indexed keeps exceeding index_gained_indexed across a week of re-checks'. If that holds, T027 becomes the run's work without waiting for September. I ALSO GOT LUCKY, AND THAT IS A BUG. The finding above exists because I happened to diff index_status.json against git. That file keeps only the latest state per URL, so nothing in the engine could ever have shown a page LOSING indexing — every metric in the series is a level, and the one question that now matters is a direction. An instrument that can only be read by hand archaeology is not an instrument. SECOND, THE INSTRUMENT BUG, and it is the same shape this project has hit twice before (2026-08-01, the deploy clock measuring our own nightly write; 2026-08-08, the orphan audit reading the docroot before its own rsync). growth_run.sh runs build --deploy first — and the build stamps last_run.json's seo_corpus and seo_pipeline from the docroot as it finds it — THEN runs the watchdog, THEN commits. The watchdog only fires when those fields read stale. So the record that gets committed is always the pre-repair snapshot: this morning the heartbeat says rc=0 'ran refresh_seo.sh to completion' at 05:41:24 while the committed last_run.json says seo_pipeline 'NO RUN FOR 2.0 DAYS — cron, the host or the checkout', and growth_daily.py status prints exactly that as its headline. The file this review is instructed to trust as ground truth was structurally guaranteed to report a dead pipeline on precisely the mornings the fix worked — and tomorrow's review, told by watch item (1) to check whether the watchdog fired two nights running, would have read 'NO RUN FOR 3.0 DAYS' and concluded the guard band failed. WHAT IS STILL NOT WORKING, PLAINLY: 0 of 265 tracked queries rank, 24 days at 0.0% share against a 90% goal. Serving pages down 40% in three weeks. 80% of the sampled corpus has never been fetched at all. $0 revenue, 0 subscribers, 0 report sales in 24 days. Blocked and not worked around: AdSense still has no slot id so no ads render, api@findacrib.com may still not forward, and the Anthropic balance is empty so scout and outreach are dead. From research: Google still does not participate in IndexNow as of mid-2026 while Bing, Yandex, Naver and Seznam do, and ChatGPT Search retrieves through Bing's index — so if Google's acceptance really is being withdrawn, the AI-citation route (T042, T047) may be reachable without Google at all. Nobody here has ever measured Bing coverage separately. That is a future run's work, not tonight's.

**Changed.** Two changes, both instrumentation, both aimed at the same failure: the engine could not say what it had just seen. (1) growth/indexstatus.py — collect() now counts bucket transitions among URLs it had already read once, and records index_rechecked, index_lost_indexed and index_gained_indexed in results.jsonl plus rechecked/lost_indexed/gained_indexed in last_run.json's indexstatus record. A URL that drops out of the index gets left_indexed stamped on its cohort record, the mirror of first_indexed, so a later review can find the evicted pages in the file instead of diffing git. Three deliberate rules: both sides of the comparison go through bucket() afresh (the convention summarise() already documents — comparing a stored bucket against a fresh one would report every _STATE_BUCKETS improvement as a transition); a move into or out of 'unknown' is never counted, because that is Google returning no coverage state at all, an instrument condition and not a verdict changing; and on a night nothing was re-read the three metrics are omitted entirely rather than written as zeros, the same rule as the tot['read'] gate above them — an absent day is honest, a zero is a claim. (2) growth_run.sh + growth_daily.py + growth/ledger.py + growth/report.py — the watchdog now ends by calling a new 'growth_daily.py seo-status --docroot … --watchdog-rc … --watchdog-note …', which re-reads the docroot and merges corrected seo_corpus/seo_pipeline into the existing build record via a new ledger.patch_last_run() — a MERGE, not a write, so 'at' keeps meaning 'when the build ran' and the techniques block survives — and records what the watchdog itself did as build.seo_watchdog (ran, ok, rc, note, at). 'ran' is set on every path, per the 2026-07-28 rule that a missing field must never be readable as a failure. A re-read that comes back EMPTY leaves the build's own reading standing rather than blanking it: both readers fall silent on a missing seo_corpus, so clobbering a real 'frozen since 08-18' with null would suppress the very alarm this exists to keep honest, and keeping the older value can only under-report freshness, never call a frozen corpus fresh. The call is non-fatal — a failure there must not cost the run its commit. report.py's 'SEO corpus frozen' callout now names the watchdog's own rc and note (including the case where it exits 0 and the corpus is STILL stale, which means refresh_seo.sh is reporting success without writing the corpus) instead of sending the owner to a heartbeat file the report cannot read; status prints a 'seo watchdog:' line. README carries both. Added no keywords: 265 tracked, 0 ranking, and the tracked universe is not the constraint while Google is removing pages it already accepted. Retired nothing; no revisit was due. Did NOT run keywords.check_coverage(). Did NOT start T027 — see Concluded. VERIFIED: py_compile clean on growth/*.py and growth_daily.py; bash -n clean on growth_run.sh; build --dry-run --docroot . --build-dir . exits 0; report.build() renders with both parts agreeing and table-layout:fixed intact x8; status exits 0. The transition rule was replayed against the REAL cohort diff (08-19 → 08-20) and reproduces the hand-found result exactly — rechecked 53, lost 2, gained 0 — and collect() was run end-to-end against a stubbed inspect() in a sandboxed STATUS_PATH/RESULTS_PATH/LAST_RUN_PATH, confirming 1 lost / 1 gained / 2 rechecked out of four URLs, left_indexed stamped on the evicted one only, and no movement metrics written at all for an all-new cohort. patch_last_run() was checked for field preservation, for a command with no prior record, and against a corrupt last_run.json. _watchdog_clause() was checked on all five branches. seo-status was run against a healthy fake docroot (corrects both fields) and an unreadable one (omits both keys, keeps the watchdog record). growth/last_run.json and growth/index_status.json were both restored to the droplet's committed state after testing — the working tree contains only source and README.

**Watching.** (1) THE EVICTION SERIES IS NOW THE MAIN INSTRUMENT. From tomorrow, results.jsonl carries index_lost_indexed, index_gained_indexed and index_rechecked.
