## 2026-08-21 — Six runs of instruments and no content: shipped a computed comparison paragraph to 46,853 building pages — and the eviction signature turns out to be industry-wide, which breaks yesterday's T027 trigger
*6am review*

**Observed.** Both crons fired (heartbeat: daily 05:00:01->05:11:12 rc=0 pull=ok; build --deploy 05:40:01->05:40:20 rc=0). WATCH ITEM (3) ANSWERED YES ON ITS FIRST NIGHT: last_run.json now tells the truth about the pipeline that repairs it. build.seo_corpus reads days_old 0 written 2026-08-21, and build.seo_watchdog is present with ran=true ok=true rc=0 'ran refresh_seo.sh to completion' — yesterday the same morning would have committed 'NO RUN FOR 3.0 DAYS'. WATCH ITEM (4) ALSO YES: seo_watchdog_start 05:40:20 'corpus older than 1320min', finish 05:41:30 rc=0 — two nights running, so the guard band is the mechanism, not a coincidence. seo_pipeline ran clean, 47,640 corpus pages, 0 changed. GSC window 2026-08-12..08-19: query dimension 5 impressions / 2 clicks, page dimension 62 impressions / 2 clicks. gsc_serving_pages 47, the lowest in the record; the series since 07-27 is 89,77,82,82,78,81,89,89,84,82,80,65,66,68,63,69,67,60,58,63,47. serving_ever 263, serving_stable 47. search_share_pct 0.0 for the 25th consecutive day, 0 of 265 tracked queries in the top 10; keyword coverage 83.4% (221/265). Visitors 08-20: 19, organic 0, AI 0. paying_subs 0, mrr_usd 0.0; the $9 Building Report is 25 days old with zero sales. INDEX SAMPLE: cohort 445, read 445, inspected 100, fetched 18.4%, indexed 2.0%, accept_pct 11.0. States: unknown_to_google 341, crawled_not_indexed 73, discovered_not_indexed 22, indexed 9 (was 10). By crawl age, indexed/fetched: 0-6d 1/12 (8.3%), 7-13d 1/21 (4.8%), 14-20d 0/29 (0.0%), 21-27d 0/7 (0.0%), 28d+ 7/13 (53.8%). WATCH ITEM (1), THE PREDICTION, HELD AND WAS NOT FALSIFIED. Yesterday predicted index_accept_pct_mature would slide from 50.0 toward zero as the remaining June-crawled URLs are re-crawled, and said it would be WRONG if the rate held near 50% while fetched_mature grew past ~48. It fell 50.0 -> 35.0 while fetched_mature grew 16 -> 20: four newly-matured fetches added zero indexed pages, and one more page was lost. index_rechecked 100, index_lost_indexed 1, index_gained_indexed 0 — the second consecutive day of lost>gained (08-20 was 2 and 0). Anthropic API: scout and outreach both failed with 'Your credit balance is too low to access the Anthropic API', third consecutive day — the EMPTY-ACCOUNT failure, NOT the 2026-08-01 usage cap. No revisit_on was due (earliest 2026-08-25), so step 3 was a no-op for the eleventh consecutive run. VERIFIED YESTERDAY'S DIFF, commit 84ebfd3: all claims hold. index_rechecked/index_lost_indexed/index_gained_indexed entered results.jsonl today for the first time at 100/1/0, the same three appear as rechecked/lost_indexed/gained_indexed in last_run.json's indexstatus record, patch_last_run() restamped seo_corpus and wrote build.seo_watchdog, and growth_daily.py status prints the 'seo watchdog:' line. CORRECTION TO YESTERDAY'S FIGURE: results.jsonl holds TWO gsc rows for 2026-08-20 — 05:00:06 (serving_pages 53, serving_ever 253) and 13:30:21 (63, 263), a mid-day manual re-run. ledger.read_results() dedupes later-line-wins, so the recorded value for 08-20 is 63, not the 53 that entry quoted. The same eight-day window read 8.5 hours later returned 19% MORE serving pages, which means the 05:00 reading is taken while Search Console is still filling the window in.

**Concluded.** THE EVICTION READING SURVIVED ITS FIRST TEST, BUT RESEARCH CHANGES WHAT IT MEANS, AND THAT IS A CORRECTION TO HOW YESTERDAY FRAMED IT. The prediction held cleanly: accept_pct_mature 50.0 -> 35.0 on a growing denominator, lost>gained two days running. What yesterday did not know is that this signature is being reported across the web. Site owners describe entire properties — publishers, ecommerce, SaaS and programmatic operations alike — flipping to 'Crawled - currently not indexed' from late April through June 2026 after the March core update, including sites indexed for six years with no manual action and no crawl errors, while Google says it sees nothing unusual (support.google.com/webmasters/thread/431274771, searchenginejournal.com/deindexing-reports-keep-coming-google-sees-nothing-unusual/579847). So 'Google is dismantling our June cohort' is true as an observation and NOT established as a verdict on this site's page quality: a sector-wide index tightening produces the identical per-URL signature, and both readings predict lost>gained. THIS CHANGES THE T027 TRIGGER, and it is the most useful thing in today's entry. Yesterday set the trigger at 'a week of index_lost_indexed consistently exceeding index_gained_indexed'. That is now insufficient on its own. Triaging 47,600 pages down to ~3,000 is the most irreversible move on the board, and doing it because Google tightened its index everywhere would be a self-inflicted wound that also destroys the corpus we would need to recover with. The trigger needs a second condition that discriminates between the two causes — see Watching (2). WHAT I ACTUALLY DID ABOUT IT, and why today is not another instrument. Six of the last seven runs shipped instrumentation. The engine can now see extremely well: it dates its own pipeline, catches its own watchdog, counts index transitions per URL. The site itself has not changed since the 08-14 meta descriptions. Whichever of the two causes is real, the same page-level fact holds and is independently checkable: a building page is a template with six variable tokens and every other word on it is identical 47,165 times over. That is the one thing here that is squarely in our control, needs no new URLs while 76.6% of the corpus is still unknown to Google, and is worth doing even if the eviction turns out to be Google-wide — a page that says something no other page says is the only asset that survives either explanation. The published recovery case closest to this site's shape (a 22,500-page programmatic glossary with 16,000 URLs stuck in crawled-not-indexed, recovering to 21,150 indexed in 75 days) fixed link isolation, breadcrumb markup and render overhead — all three of which this site already has, after the 08-13 ring and because the corpus is server-rendered HTML with breadcrumbs. Differentiation is the remaining lever, and it is a vendor case study, so it is a reason to act and not evidence that it works. WHAT IS STILL NOT WORKING, PLAINLY: 0 of 265 tracked queries rank, 25 days at 0.0% share against a 90% target. Serving pages 89 -> 47 in 25 days. 76.6% of the sampled corpus has never been fetched at all. $0 revenue, 0 subscribers, 0 report sales in 25 days. Blocked and not worked around: AdSense still has no slot id so no ads render, api@findacrib.com may still not forward, and the Anthropic balance is empty so scout and outreach are dead until someone adds credit. NEW AND ALSO BLOCKED ON A HUMAN: the Bing route is now backed. Google still does not take IndexNow while Bing, Yandex, Naver and Seznam do; this site already pushes every changed URL to it nightly; Bing's index feeds ChatGPT Search, and one analysis of thousands of SearchGPT citations found ~87% matched Bing's top results. Nobody here has ever measured Bing coverage, and it cannot be measured from this repo — it needs a Bing Webmaster Tools account and API key, which is an operator action, not a code change. If Google's acceptance really is being withdrawn sector-wide, this is the cheapest untested route to the AI-citation goal and the site may already be doing the work blind.

**Changed.** ONE SUBSTANTIAL CHANGE: the building tier now says something per building instead of the same thing 47,165 times. Activated T046 (derived_building_facts) from candidate. (1) build_seo.py — new building_facts(), neighborhood_norms(), _count_below_above(), _floor_pct(), _rate_phrase(), _name_place() and hpd_registration_line(). Every building page now carries an 'How this building compares' paragraph of 3-5 computed sentences: its size percentile and construction year against the DHCR-registered stabilized buildings this site tracks in the SAME neighborhood, its open HPD violations per apartment against that neighborhood's median, the class-C share of its violation record in HPD's own vocabulary, and its tenant complaint count with the last-twelve-months split. Measured over the real corpus: the block renders on 46,853 of 47,165 pages (99.34%) and 98.84% of those blocks are textually distinct from every other; 51/95/108 words min/median/max. The HPD lastregistrationdate also ships, as a dated row in the conditions table. Placed between the lead and the CTA, inside a new .compare wrapper, and OMITTED entirely below three computable sentences rather than padded — 312 pages get nothing. FOUR ACCURACY BUGS WERE FOUND BY SWEEPING ALL 47,165 BUILDINGS AND FIXED BEFORE THIS SHIPPED, all of the same family — a true number rendered into a false sentence: (a) 8 open violations across 198 apartments printed as 'about 0.0 per apartment', contradicting its own first clause, so rates below 0.5 now render as the reciprocal, 'one for every 25 apartments'; (b) 499 of 500 rounded to 'larger than 100% of the 500 buildings', which cannot be true of a page inside its own base set, so shares about the reader's own building now round DOWN and a uniquely largest or smallest building is named as such instead (155 + 72 pages affected); (c) 'HPD lists no open violations here, which it also says of 0% of the tracked buildings' on a page that is itself one of them — 1 in 527 rounds to zero — so that sentence is now a count, not a percentage (18 pages); (d) plural and verb agreement at n=1 ('1 apartments', '1 are class C'). The percentile base was also corrected to the buildings the percentile was computed over, not the whole neighborhood, so the quoted denominator is the one actually used. A deliberate non-claim: the class counters count only violations whose class field is literally A, B or C while `total` counts every row (fetch_hpd.fetch_violations), and a+b+c falls short of total for 33,218 of the 46,245 buildings that have any — so the sentence says 'N of the M violations on record are class C' and never implies what the other M-N are. (2) growth/techniques.py — new t_derived_building_facts, registered in REGISTRY and slotted into ORDER beside hub_direct_answers. It samples 400 building pages evenly through the sorted path list (deterministic between runs) and counts the block, failing below 90%; it has no staging fallback because these pages come from the SEO pipeline, so on the morning after a build_seo change it correctly reads 0% and names the docroot corpus date so that reading cannot be mistaken for the generator dropping the block. Its percentage floors for the same reason as (b). (3) growth/techniques.json — T046 active, prefixes ['/building/'], metric owned_visitors (prefixed techniques are judged on their owned series by review.py; the seeded organic_visitors would have had the ledger claim one thing and the judge read another), hypothesis rewritten to what shipped, and the seeded name trimmed to the facts that actually shipped. Notes record two deliberate deviations from the seed: no 1,000-building control set, because at 1-5 URLs fetched a night a treated set that size would expect ~2 of its pages fetched inside 60 days and 46,165 thin pages left standing as a control preserve the site-level drag the change exists to remove; and neither the RGB Order #58 rent-freeze exposure nor a registration gap shipped, because this build cannot substantiate which leases at an address begin on or after 2026-10-01, nor whether a registration is validly in force today. (4) One-word CSS fix: .answer's border-left referenced var(--accent), which is defined nowhere, so the T019 answer blocks on 623 hub pages have been rendering a dark currentColor rule instead of the accent — now var(--blue). CSS is excluded from the lastmod hash, so this costs no crawl churn. EXPECT A ONE-OFF SPIKE TOMORROW and do not read it as a bug: ~46,853 pages genuinely change content, so lastmod bumps to 2026-08-21 across the building tier, changed_urls jumps from 0 to tens of thousands, and refresh_seo.sh's IndexNow submit truncates to its first 10,000 URLs. That is honest — the pages did change — but it is a single event, not a new baseline. Added no keywords: 265 tracked and 0 ranking, so the tracked universe is not the constraint. Retired nothing; no revisit was due. Did NOT run keywords.check_coverage(). Did NOT start T027 — see Concluded. VERIFIED: py_compile clean on growth/*.py, growth_daily.py and build_seo.py; growth_daily.py build --dry-run --docroot . --build-dir . exits 0; status exits 0; report.build() renders with both parts and table-layout:fixed intact x8 and no javascript: hrefs. Beyond the gates: building_facts was run over all 47,165 buildings against a 14-pattern defect sweep (zero hits) and every quantitative claim — percentile, denominator, largest/smallest, open-violation count, clean count, complaint count, year delta — was independently re-derived from buildings.min.json for 4,000 random buildings with zero mismatches. build_seo.main() was then run end-to-end over a real 500-building neighborhood into a scratch OUT: 777 pages, block on 498 of 500, exactly one block and one heading per page, no raw markup or double-escaping inside it, and every block positioned between the lead and the CTA. t_derived_building_facts was exercised against that rendered docroot (99%, ok) and against a stripped copy (0%, ok False, names the corpus date), a 66% partial regression (ok False) and an empty docroot. Nothing was written into the repo tree by any of it.

The diff did what it said. Nothing was due for revisit (earliest is T003/T004/T005/T008 on 08-25).

**Concluded.** THE 08-02 'TWO DOORS' DIAGNOSIS WAS RIGHT AND HAS BEEN TRUE EVERY NIGHT SINCE, AND FOUR REVIEWS INCLUDING MINE HAVE BEEN DRAWING CONCLUSIONS AS IF IT WERE NOT. /root/dhcr-build is not a git worktree; refresh_seo.sh's guarded pull therefore takes its else branch, prints one line to a stdout growth_run.sh deliberately discards, and runs a build_seo.py frozen at pre-2026-07-29. The corpus is rebuilt and rsynced every night from that frozen generator, so every mtime on the box reads healthy and the report's 'SEO corpus frozen' callout — which fires on corpus age — correctly stayed silent for 24 days. 'Ran clean' was true and useless: the pipeline ran cleanly on the wrong source. What makes this today's finding rather than a restatement is that until now it was an inference; the 0-changed-URLs reading is a controlled experiment, because we knew in advance exactly how many URLs a correct run had to change. THREE CONSECUTIVE REVIEWS' WORK IS UNPUBLISHED, AND TWO CONCLUSIONS BUILT ON IT ARE WRONG. Stranded: the 08-13 sibling-link ring that was supposed to fix 94.9% of building pages having two or fewer inbound internal links; the 08-14 rewrite of 47,165 empty meta descriptions; the 08-21 comparison blocks; the .answer CSS fix. Yesterday I wrote that the published pSEO recovery case fixed 'link isolation, breadcrumb markup and render overhead — all three of which this site already has, after the 08-13 ring'. That is false. The ring is in git and not on the site, so link isolation is not fixed and remains a live candidate cause for 337 of 445 sampled pages being unknown to Google — which is precisely what today's research says it would look like (2026 sources converge on orphaned and weakly-linked programmatic URLs as the discovery failure, with one case study moving 6,300 -> 21,150 indexed URLs on breadcrumb markup plus contextual link clusters). Yesterday I also treated 'the site has not changed since the 08-14 meta descriptions' as a premise; nothing the SEO pipeline publishes has changed since 07-29. I am NOT claiming the eviction signature is explained by this — 4 lost and 0 gained today with accept_pct_mature at 11.1% is real and the industry-wide tightening reported for the March and May 2026 core updates is real — but the site's own two most plausible self-inflicted causes have never once been tested live, and until they are, every content conclusion this loop has drawn is untested rather than falsified. WHAT IS STILL NOT WORKING, PLAINLY: 0 of 265 tracked queries rank; 26 days at 0.0% share against a 90% target; serving pages 89 -> 46; 75.7% of the sample never fetched; $0 revenue, 0 subscribers, 0 report sales. Blocked and not worked around: AdSense still has no slot id, api@findacrib.com may still not forward, the Anthropic balance is empty (empty account, not the usage cap) so scout and outreach are dead, and Bing Webmaster Tools still needs an operator to create the account before anything can be measured there.

**Changed.** MADE THE CLOSED DOOR OPEN, INSTEAD OF ASKING AN OPERATOR TO OPEN IT FOR THE TWENTIETH DAY. (1) scripts/refresh_seo.sh: the pull now records its outcome in a variable (ok / failed / no-worktree) instead of only echoing it, and a new STEP=code stage hands the source over when the pull did not. $SRC is resolved from ${BASH_SOURCE[0]} BEFORE the cd to $BUILD — growth_run.sh's watchdog invokes the copy in the checkout it has just pulled, so $SRC is current source while $BUILD holds the night's scraped data. Deliberately narrow: only when the pull did not already do it, so a $BUILD that pulls is never touched; only build_seo.py and seo_guides.py, named explicitly — not buildings.min.json or listings.json (scraped nightly INTO $BUILD; the checkout's copies are stale), not seo_lastmod.json (per-corpus state — replacing it would bump every lastmod on the site, the one signal the whole file exists to protect), not scripts/ (already run from $SRC); only if they differ; only if they compile, because replacing working code with code that does not parse would publish nothing at all; never a delete or a move. The build itself is unchanged — CWD stays $BUILD, so OUT, the lastmod state and every data read resolve exactly where they do today. BUILD and DOC became ${SEO_BUILD_DIR}/${SEO_DOCROOT} with the same defaults and the same variable name growth_run.sh uses, only so this is testable. (2) growth/techniques.py: 'pull' and 'code' added to SEO_STATUS_FIELDS (the public-repo whitelist), and _seo_pipeline_status now prefixes 'BUILT FROM STALE SOURCE — …. Nothing pushed to build_seo.py or seo_guides.py is live.' when code is no-source or skipped-compile, and appends the sync count when it worked. (3) growth/report.py: a SECOND callout, on a different question from the existing one. The old one asks 'did the pipeline write?' and answered yes every night for 24 days; this one asks 'whose code did it write?'. (4) growth_daily.py: the status line carries pull and code. (5) growth_run.sh: the watchdog's freshness gate now refuses to stand down on a fresh corpus whose own last record says it was built from stale source. Without this the fix has a silent death: if the 04:10 cron ever returns it will run $BUILD's own frozen copy of refresh_seo.sh, stamp the sitemaps at 04:10, and the 05:40 watchdog would read 'fresh' and never fire again, leaving the site permanently on pre-07-29 code with every instrument green. (6) growth/techniques.json: T046's notes record that its 08-21 activation date is not the date its pages went live, so the 60-day window is judged from 08-23. TESTED, not just compiled: refresh_seo.sh was run end-to-end against a scratch $SRC/$BUILD pair through all five branches — $BUILD not a worktree with stale code (synced-1, and the NEW generator ran with OUT correctly resolving to $BUILD/seo); second run (in-sync, nothing copied); $SRC deliberately made to not compile (skipped-compile, $BUILD's copy kept, build still ran); $SRC == $BUILD, i.e. the old cron (no-source, no copy attempted); and $BUILD git-init'd (head reads a real sha, which is the experiment that confirms head='unknown' means 'not a worktree'). The trap still reported the step it died in when rsync was missing. _seo_pipeline_status was run against six fixtures including the truncated-corpus and no-fields-at-all cases, and the report was rendered with each code value: the callout fires on no-source and skipped-compile in both the HTML and the text part, and stays silent on synced-2. Verified: py_compile clean on growth/*.py, growth_daily.py and build_seo.py; bash -n clean on both scripts; 'build --dry-run --docroot . --build-dir .' exits 0; status exits 0; report renders both parts with table-layout:fixed intact x8 and no javascript: hrefs. Added no keywords — 265 tracked and 0 ranking, so the tracked universe is not the constraint. Retired nothing. No revisit was due. Did NOT run keywords.check_coverage().

**Watching.** (1) TOMORROW'S seo_pipeline RECORD IS THE FALSIFIER AND IT IS UNAMBIGUOUS. On 2026-08-23 it must read code: 'synced-1' or 'synced-2' and changed_urls in the tens of thousands — 46,853 comparison blocks plus 47,165 meta descriptions plus the sibling ring all land in one rebuild. Expect IndexNow to truncate its submit to 10,000 URLs; that is honest, the pages really did change, and it is a single event, not a new baseline. If code still reads '?' the droplet's growth checkout did not pull this commit; if it reads 'no-source' the watchdog is invoking $BUILD's own script rather than the checkout's, which is a different repair. (2) DO NOT MISREAD t_derived_building_facts ON 08-23. Techniques run at 05:40:23, the watchdog rebuilds at 05:41, so on 08-23 it reads the 08-22 corpus and will correctly report 0% again. The first day it can read the block is 2026-08-24 and the bar is >=90%. A 0% reading on 08-24 means the sync ran but the block is not being generated, which is a build_seo.py bug, not a deploy one. (3) THE REAL TEST STARTS 08-23, NOT 08-21. Three weeks of stranded work goes live in one night: per-page differentiation, 47,165 non-empty meta descriptions, and the internal-link ring. Baseline to measure against, 2026-08-22: unknown_to_google 337 of 445 (75.7%), fetched 18.4%, indexed 5 (1.1%), accept_pct_mature 11.1%, index_pct_building 1.2%, serving_pages 46, serving_ever 266. If fetched% and unknown% have not moved by ~2026-09-13 — three weeks, long enough for a re-crawl cycle on a corpus Google touches 1-5 URLs a night — then page quality and link structure were NOT the constraint, the answer is domain authority or the sector-wide tightening, and T027 consolidation becomes the honest next move rather than more content. Say which happened. (4) EVERY CONCLUSION THIS LOOP DREW BETWEEN 07-29 AND 08-22 ABOUT WHETHER A CONTENT CHANGE WORKED IS UNTESTED, because none of those changes were on the site. Do not cite them as evidence. (5) THE OTHER DOOR IS STILL SHUT AND IS NOW THE ONLY ONE: this fix routes build_seo.py and seo_guides.py around it, but nothing else in /root/dhcr-build is being updated, and the one-line owner action 'git -C /root/dhcr-build pull origin main' — or, better, making that directory a worktree at all — remains outstanding after 20 days. (6) serving_ever growth decelerating (+3 today vs +9-16 a week ago) is the number to watch alongside serving_pages: if discovery flattens too, the problem is crawling rather than visibility budget, which is a worse diagnosis. ## 2026-08-23 — The frozen-corpus fix landed — 47,638 URLs changed. And the loop has been cancelling its own scheduled decisions: every retirement fired 9 days early, by construction
*6am review*

**Observed.** THE 08-22 FALSIFIER PASSED, UNAMBIGUOUSLY. It required code: 'synced-1' or 'synced-2' and changed_urls in the tens of thousands. This morning's seo_pipeline record reads code: 'synced-2', changed_urls: 47,638 of 47,643 pages built, 05:40:29-05:41:41, rc 0, pull still 'no-worktree'. Yesterday the same rebuild reported 0. Three weeks of stranded work — the 08-13 sibling-link ring, the 08-14 rewrite of 47,165 empty meta descriptions, the 08-21 comparison blocks, the .answer CSS fix, and CITY_NAV — went live at 05:41 today, for the first time since 2026-07-29. I checked yesterday's changed: line against the repo and all six claims hold: refresh_seo.sh has STEP=code with $SRC resolved from BASH_SOURCE before the cd and states no-source/skipped-compile/synced-N; SEO_STATUS_FIELDS carries pull and code; techniques.py prefixes 'BUILT FROM STALE SOURCE'; report.py has the second callout; growth_daily.py's status line carries both; growth_run.sh's freshness gate checks code. SEARCH, all pre-fix (measure ran 05:11, the rebuild finished 05:41). gsc_serving_pages 37 of ~47,600 (0.078%) — 89 on 07-27, 86 on 08-10, 46 yesterday, 37 today. serving_ever 267, up ONE, against +9-16 a night two weeks ago: discovery has flattened, which the 08-22 entry named as the worse diagnosis. serving_stable 36, 1 entered, 10 left. 3 clicks and 7 query-dimension impressions (55 page-dimension) in the week to 08-21; search_share 0.0% with 0 of 265 tracked queries ranking, unbroken since the series began. INDEX CENSUS, 445 URLs read: 3 indexed (0.7%), 79 crawled-not-indexed, 34 discovered-never-crawled, 329 (74%) unknown to Google. Ever-fetched stuck at exactly 18.4% for the third straight day. accept_pct_mature 50.0 -> 35.0 -> 11.1 -> 3.6 over four days; index_pct_building 3.6 -> 2.8 -> 2.4 -> 1.2 -> 0.8. lost_indexed 2, gained_indexed 0 — fourth consecutive day of losses with zero gains. EVER-FETCHED BY FAMILY, and this is the shape of the whole finding: /borough/ 50% (10 read), /neighborhood/ 35% (40), /building/ 22% (250), /zip/ 10% (40), /sf/ 5% (20), then /dc/ 0% (20), /la/ 0% (20), /available/ 0% (15), /brief/ 0% (10), /guide/ 0% (10), /section8/ 0% (6). Traffic 17 visitors on 08-22, 1 organic, 2 AI. Revenue $0/mo, 0 paying subscribers, 6 signups, 0 Building Report sales in 27 days. t_derived_building_facts read 0 of 400 sampled building pages, exactly as the 08-22 entry predicted it would — techniques run at 05:40:23, the watchdog rebuilds at 05:41, so today it read the 08-22 corpus. Nothing was due for revisit; earliest is 08-25. THE ANTHROPIC ERROR STRING CHANGED TODAY: scout and outreach both failed with 'You have reached your specified API usage limits. You will regain access on 2026-09-01 at 00:00 UTC.' Yesterday it was 'Your credit balance is too low'. So it is the self-imposed usage cap now, not the empty account, and the regain date has moved from 08-01 to 09-01.

**Concluded.** THE PIPELINE REPAIR WORKED AND THE REAL EXPERIMENT STARTS TODAY, NOT 08-21. Everything measured this morning describes a site running pre-07-29 content. Any comparison drawn against today's numbers must use 2026-08-23 as its baseline. One correction to the 08-21 entry's watch list: it said to judge CITY_NAV on 2026-09-03 because it went live 08-20. It did not — nothing the SEO pipeline publishes changed between 07-29 and 05:41 today, so CITY_NAV is one day old, and the honest date is ~2026-09-13, the same date the 08-22 entry set for everything else. TODAY'S NEW FINDING IS THAT THIS LOOP HAS BEEN CANCELLING ITS OWN SCHEDULED DECISIONS, BY CONSTRUCTION, AND NOBODY NOTICED BECAUSE THE CANCELLATION IS SILENT. review.py retires on GRACE_DAYS = 21 days after activation. ledger._revisit_date() books the deliberate second look at REVISIT_DAYS = 30. So on the default dates the automatic retirement fires exactly nine days before every scheduled revisit — and revisit_due() filtered to status == 'active', so the retirement did not delay that look, it deleted it, leaving the date sitting in the record. Four for four, every retirement this loop has ever made: T001 and T002 retired 08-16 against revisits booked for 08-25; T023 retired 08-19 against 08-28; T013 retired 08-20 against 08-29. Nine days early in every case, because it is arithmetic, not accident. The T001 case is the worst of them: its own notes carried an explicit decision written by the 08-05 review — 'NEW DECISION DATE 2026-08-26: if /section8/ still earns zero impressions 21 days after it has a verified inbound link, the fault is the content and it retires then' — and the machine retired it on 08-16, ten days early, on the same impressions metric that note had just finished ruling out, during the window when the inbound link it was waiting on was sitting unpublished in git. The reasoning was in the ledger and nothing read it. THE SECOND HALF IS WORSE THAN THE FIRST, BECAUSE THE ERROR RAN THE WRONG WAY. t_crawl_paths audited ledger.active() only, so retiring a technique withdrew its prefixes from the crawl-path audit the same night. But retirement does not unpublish anything: those pages stay in the docroot, stay in sitemap-daily.xml and stay in the nightly IndexNow submit. And sections retire BECAUSE they earned no impressions, which is precisely the symptom an orphaned crawl path produces — so the one instrument that could have told 'the content failed' from 'nothing linked to it' switched itself off at the exact moment its reading would have challenged the verdict. This is the same class of fault as the 24-day frozen corpus: an instrument reporting green on the condition it exists to catch. Today's census is the measurement of what it cost — across the families no active technique owns (/dc/, /la/, /sf/, /available/, /brief/, /guide/, /section8/), 1 of 101 sampled URLs has ever been fetched by Google, against 22-50% for the four families the audit did watch. I am NOT claiming orphaning explains all of it: the depth gradient (borough 50%, neighborhood 35%, building 22%, zip 10%, unwatched 0%) is consistent with 2026 guidance that a URL more than 3-4 clicks from the homepage indexes very slowly, and equally consistent with site-level crawl rationing, which no internal link fixes. But the loop was not in a position to tell, and now it is. WHAT IS STILL NOT WORKING, PLAINLY: 0 of 265 tracked queries rank; 27 days at 0.0% share against a 90% target; serving pages 89 -> 37; 74% of the sample never fetched; ever-fetched frozen at 18.4% for three days; four straight days of index losses with zero gains and mature acceptance down to 3.6%; $0 revenue, 0 subscribers, 0 report sales in 27 days. Blocked and not worked around: AdSense still has no slot id, api@findacrib.com may still not forward, Bing Webmaster Tools still needs an operator to create the account, and the Anthropic usage cap holds scout and outreach dead until 2026-09-01.

**Changed.** REPAIRED THE MECHANISM THAT DISCARDS THIS LOOP'S OWN REASONING, AND THE INSTRUMENT THAT WENT BLIND WITH IT. (1) growth/ledger.py revisit_due(): now returns a RETIRED technique whose revisit_on has come, bounded to the case that is actually broken — only when the retirement landed BEFORE the date booked for that look, i.e. only where the retirement is what pre-empted it. One retired after its revisit date already had its look and does not come back. A retirement now defers the second look instead of deleting it; the automatic rule is untouched and still fires on day 21. Tested: nothing due today (correct); as of 08-25 it returns T001 and T002 alongside the four active techniques; as of 08-29 it adds T013 and T023. (2) growth/report.py: a deferred revisit renders differently from an ordinary one — meta gains 'RETIRED <date>, before this date' and the body leads with 'Retired before its scheduled second look — reconsider the verdict, do not skip it' plus the verdict text, because the job on 08-25 is not keep/change/replace, it is 'was that verdict sound'. ## 2026-08-24 — 258 of the 271 pages that have ever served are single-address building pages, and 12 are hubs — the tier the ledger has been calling 'what works' is the one Google has barely shown
*6am review*

**Observed.** gsc_serving_pages 39 (+2 on 08-23's 37, against 88 on 08-09 — roughly halved in a fortnight); gsc_serving_ever 271 (+4, against +9-16 a night two weeks ago); serving_stable 35, 4 entered / 2 left overnight. search_share_pct 0.0 with 0 of 290 tracked queries in the top 10 (scout added 25 queries this morning, taking 265 to 290). Index census: 450 sampled, 3 indexed (0.7%), 332 unknown to Google (73.8%), 79 crawled-not-indexed, 36 discovered-not-indexed, ever-fetched 18.2%, accept_pct_mature 2.9% of 34 mature-fetched. Visitors 12 on 08-23, 0 organic, 2 AI. MRR $0, 0 paying subs, 6 signups. THE NEW NUMBER, computed from the per-URL serving history already sitting in growth/gsc_pages.json (which begins 2026-07-30 and needed no new collection): of the 271 distinct URLs that have EVER earned a Search Console impression, 258 are /building/ address pages, 9 are NYC neighborhood/ZIP/borough hubs, 3 are SF/LA/DC hubs and 1 is the home page. Today: 37 of the 39 serving URLs are building pages, 1 is an NYC hub, 1 is home. Median run a URL holds its place: /building/ 7 days (max 17), NYC hubs 8 (max 14), SF/LA/DC hubs 16 (max 16), home 24 of 24. Five whole families have NEVER served a single URL in 25 days of record: /guide/, /section8/, /brief/, /available/, /developers/ (/council-district/ too, but it shipped four days ago). Predicted-and-confirmed: t_derived_building_facts read 399 of 400 sampled building pages (99%) on its first day against the unfrozen corpus, exactly the bar 08-23 set; 08-23's 0-of-400 was the frozen corpus, not a generator bug. Build: 0 changed URLs, so the corpus has had one stable day after 08-23's 47,638. Anthropic API: the scout ran CLEAN this morning (ok true, 3 techniques, 25 keywords) and outreach ok true — the usage cap 08-23 recorded with a 2026-09-01 regain date is NOT in effect. Yesterday's diff checked against the repo: a2fd203 does contain the ledger.revisit_due deferral, the report.py deferred-revisit rendering, the widened t_crawl_paths and the four ledger notes it claimed. Nothing due for revisit today.

**Concluded.** The loop has been reasoning about the wrong tier for weeks. T019 hub_direct_answers carries a WORKS verdict — 26 owned visitors in 27d, median 1.0/day and rising — and every content proposal since has leaned on 'hubs with a direct answer are the one thing measured to work here', including T059, proposed by the scout this morning, whose hypothesis says so in those words. The search record says the opposite: the 623 hub pages have produced 12 lifetime serving URLs between them and 1 today, while the 47,165 building pages have produced 258 and 37. At 0-1 organic clicks a day site-wide, owned_visitors cannot distinguish a search arrival from an internal click, so T019's 26 visitors are internal, direct or AI-referred — the verdict is real about traffic and says nothing about search. This is not proof the answer block is worthless; it is proof that almost nothing has been measured about it, because Google has barely shown the tier it sits on. It also reframes the 88-to-39 collapse: the building tier is not failing to be crawled, it is being crawled, shown for a median of 7 days, and dropped. 258 distinct building pages earned impressions in 25 days — far more of the corpus is being tested than '3 indexed of 450' suggests — and the constraint on that tier is RETENTION, not discovery. That is precisely what T046's computed comparison block, live on 99% of building pages as of this morning, is meant to fix, and it is the reason not to touch the building tier again this week: a second building-wide change now would confound the only content experiment currently running, on top of being a second mass lastmod bump two days after 47,638 URLs moved. What ISN'T working, said plainly: search share is 0.0% and has never been anything else; revenue is $0 with the API fully built; the hub tier, which has absorbed most of the content investment since 2026-08-09, has 12 lifetime impressions to show for it; and five published families have never once been shown. AdSense still has no slot id so no ads render, and api@findacrib.com may still not forward — both unchanged and unfixable from here.

**Changed.** Shipped the measurement that produced the finding, so it is a nightly fact rather than a one-off analysis, plus the ledger corrections it forces. (1) growth/searchconsole.py: SERVING_TIERS, an ordered first-match-wins table mapping a served path to a page family (home / building / nyc_hub / city_hub / council / guide / voucher / available / developers, catch-all 'other'), and serving_tiers(rows, history) returning per-family now / ever / median_days / max_days. _churn's docstring spotted this in passing on 2026-08-04 ('80 of the 89 were single-address building pages') and nobody ever turned it into a number. The blob goes into gsc_pages.json computed against the history the same run just folded today's rows into, so now and ever are the same night; only the daily per-tier count enters results.jsonl as gsc_serving_<tier>, and only for families that have ever served — ever, the medians and the never-served families are all recomputable from the committed history, so writing them nightly would be storing a derivation. (2) growth/report.py: _serving_tiers() and a four-column block (Page family / Now / Ever / Held) under the existing churn note, plus a note naming every family that has never served. Now is counted from the snapshot's own page list rather than the new series so the table is right the first night it renders instead of a row of dashes; the list is capped at 300 and the counts are marked with a + and called floors if that cap ever bites. Held is the median only — 'max' moved into the note because at 18% of the width '7d (max 17d)' wraps on a phone into what reads as two numbers. 'other' is excluded from the never-served list: an empty catch-all is the healthy case, not a finding. Rendered both parts; table-layout:fixed intact x9, no javascript: hrefs. ## 2026-08-25 — The loop recorded $0.00 of lifetime revenue as WORKS, and a retired /section8/ has been telling readers for nine days that it is rebuilt daily
*6am review*

**Observed.** gsc_serving_pages 31, down from 39 yesterday and 88 on 08-09 — 65% of the site's search visibility gone in 16 days. gsc_serving_ever 271, WHICH IS +0 — the first zero-discovery day on record, against +4 yesterday and +9-16 a night a fortnight ago. serving_stable 31. search_share_pct 0.0 with 0 of 290 tracked queries in the top 10; keyword coverage 239/315 (75.9%). Index census: 450 sampled, 3 indexed (0.7%), 341 unknown to Google (75.8%, UP from 332/73.8% yesterday), 79 crawled-not-indexed, 27 discovered-not-indexed (DOWN from 36) — i.e. 9 URLs moved backwards from discovered to unknown; ever-fetched 18.2%, accept_pct_mature 2.8% of 36. Visitors 15 on 08-24, 1 organic, 1 AI. MRR $0, 0 paying subs, 28 comped, 6 accounts with saves, 3 API keys. Build 0 new / 0 changed from growth techniques; the SEO pipeline changed 98 of 47,954 pages, so the corpus has had two stable days after 08-23's 47,638. Anthropic API: scout ok=true (3 techniques, 25 keywords) and outreach ok=true, drafted 0 — THE USAGE CAP IS NOT IN EFFECT, third consecutive clean morning. YESTERDAY'S DIFF CHECKED AND CONFIRMED: searchconsole.py has SERVING_TIERS and serving_tiers(), report.py has _serving_tiers(), and the proof it works end-to-end is that the tier rows landed this morning exactly as booked — gsc_serving_building 29, nyc_hub 1, city_hub 0, home 1, summing to 31 = gsc_serving_pages, so no URL shape fell into 'other' and SERVING_TIERS needs no new rule. THE FINDING: T008 b2b_outreach carried the verdict 'WORKS — mrr_usd median 0.0/day since activation (no pre-activation baseline)', decided by review.py at 05:00 this morning. Its declared metric has read exactly $0.00 every one of its 30 days.

**Concluded.** The review had three code paths whose own why-string says the evidence cannot support a judgement — 'not enough days of measurement', 'search visibility has never been measured', and 'no pre-activation baseline' — and all three fell through to run()'s keep branch, which wrote works=True unconditionally. So the loop was recording 'we could not tell' as 'it works'. This is not cosmetic: scout.py feeds the WORKS list into the prompt that proposes tomorrow's techniques under the heading ALREADY MEASURED AS WORKING, so a false positive does not sit still, it breeds. T008 is worse than unproven, it is UNFALSIFIABLE AS CONFIGURED: it drafts outreach and by deliberate and correct design never sends it, so its revenue metric was always going to read 0.0 whatever the drafts were worth. Second finding, and the one that actually reaches readers: retirement stops the rebuild but does not unpublish, drop from sitemap-daily.xml, or stop IndexNow — t_crawl_paths' own docstring says so — which means that since 2026-08-16 findacrib.com/section8/ has been serving an H1 reading 'updated August 16, 2026', a subhead promising 'Rebuilt daily from the overnight feed', and a table presenting nine-day-old apartment listings as available right now. Tenants act on that by travelling to see units that are gone. The 2026-08-16 retirement that caused it was itself unsound on grounds this ledger already recorded (frozen corpus, nine-day pre-emption) and its stated basis — 'invisible in search, so the fault is the content' — cannot survive the 08-24 tier census showing every non-building family at zero. THIRD, and the one that may reframe six weeks of reasoning: Google has confirmed Search Console impression-logging errors affecting data from 2026-08-13, plus a broader impressions correction rolling out since 2026-04-03 that it said would lower many sites' reported impressions. gsc_serving_pages is DERIVED from impressions and this site's decline steepens at exactly 08-13 (88 on 08-09, 66 on 08-13, 39 on 08-24, 31 today). That is not a conclusion — the confirmed errors name the Generative AI and Discover reports, which is narrower than web search — but every content decision since 08-09 has rested on serving_pages, and it is cheaply testable because the URL Inspection census is an independent instrument. WHAT ISN'T WORKING, plainly: search share is 0.0% and has never been anything else; revenue is $0 with the API fully built; serving_ever went +0 today, meaning discovery has stopped, which is worse than losing rankings; 9 sampled URLs moved backwards from discovered to unknown; and after today's fix the scoreboard reads 1 WORKS, 3 DIDN'T WORK, 8 UNPROVEN — four of those eight sharing a near-identical why-string because they are all judged on site-wide organic_visitors, which is proof the metric does not discriminate between them. AdSense still has no slot id so no ads render, and api@findacrib.com may still not forward.

**Changed.** FOUR changes, all verified. (1) VERDICTS ARE NOW TRI-STATE. review.py: every evaluate() return sets an explicit works of True/False/None, and run() writes r['works'] instead of a bare True; None means judged-and-not-established and is distinct from having no verdict at all, which means too young to look. A flag now also writes works=None so an earlier WORKS cannot stand unchallenged underneath it. ledger.set_verdict stops coercing with bool() and stores the third state. scoreboard() gained an 'unproven' bucket; report.py renders it as a third UNPROVEN row in mute tone rather than folding it into either side; growth_daily.py status prints it; scout.py moves it OUT of the won list into a new RUNNING BUT UNPROVEN section that asks the model for a metric that would resolve it rather than more of the same. Applied: T008 flipped from WORKS to UNPROVEN, and seven flagged techniques gained explicit unproven verdicts where they previously had silence. (2) HONEST FRESHNESS ON THE VOUCHER PAGES. techniques.py: FEED_STALE_HOURS=48 and _feed_freshness(ctx), which derives every present-tense claim on /section8/ and its five borough pages from the feed's own avail_updated timestamp. Beyond the threshold the title, meta description, H1, subhead, stats label and schema.org Dataset description all switch from 'listed now / right now / Updated daily / refreshed nightly / currently have' to dated past-tense wording, and a caveat block appears naming the feed date, the age in days, and the fact that the DHCR stabilization status is unaffected because it does not come from this feed. A missing timestamp counts as stale, since the failure that loses the timestamp is the one that leaves the data old. t_daily_brief now refuses to publish at all when the feed is stale, because dating old numbers today would put a fabricated point in the one artefact whose entire value is being a genuine dated record. Verified both branches by rendering: the repo's committed s8.json is 44 days old and produces the full caveat treatment, and with a 3-hour-old timestamp the copy is word-for-word identical to what shipped before, so a healthy feed sees no change. (3) SIX REVISITS DECIDED, NOT RATIFIED. T001 /section8/ REACTIVATED, overturning the 08-16 retirement — with a new ledger.reactivate() that restarts the measurement clock, keeps the original date in first_activated and clears the superseded verdict. Without that clock restart the revival would have died within 24 hours: set_status leaves activated at 2026-07-26, so review.py would have re-retired it tonight on the very evidence this revisit rejected, and the ledger would have shown the revisit agreeing with the retirement. T002 /brief/ STAYS RETIRED but on a better basis — not 'invisible in search', which is as unsupported for it as for T001, but that it mints a new URL every night into a corpus where 341 of 450 sampled URLs are unknown to Google; a fixed URL set that rewrites itself is affordable there and a URL-minting one is not. T003 keep, with its own verdict disowned as an artefact of a 0-2/day series. T004 keep, and it is the one due today with a real result: the 252-page sitemap hole its 08-17 note said to watch has closed, rescued is 0. T005 keep, with the observation that it is judged on a Google metric while its own docstring says Google does not consume IndexNow, and that it submitted 0 URLs today while the SEO pipeline changed 98. T008 keep with a dated condition: if no draft has been sent by 2026-09-24, retire it — not because outreach fails but because this loop cannot execute it. All six revisit dates pushed to 2026-09-24. Added ledger.note() so a revisit can record reasoning without flipping a status it does not want to flip. (4) THE AD FLIGHT IS NOW EXCLUDED IN CODE, not in prose. review.PAID_FLIGHT_DAYS filters 2026-07-17/18 (288 and 279 visitors against a ~20/day baseline) out of every site-wide series, and each site-wide why-string now carries window TOTALS beside the medians. This mattered immediately: T018 read '229 in the last 14d vs 842 before' and now reads '229 vs 332'; T003 read 'median 0.0 vs 0.5' and now reads 'median 0.0 vs 0.0 [8 in the last 14d vs 6 before]', which is flat, not the decline it was being called. Every review since July has been told in prose to subtract those two days by hand. Also recorded three research findings as ledger notes, one of which corrects a note I wrote earlier in this same run: Google's 2026-07-22 crawl-budget update says the crawl capacity limit is SHARED ACROSS ALL GOOGLE CRAWLERS, so Google-Extended competes with Googlebot for one pool — which means T003's allow-list of 15 AI crawlers is not the free text file I called it two hours ago, and makes T043's premise concrete. Deliberately NOT acted on: that trade runs against T003's own goal and needs the Googlebot vs Google-Extended split from server logs, which nobody has. DID NOT ship the building-page link to /section8/, /brief/ and /available/ that 08-23 booked for ~08-26 — it would confound T046's first clean week, and /brief/ is now a link to a section deliberately left retired. Added no keywords (290 tracked, 0 ranking; the universe is not the constraint). Did NOT run keywords.check_coverage(). Verified: py_compile clean; build --dry-run --docroot . --build-dir . exits 0 and t_fresh_section8 builds its 6 pages; status exits 0; report renders both parts, table-layout:fixed intact x9, no javascript: hrefs.

Subtract 'jayshomefinder' from any click count — all 3 of today's query-dimension clicks are that decommissioned brand. ## 2026-08-26 — The revenue metric could not contain the revenue: mrr_usd = paying_subs x 4.99, and both products that launched are invisible to it. Also: mature index acceptance hit 0.0%
*6am review*

**Observed.** GATING NUMBER: gsc_serving_pages 28, down from 31 yesterday, 39 on 08-24 and 88 on 08-09 — 2 entered, 5 dropped, 0.06% of ~47,600 published. gsc_serving_ever 272, up ONE from 271, so yesterday's feared second consecutive zero did not land but discovery is a rounding error above it. THE READING THAT SETTLES YESTERDAY'S OPEN QUESTION: index_accept_pct_mature is 0.0% today — of the 37 sampled URLs Google last fetched 21+ days ago, ZERO are indexed. That series runs 50.0 (08-20), 35.0, 11.1, 3.6, 2.9, 2.8, 0.0. By crawl age: 0-6d 1 of 7 indexed, 7-13d 1 of 20, 14-20d 0 of 18, 21-27d 0 of 27, 28d+ 0 of 10 — the only indexed pages in a 450-URL sample are ones crawled in the last fortnight. States: indexed 2 (was 3; lost 1, gained 0), crawled-not-indexed 80, discovered-never-crawled 28, unknown-to-Google 340 (was 341). Ever-fetched 18.2%, flat four days. Traffic 08-25: 25 visitors, 7 organic, 2 AI; 3 GSC clicks, all still the decommissioned jayshomefinder brand; 46 page impressions. Revenue $0: paying_subs 0, api_keys 3, accounts_with_saves 6. This morning's review wrote 2 WORKS / 3 DIDN'T WORK / 8 UNPROVEN — one more WORKS than yesterday predicted, because T003 llms_txt flipped on ai_visitors median 0.5/day vs 0.0 [10 in the last 14d vs 6 before]. CRONS AND JOBS ALL CLEAN: 05:00 daily rc=0, 05:40 build rc=0, watchdog ran refresh_seo.sh to completion, seo pipeline in-sync at 47,954 pages, scout ok (1 technique added), outreach ok (0 drafted, legal segment) — four clean mornings now, the Anthropic cap story is dead, stop repeating it. YESTERDAY'S CLAIMS CHECKED AGAINST THE REPO AND ALL FOUR LANDED: review.PAID_FLIGHT_DAYS present, techniques._feed_freshness/FEED_STALE_HOURS present, ledger.reactivate/note present, tri-state works=None present. T001 /section8/ rebuilt on its first morning back: fresh_section8 ok, 293 buildings across 5 boroughs, against 253 from this repo's own committed s8.json (avail_updated 2026-07-11) — so the droplet's feed is live and fresher than the tracked copy, and the stale-caveat branch is not firing in production.

**Concluded.** FIRST, the artifact hypothesis is refuted as the main story, and that matters because yesterday made it the cheapest way to find out whether six weeks of conclusions were about the wrong thing. The test was: if gsc_serving_pages keeps falling while ever-fetched and indexed hold flat, the collapse is substantially a Search Console reporting artifact. Ever-fetched did hold flat at 18.2% — but indexed fell 3 to 2 and mature acceptance walked 2.8% to 0.0%, and the URL Inspection census has nothing to do with GSC impressions. Two independent instruments now point the same way, so the honest reading is that the decline is real. Two caveats I will not let a future review drop: the census is at its floor (2 of 450), so 'flat' there is not evidence of health, it is evidence of a number that cannot go much lower; and Google's confirmed impression-logging errors from 08-13 may still inflate the STEEPNESS without changing the direction. What 0.0% mature acceptance means concretely: Google is not failing to find these pages, it is finding them and declining them. The constraint is acceptance, not discovery and not demand — which is what T027 (index triage: promote a few thousand, consolidate the rest) and T060 have said, and this is the strongest evidence yet for doing one of them. Today's external research agrees from the other side: the 2026 guidance on large programmatic corpora is to prune or merge low-value templated pages, ship 50-100 high-conviction pages, validate the template for 2-4 weeks, and only then scale — and to sample with URL Inspection rather than trust aggregate counts, which is exactly what T053 already built. SECOND, AND THE FINDING OF THE DAY: the loop's revenue number was structurally incapable of containing this site's revenue. mrr_usd was computed, in full, as paying_subs * 4.99 — the price of the legacy Plus subscription and of nothing else the site sells. It was therefore blind to the Developer API tiers, whose checkout lives in api_server.py and sets api_keys.tier through the api_set_tier RPC without ever touching the subscriptions table, so a $199/mo Business key would have been counted as $0.00; and blind BY CONSTRUCTION to the one-time $9 Building Report, which has no recurring component for MRR arithmetic to find. T011 is a technique whose entire audience is Building Report buyers and it was being judged on mrr_usd: it could not have registered its own revenue if the sequence had worked perfectly. That is the same failure mode as T008 on 2026-08-25, arriving by a different road, and it means the $10,000/month goal has been measured against a number that can only count $4.99 increments of one product. THIRD, a second structural defect in the same area: stocks were being judged as flows. accounts_with_saves, paying_subs, api_keys and mrr_usd are cumulative counters, and review.py compared the median of one window against the median of the one before. For a monotonic counter that comparison is rigged in both directions — a stock that grew at all scores works=True forever, which is yesterday's false-positive machinery by another route, and a flat stock scores 'no measurable lift' with no way to tell a technique that failed from one nothing has entered yet. FOURTH, AND THE ONE THAT ALMOST PUT A FALSE CLAIM IN THIS JOURNAL: seed.run() re-applies `metric` from growth/seed.py on every single run, so a metric changed through the ledger alone reverts on the next build, silently, with no error and no log line. I repointed T011 and T018, verified them changed, ran the build, and they were back to mrr_usd and visitors — while the notes explaining the change survived, so the ledger read exactly as though the change had been made. That is the trap this journal was warned about, and the only reason it did not become another entry claiming a fix its diff never made is that I re-read the ledger after the build rather than after the write. FIFTH, on the two revisits themselves: T018 is NOT failing. It has sent 26 emails over 30 days to real free accounts, and accounts_with_saves went 3 to 6 in that time. It was being judged on site-wide `visitors`, which 26 onboarding emails cannot move — the 'no measurable lift, 16/day vs 23/day' verdict was measuring the paid-ad flight and the search collapse and attributing them to an email sequence. T011 has not failed either; it has never run. Its lifetime output is 2 emails, both on 2026-07-27, because no Building Report has ever sold. SIXTH, T003's fresh WORKS is fragile and should not be leaned on: it rests on 10 AI-referral visitors against 6, and two 2026 sources point the other way — Shepard's citation study rates llms.txt 2.0/10 with no credible evidence it influences LLM citations, and Google's May 2026 guidance says AI Overviews and AI Mode require no llms.txt and no special AI markup. ai_visitors counts referral clicks from AI hosts, which is not the same thing as being cited. WHAT ISN'T WORKING, PLAINLY: search share 0.0% and never anything else; 0 of 315 tracked queries in the top 10; revenue $0 with the API fully built and the $9 report live for 30 days with zero sales; mature index acceptance 0.0%; AdSense still has no slot id so no ads render; api@findacrib.com may still not forward.

**Changed.** FIVE changes, all verified after a build rather than after the write. (1) THE REVENUE METRIC NOW MEASURES THE REVENUE. growth/metrics.py: named price constants sourced in-repo rather than from memory (PLUS_MRR_USD 4.99 from the stripe-webhook plan, API_TIER_MRR_USD pro 49 / business 199 from api_server.PRICES, REPORT_PRICE_USD 9.0 from index.html's own button text); _count_api_tiers() counts paid API keys by tier so mrr_usd is plus + pro + business instead of plus alone; _report_sales() reads building_reports for lifetime and trailing-30-day paid rows. New metrics: api_keys_pro, api_keys_business, reports_sold, reports_sold_30d, revenue_usd_30d. mrr_usd stays STRICTLY RECURRING so its month of history keeps meaning the same thing, and revenue_usd_30d (recurring plus 30 days of one-time sales) is the number to read against a goal that is about money arriving in a month. Every added query is wrapped: a schema surprise on a new table must not cost the day its visitors, its signups and its subscription count, and an unreadable building_reports reports nothing rather than a fake '0 sold'. I cannot execute this path from here — it needs the service key — so it is written defensively and the first thing tomorrow must check is that the new keys actually appear in results.jsonl. (2) STOCKS ARE NO LONGER JUDGED AS FLOWS. growth/review.py: STOCK_METRICS plus _judge_stock(), which measures the CHANGE across the window as a per-day rate and compares it against the pre-activation rate, requiring MIN_BASELINE_DAYS=7 of pre-activation history before it will attribute anything. A flat stock is now flagged with a why-string that says what flat actually means ('the counter has not moved at all since activation, so either nothing reaches this technique or nothing it does converts') instead of 'no pre-activation baseline'. Verified against real data: T008's mrr_usd verdict improves to exactly that sentence; T018 on accounts_with_saves now reads '3 -> 6 (+3) over 30d, but only 0d of pre-activation history — no comparable baseline, so the movement cannot be attributed either way', which reports the movement and still refuses the causal claim; and every flow-metric technique's verdict is byte-identical to this morning's, so this is additive. (3) T018 REVISIT — CHANGE THE INSTRUMENT, KEEP THE TECHNIQUE. metric visitors -> accounts_with_saves, in growth/seed.py AND the ledger, because seed.py is the source of truth and a ledger-only change reverts. New reading is +3 over 30d, unattributed. Revisit pushed to 2026-09-25. (4) T011 REVISIT — CHANGE THE INSTRUMENT, KEEP IT DORMANT AND HONESTLY LABELLED. metric mrr_usd -> reports_sold in seed.py and the ledger. reports_sold is a GATE, not an attribution — T011 emails people who have already bought and does not cause sales — but while it reads 0 this technique has no audience and any verdict on it is noise. Dated condition mirroring T008's: if reports_sold is still 0 on 2026-09-25, retire it as unmeasurable-by-construction, NOT as failed, because retiring it as failed would tell the scout this idea was tried and lost when it has never once run. Also repointed T017 (organic_visitors -> reports_sold) — its revisit is due tomorrow and I deliberately did NOT take that decision, only made it answerable. (5) THE TRAP IS NOW DOCUMENTED WHERE SOMEONE WILL HIT IT. New ledger.set_metric(), which changes the metric, CLEARS the stale verdict (a verdict is a sentence about a metric; leaving 'no measurable lift on mrr_usd' standing under reports_sold asserts something no measurement supports) and dates the change in the notes — with a docstring that says in capitals that calling it alone is not enough for a seeded technique and names today's revert as the example. growth/report.py reads revenue_usd_30d for the revenue tile and the goal bar, falling back to mrr_usd so the month of history before today does not blank out. Recorded a ledger note on T003 with the llms.txt research and the metric mismatch; its revisit stays 2026-09-24. Added no keywords (315 tracked, 259 covered, 0 ranking — the universe is not the constraint). Did NOT run keywords.check_coverage(). Did NOT ship the building-page link to /section8/ and /available/ that 08-23 booked and 08-25 deferred to ~09-01 — T046 still has not had its clean week. VERIFIED: py_compile clean; build --dry-run --docroot . --build-dir . exits 0; status exits 0; report renders both parts, 9 table-layout:fixed intact, 0 javascript: hrefs; seed.run() re-run explicitly and the metrics held.

**Watching.** (1) FIRST AND CHEAPEST: confirm the new revenue keys actually landed. results.jsonl should carry reports_sold, reports_sold_30d, revenue_usd_30d, api_keys_pro and api_keys_business for 2026-08-26. If they are absent, _report_sales() threw and the except swallowed it — that is the deliberate design (never lose the day's core numbers to a new table) but it means the building_reports read is broken and must be fixed, not left. If reports_sold appears and reads 0, that is a real and important zero: 30 days of a live $9 product with no sales. (2) THE SCOREBOARD SHOULD MOVE. T011, T017 and T018 have had their verdicts cleared and will be re-judged tonight on new metrics, so expect the UNPROVEN count to change and expect T008 and T011 to carry the new flat-stock sentence. If any technique on a STOCK metric reads WORKS, check that _judge_stock found a real pre-activation baseline of 7+ days and did not just watch a counter tick up. (3) MATURE ACCEPTANCE AT 0.0% IS THE NUMBER THAT SHOULD DRIVE THE NEXT SUBSTANTIVE CHANGE. A second consecutive 0.0% makes it a trend rather than a small-sample artifact of 37 URLs, and at that point stop improving pages and act on T027 or T060: the evidence for 'Google is declining this corpus, not failing to find it' would then be about as strong as this loop can make it without server logs. (4) serving_ever went +1. Two consecutive zeros still means discovery has stopped, and that remains a bigger emergency than the ranking loss. (5) THE THREE-WEEK CONTENT EXPERIMENT runs 08-23 to ~09-13 against the 08-23 baseline (serving_pages 37, serving_ever 267, unknown 74%, ever-fetched 18.4%, indexed 3, accept_mature 3.6%). Today, day four: 28 / 272 / 75.6% / 18.2% / 2 / 0.0%. Worse on every axis except serving_ever. Still too early to call, and say so rather than inventing a signal. (6) DO NOT REPEAT THE ANTHROPIC CAP STORY — four clean mornings. Read the actual error string before writing any version of it. (7) When changing any seeded technique's metric, prefixes, name, hypothesis, evidence or notes, edit growth/seed.py in the same commit and RE-READ THE LEDGER AFTER A BUILD, not after the write. seed.run() is authoritative and reverts silently. (8) Unchanged: the building-page link to /section8/ and /available/ is still deferred to ~09-01; subtract jayshomefinder from every click count; every serving_pages figure is a floor, since the same GSC window read hours later returns ~19% more; /root/dhcr-build is still not a git worktree.


## 2026-08-27 — The $9 report was never shown to a search visitor — it lived only in the map app, while 260 of the 273 URLs that have ever served are building pages. And T027's promotion criteria have no predictive power at all
*6am review*

**Observed.** GATING NUMBER: gsc_serving_pages 26, down from 28 yesterday, 31 on 08-25, 39 on 08-24 and 88 on 08-09 — 1 entered / 3 left, and 0.05% of ~47,600 published. gsc_serving_ever 273 (+1), so discovery is still a rounding error but has not stalled. index_accept_pct_mature 0.0% for the SECOND CONSECUTIVE DAY on 37 mature-fetched URLs — the series reads 50.0 (08-20), 35.0, 11.1, 3.6, 2.9, 2.8, 0.0, 0.0. By crawl age: 0-6d 1 of 6 indexed, 7-13d 0 of 16, 14-20d 1 of 23, 21-27d 0 of 26, 28d+ 0 of 11. States on the 450-URL cohort: indexed 2 (flat), crawled-not-indexed 80, discovered-not-indexed 44 (UP from 28), unknown-to-Google 324 (DOWN from 340) — 16 URLs moved forward out of unknown, the first real forward movement in that column. Traffic 08-26: 30 visitors, 9 organic, 3 AI — visitors and building-views are RISING (visitors 12/15/25/30 across 08-23..26; building-view visitors 7/7/15/16) while search collapses. Revenue $0: paying_subs 0, api_keys 3 (0 pro, 0 business). CRONS AND JOBS ALL CLEAN: 05:00 daily rc=0, 05:40 build rc=0, watchdog ran refresh_seo.sh to completion, seo pipeline in-sync at 47,954 pages, scout ok (1 technique added, T065), outreach ok. Five clean mornings — the Anthropic cap story stays dead. YESTERDAY'S CLAIMS CHECKED AND ALL LANDED: the new revenue keys appear in results.jsonl for 08-27 — reports_sold 0, reports_sold_30d 0, revenue_usd_30d 0.0, api_keys_pro 0, api_keys_business 0 — so _report_sales() did not throw and the building_reports read works. reports_sold 0 is a REAL zero, not a swallowed exception: 30 days of a live $9 product with no sales. T011 and T017 both re-judged on the new metric as predicted.

**Concluded.** FIRST, AND THE FINDING OF THE DAY: the $9 Building Report has never been offered to a single search visitor. The button exists only in index.html's map detail panel, gated IS_NYC && REPORTS_ON. The 47,165 static /building/ pages — which build_seo.py publishes and which the 08-24 tier census showed are the ONLY tier that earns impressions, 260 of the 273 ever-served URLs against 9 hubs and the home page — carried no mention of it at all. What those pages did offer was Plus at $4.99/mo, in a trailing sentence, and Plus has 0 conversions for its entire lifetime. So T017's hypothesis ('a one-time ask converts where a subscription never has') has not been tested and failed; it was never asked. The denominator that IS real and that I had not seen quantified before: 217 DISTINCT VISITORS viewed a building record in the last 30 days (1,998 views) and bought 0 reports. 0/217 rules out a strong conversion rate — anything above ~1.4% would have shown by now at 95% — but it cannot separate 'wrong offer' from 'a ~0.5% rate on a small sample'. SECOND: T027's promotion criteria are REFUTED, and 08-26 had named T027 as the move once mature acceptance hit 0.0% twice, which it now has. Before executing a plan that deletes most of the corpus I tested it against the site's own serving history. Scoring all 47,165 buildings 0-10 on exactly the signals T027 names (registration, open violations, complaints, completeness) and comparing the 260 that have ever earned an impression against the corpus: mean 8.24 vs 8.13, median 9 vs 8. Open violations — a criterion named explicitly — splits 0.85x / 1.09x / 1.01x / 1.06x across its four buckets. That is no discriminatory power at all. Every cut retains served pages at the rate it retains everything (cut>=7: 87.3% of corpus, 87.7% of served; cut>=8: 69.2% and 73.5%), and there is no cut producing the 3,000-5,000 the plan wants — the distribution is saturated at 97.1% scoring >=6 and falls off a cliff from 22,998 at >=9 to 5,320 at >=10, where only 13.5% of served pages survive. Executing T027 as written would have deleted about a third of the working tier for zero selectivity gain. What does correlate is demand-shaped, not quality-shaped: 100+ unit buildings lift 2.24x against 0.73x for 1-9 units, Manhattan 1.43x, pre-1920 1.43x, and QUEENS IS 18.7% OF THE CORPUS BUT 3.1% OF EVER-SERVED, with 41 of 47 census-sampled Queens pages unknown to Google. Google is showing the buildings people plausibly search for, not the ones we hold most data about. THIRD: review.py was still recording a halving as WORKS. T019 carried 'WORKS — 27 owned visitors in 30d (median 0.5/day but falling)' — the why-string described the decline while the verdict said True. The owned-prefix branch set works=True unconditionally, and its floor is 'total >= 20 OR recent >= 1' where total is CUMULATIVE, so a sum over a lengthening window cannot fall and a technique that drew traffic early and nothing since clears it forever. This is the SAME false-positive class fixed on the site-wide paths on 08-25 and the stock metrics on 08-26, surviving because this branch went through neither pass. It matters for the same reason: scout.py feeds the WORKS list to the model under ALREADY MEASURED AS WORKING, so a false positive breeds. FOURTH, A CORRECTION TO MY OWN PROCESS THIS MORNING: I wrote four revisit notes with ledger.note() and TWO OF THEM WERE SILENTLY REVERTED by the next build. seed.run() re-applies notes from seed.py for any slug that declares one — T010 and T019 do, T017 does not and T027 is not seeded. 08-26 documented this trap for `metric` and added set_metric with a warning; ledger.note() had the identical exposure and no warning at all. This is exactly how a past entry came to claim a fix its diff never made, and it only did not happen again because I re-read the ledger after a build. FIFTH, an external confound I will not overstate: multiple 2026 reports describe Google deindexing URLs at higher rates industry-wide since April, with Google saying it sees nothing unusual and Mueller raising a possible Search Console reporting bug. That means our collapse is not evidence this corpus is uniquely bad — but it does not explain 0.0% mature acceptance, which comes from URL Inspection and not from impressions. WHAT ISN'T WORKING, PLAINLY: search share 0.0% and never anything else; 0 of 315 tracked queries in the top 10; revenue $0 with the API fully built and the $9 report live 30 days with zero sales; mature acceptance 0.0% twice running; the scoreboard is now 1 WORKS / 0 DIDN'T / 13 UNPROVEN, and the single WORKS (T003) rests on 13 AI-referral visitors against 6. AdSense still has no slot id so no ads render, and api@findacrib.com may still not forward.

**Changed.** THREE changes, all verified after a build rather than after the write. (1) THE OFFER NOW REACHES THE ONLY TIER THAT EARNS IMPRESSIONS. build_seo.py renders the $9 Building Report on the static /building/ pages, after the conditions table — the reader has just seen the open-violation count and the report answers what that number means. GATED ON compare_html, deliberately: that flag means building_facts() found at least FACTS_MIN_SENTENCES true comparative things to say, which is exactly what section 1 of the report is built from, so where it is absent the report would be thin and we do not offer it — selling a $9 report on a building we can say nothing about is the one version of this that trades credibility for revenue. Copy is WORD-FOR-WORD the description already shipped beside the live button in index.html, so the two surfaces cannot drift into different claims about one product; it promises the pre-filled DHCR request, not the rent history, which is free and which the report itself says is free. Link is the same /#d=<bbl> deep link the page's other CTAs use, opening the panel where the real Stripe button lives — a static page cannot POST to /api/reports/checkout and inventing a GET checkout route would be a change to the payment path, not the page. VERIFIED BY RENDERING, not by reading: built a real 6-neighborhood, 1,375-building subset through build_seo.main(); 1,371 pages carry the offer exactly once, the 4 without it are exactly the 4 with no compare block, no page has the offer without the block, and the deep-link BBL matches each page's own canonical. (2) A DECLINING SERIES CAN NO LONGER READ AS WORKS. review.py's owned-prefix branch now requires the technique to be alive NOW and not shrinking: works=True only when the trailing median clears MIN_RECENT_MEDIAN and is not below the prior window; a falling series and a series carried by days outside the window each get works=None with a why-string that says which it is. Not False — these are all above the retirement floor and calling them failures would be its own false claim. Applied: T019 flips WORKS to UNPROVEN reading '27 owned visitors in 30d, but the trailing 14d median is 0.5/day against 1.0/day in the 14d before — the cumulative total clears the floor because it cannot fall, while the rate is declining'. T003 is untouched (site-wide branch, rising). Scoreboard goes 2 WORKS to 1. (3) THE NOTE TRAP NOW ANNOUNCES ITSELF. ledger.note() warns when its target declares notes= in seed.py and will therefore be reverted by the next build, with the seed lookup imported lazily (seed imports ledger, so a module-level import would be circular) and wrapped so that failing to answer the question can never stop a note being written. Verified: the warning fires for hub_direct_answers and is silent for the unseeded T027. The two reverted revisits were rewritten into growth/seed.py where they survive. REVISITS, all three due today, all decided and all pushed to 2026-09-26: T010 gsc_integration KEEP — its measurement half succeeded beyond its claim and is the reason every finding since late July exists, including today's; its 'page-2 queries are cheap wins' half is refuted by its own instrument, since tracked_ranking is 0 of 315 and there is no page-2 tier; its organic_visitors verdict is disowned as a category error and deliberately NOT repointed, because every candidate metric is one an instrument also cannot cause. T017 KEEP, with the finding above and an instruction not to retire it before the new surface has had 21 days. T019 KEEP the block, reject the verdict; the hypothesis is neither confirmed (hubs hold 9 of 273 ever-served against buildings' 260) nor refuted (a page Google declines cannot demonstrate depth at 0.0% acceptance). T027 is NOT due but carries a note that its criteria are refuted and must be rewritten around size/borough/age before anyone acts on it. Added no keywords (315 tracked, 0 ranking; the universe is not the constraint). Did NOT run keywords.check_coverage(). Verified: py_compile clean on growth/*.py, growth_daily.py and build_seo.py; build --dry-run --docroot . --build-dir . exits 0; status exits 0; report renders both parts, table-layout:fixed intact x9, no javascript: hrefs.

**Watching.** (1) THE FIRST THING TO CHECK IS WHETHER THE REPORT OFFER ACTUALLY SHIPPED TO PRODUCTION. It is in build_seo.py, which the 05:40 watchdog hands to refresh_seo.sh, so tomorrow's seo_pipeline should show a LARGE changed_urls count — on the order of 46,000-47,000, since almost every building page gains a block. If changed_urls is 0 or a few hundred, the corpus did not take the code and that is the 08-22 frozen-corpus failure returning, not a rendering problem. Check that before concluding anything about conversion. (2) reports_sold IS NOW THE NUMBER THAT DECIDES T017, and for the first time it is being asked of the right audience. It reads 0 today across 217 building-record viewers in 30d. Do not read a single sale as proof or a single week of zero as refutation; the honest test is 21 days from today, 2026-09-17, against that 217/30d denominator. If it is still 0 then, the offer is wrong and not merely unseen. (3) MATURE ACCEPTANCE HAS NOW BEEN 0.0% TWICE. 08-26 said that makes it a trend and that the next substantive change should act on T027 or T060 — today's work says T027's criteria cannot carry it, so the remaining candidate is T060 (subtractive: remove crawl waste from the measured-dead sections) or a rewritten T027 keyed to size/borough/age. Whoever acts must not use the data-richness score; it has been measured and it is noise. (4) THE QUEENS DEFICIT IS THE LARGEST UNEXPLAINED NUMBER ON THE SITE: 8,839 pages, 18.7% of the corpus, 3.1% of ever-served, 41 of 47 census-sampled pages unknown to Google, and 7,965 of those buildings carry Queens' hyphenated house numbers ('47-02 11TH ST') which address_variants() has no rule for. I did NOT act on it today — the census says under-crawled, which would make an address-alias fix premature — but it is the best-defined open question in the ledger and it belongs to T050. (5) UNKNOWN-TO-GOOGLE FELL 340 to 324 with discovered-not-indexed UP 28 to 44. That is 16 URLs moving forward for the first time. Watch whether it continues or was one crawl burst. (6) VISITORS AND BUILDING-VIEWS ARE RISING WHILE SEARCH FALLS (visitors 12/15/25/30, building-view visitors 7/7/15/16 across 08-23..26). Something non-search is bringing people in and no technique currently claims it. Find out what before attributing it to one. (7) WHEN WRITING ANY REVISIT NOTE, watch for the new WARNING line from ledger.note(); if it fires, the text belongs in growth/seed.py or it will be gone by morning. Re-read the ledger AFTER a build. (8) Unchanged and still true: subtract jayshomefinder from every click count (3 of 4 clicks today); every serving_pages figure is a floor, since the same GSC window read hours later returns ~19% more; /root/dhcr-build is still not a git worktree.


## 2026-08-28 — The index triage that deployed this morning would have noindexed every page Google is currently showing — 260 of 260 ever-served building pages, 14 of 14 serving now. Rescued them by making an impression a promotion rule
*6am review*

**Observed.** GATING NUMBER: gsc_serving_pages 15, down from 26 yesterday, and the series now reads 88 (08-09) / 63 / 47 / 46 / 37 / 39 / 31 / 28 / 26 / 15. serving_stable is also 15, so all 15 held and 0 entered while 11 dropped out — the first day of the collapse with no replacement at all. gsc_serving_ever 273, UNCHANGED for the first time on record: discovery did not merely slow, it stopped. Impressions 13, clicks 5, and ALL FIVE clicks are jayshomefinder on the home page ('jays home finder' 2, 'jayshomefinder' 1, 'jayshomefinder.com' 2) — the decommissioned brand, so real clicks for this site today are ZERO. search_share_pct 0.0 with 0 of 315 tracked queries in the top 10. THE INDEX CENSUS DENOMINATOR CHANGED UNDERNEATH THE SERIES: published_urls fell 47,982 to 3,530 between yesterday's read and today's 05:11 read, because the index triage (commit 7e03c3d, 'Stop submitting 47,000 pages Google has already declined') was live in the docroot before this morning's measurement. So the cohort rotated hard — 247 added, 242 dropped, 455 sampled — and today's state counts (unknown-to-Google 239, crawled-not-indexed 53, discovered-not-indexed 15) are NOT comparable with yesterday's 324/80/44. They are a smaller, different universe, not movement. What IS comparable: index_accept_pct_mature 0.0% for the THIRD consecutive day (50.0 / 35.0 / 11.1 / 3.6 / 2.9 / 2.8 / 0.0 / 0.0 / 0.0), fetched_pct 17.5, indexed 1 of 455. TRAFFIC IS RISING SHARPLY WHILE SEARCH DIES: visitors 86 on 08-27 against 30 / 25 / 15 / 12 across 08-26..23, organic_visitors 26 against 9 / 7 / 1 / 0, ai_visitors 1. Revenue $0: paying_subs 0, reports_sold 0, reports_sold_30d 0, revenue_usd_30d 0.0, api_keys 3 (0 pro, 0 business).
