SCOUT FAILED: ok false, 'no JSON object in response (stop_reason=max_tokens): I will research current growth techniques before proposing.' That is NOT the billing cap — the cap story has been dead since 08-24 — it is the 16,000-token ceiling being consumed entirely by thinking and eight server-side web searches before the model wrote a single character of JSON, the same failure shape that killed 4k and 8k. CHECKED YESTERDAY'S CLAIMS: the $9 report offer IS in build_seo.py at line 2110 gated on compare_html, ledger.note()'s seed warning is in growth/ledger.py, and review.py's owned-prefix branch does require a non-falling trailing median — all three shipped as described. Also checked and NOT a regression: owned_visitors is missing from today's per-technique rows because it is written one day in arrears (08-27's rows were written 08-28T05:00:08), not because measurement broke.

**Concluded.** THE FINDING OF THE DAY, and it is a near-miss that cost nothing only because it was caught on day one. The index triage narrows the sitemap to building pages that are advertised now, carry 100+ open class-C violations, or have 300+ apartments — about 844 of 47,165 — and noindex,follows the rest. Its reasoning is sound and its diagnosis (the crawl allowance was being spent on a 53-71% identical tier while the hand-written pages had never been fetched) is the right one. But nobody scored the rule against the site's own serving history before shipping it, and growth/gsc_pages.json has held that history since 07-30. I scored it: of the 260 building pages that have EVER earned a Search Console impression, 11 survive the three rules. Of the 14 building pages serving in the current 08-19..26 window, ZERO survive. The triage was about to un-publish the entire demonstrated working tier — 260 of the 273 ever-served URLs are building pages — to concentrate crawl budget on pages that have demonstrated nothing. Be fair to the rules: 4.2% of ever-served pages clear them against 1.2% of the corpus, a real 3.5x enrichment, and the 300-unit rule is defensible because 08-27 measured 100+ unit buildings at 2.24x lift. But the class-C rule is a data-richness criterion, and 08-27 measured that exact class of criterion — open violations, split 0.85x/1.09x/1.01x/1.06x across its buckets — to have NO power to predict what Google shows. The triage inherited the refuted signal. SECOND, and this is why the near-miss matters more than the numbers: an impression is not a proxy for Google's judgement, it IS Google's judgement. Google looked at the page, matched it to a real query, and put it in front of a person. Throwing those pages out of the sitemap is spending the only evidence the site has. THIRD, A CORRECTION TO YESTERDAY'S WATCH LIST. 08-27 said to check whether the report offer shipped by looking for a large changed_urls in seo_pipeline, 'on the order of 46,000-47,000', and to read 0 as the 08-22 frozen-corpus failure returning. It read 0 today and the corpus is NOT frozen. changed_urls is not the count of pages that changed — build_seo.py writes changed_urls.txt as the intersection of changed pages with SUBMITTED pages, deliberately, so IndexNow is not handed 46,000 freshly-noindexed URLs. After the triage that intersection is nearly empty by construction. The corpus is demonstrably live: 49,371 pages today against 47,954 yesterday, +1,417 from the new landlord tier. Do not use that field as a deploy check again. FOURTH: the traffic and the search data now contradict each other and I will not paper over it. 26 organic visitors on 08-27 by referrer classification, against Search Console reporting 5 clicks in an EIGHT-DAY window, all five of them jayshomefinder. Those cannot both be describing Google. growth/metrics.py:91-101 classifies a visit as organic when the referrer host matches SEARCH_HOSTS, which includes engines other than Google, so the most likely reading is that Bing or another engine — the ones IndexNow actually feeds — is delivering traffic Google is not. I am NOT asserting that; the referrer host is not currently recorded, only the class. WHAT ISN'T WORKING, PLAINLY: search share 0.0% and never anything else; 0 of 315 tracked queries in the top 10; zero real clicks today; mature acceptance 0.0% three days running; ever-served flat for the first time; revenue $0 with the API fully built and the $9 report live 32 days with 0 sales. AdSense still has no slot id so no ads render, and api@findacrib.com may still not forward.

**Changed.** THREE changes. (1) THE TRIAGE NO LONGER DELETES THE WINNERS. build_seo.py gains a fourth promotion rule: a building page that has ever earned a Search Console impression keeps its place in the sitemap. Source is growth/gsc_pages.json['history'], which is tracked, written nightly by the measure job, and capped at 4,000 URLs by HISTORY_CAP — which is also a sane sitemap ceiling for a domain being rationed this hard. It is not a widening of the kind the triage comment warns against: it relaxes no threshold and adds no guess, it adds ~249 already-proven URLs to an ~844-URL sitemap, and the set only grows when Google itself serves a new page. Missing or malformed file falls back to the three measured rules and never fails the build. VERIFIED BY BUILDING, not by reading: ran build_seo.main() over a 720-building subset chosen to contain 120 ever-served pages and 600 ordinary ones — 0 of the 120 ever-served pages carry noindex, all 120 appear in a sitemap, and 570 of the 600 ordinary pages are still correctly noindexed. Against the full corpus the rule takes promoted building pages from 569 to 818 locally (the droplet also has listings.json, so ~844 to ~1,093 there) and rescues 260 of 260 ever-served and 14 of 14 currently serving. The build log now prints how many ever-served pages the rule is holding, so if that number ever collapses to zero the rule can be retired rather than carried forever. (2) SCOUT GETS HEADROOM. growth/scout.py max_tokens 16,000 to 32,000, with today's actual error text recorded in the comment. There is nothing to salvage from a reply that never reached a '{', so _salvage() cannot help and the only lever is the ceiling. This is a cap and not a spend: runs that already finish inside 16k cost exactly what they did before, and today's failed run had already paid for 16,000 tokens and returned no techniques and no keywords. (3) REVISIT DUE TODAY — city_guides (T023): CHANGE, reactivated with the clock restarted, next revisit 2026-09-27. It was retired 08-19 on '0 owned visitors in 21d, 0 search impressions across 0 URLs — invisible in search, not merely un-clicked'. The 08-27 URL Inspection census refutes the premise: of the guides sampled Google had fetched NOT ONE, so that zero measured a page Google never looked at, not a page it looked at and declined. A never-crawled page cannot fail a content test, and this is the same false-verdict family as the false positives fixed on 08-25, 08-26 and 08-27, surviving on the retirement side. What makes today the day to retry rather than merely to disown the verdict is that the triage just cut the submitted corpus 13x, which is precisely the intervention meant to free crawl budget for hand-written pages like these. It also needs to be alive to do the job its notes describe — verifying every run that the LA 'likely RSO' and SF block-level caveats are still on the page — a credibility guard that has gone unchecked since 08-19 while the guides were being actively edited (commit e989406, 08-27). ledger.reactivate() cleared the stale verdict and reset activated to today, so it gets a full 21 days. ADDED NO KEYWORDS: 315 tracked, 0 ranking, and the universe is not the constraint. Did NOT run keywords.check_coverage(). VERIFIED: py_compile clean on growth/*.py, growth_daily.py, build_seo.py; growth_daily.py build --dry-run --docroot . --build-dir . exits 0 and city_guides now runs and reports; report renders both parts, table-layout:fixed intact x9, no javascript: hrefs.

**Watching.** (1) THE FIRST CHECK IS THE SITEMAP COUNT, NOT changed_urls. Tomorrow's build log should print two 'index triage' lines; the second must read roughly '260 of 260 building pages that have ever served are promoted'. If it reads 0 of 260, growth/gsc_pages.json was not readable from the build's working directory and the rescue is not live. Do not use seo_pipeline.changed_urls as a deploy check — see today's correction. (2) DOES RESCUING THE WINNERS STOP THE BLEED? gsc_serving_pages is 15 with serving_stable 15 and 0 entering. The 14 building pages in that set are now back in the sitemap. If serving_pages keeps falling anyway, the decline was never about submission and the triage's whole premise needs re-examining; if it stabilises or ticks up within a week, keep the rule. Either way gsc_serving_ever, flat at 273 today for the first time, is the cleaner signal — it cannot fall. (3) THE ORGANIC/GSC CONTRADICTION IS THE BEST-DEFINED OPEN QUESTION ON THE SITE and I deliberately did not guess at it. 26 organic visitors in a day against 5 Google clicks in eight days, all five of them a decommissioned brand. The fix is one measurement: growth/metrics.py:152-159 builds the channel dict by class only, so record the referrer HOST alongside it and split organic into per-engine counts. I did not ship it because metrics.py needs Supabase and cannot be exercised here, and because a metric key added without deciding how review.py and report.py consume it is exactly the half-wired instrument that cost 08-26 and 08-27 a day each. Whoever ships it should wire all three at once. If Bing is the answer, bing_index_coverage_program is already sitting in the ledger as a candidate. (4) SCOUT: if it fails again at 32,000 tokens with the same preamble-then-nothing shape, the ceiling is not the problem and the next move is to cut web_search max_uses from 8, or to split research and proposal into two calls. Read the actual stop_reason before assuming. (5) MATURE ACCEPTANCE HAS NOW BEEN 0.0% THREE DAYS RUNNING on a cohort that has just been re-drawn against the 3,530 submitted URLs rather than 47,982. That re-draw makes the next few readings the first honest measurement of whether concentration works — the census is finally sampling the pages we actually want indexed. Give it a week before reading a trend, and do NOT compare the new state counts to anything before 08-28. (6) reports_sold is still 0, and the honest 21-day test of the offer on static building pages is 2026-09-17 against the 217-viewers-in-30d denominator. (7) Unchanged and still true: subtract jayshomefinder from every click count (today it is all five); every serving_pages figure is a floor, since the same GSC window read hours later returns ~19% more.


## 2026-08-29 — The retirement rule has been judging pages Google never fetched — three times
*6am review*

**Observed.** CRONS ALL CLEAN: 05:00 daily rc=0, 05:40 build rc=0, watchdog ran refresh_seo.sh to completion, seo_pipeline in-sync at 49,371 corpus pages, outreach ok (0 drafted, lending segment). SCOUT RECOVERED: ok true, 3 techniques and 25 keywords proposed. Yesterday raised scout.py max_tokens 16,000 to 32,000 after a run that spent the whole ceiling on thinking and web searches before writing a character of JSON; at 32,000 it finished. Tracked queries 315 to 340, still 0 ranking. THE SEARCH FOOTPRINT IS COLLAPSING AND IT DID NOT START WITH THE TRIAGE: gsc_serving_pages 11 today against 15 / 26 / 28 / 31 / 39 / 37 / 46 / 47 / 63 / 58 / 60 / 67 / 69 / 63 / 68 across 08-28 back to 08-14 — down 84% in a fortnight, and the fall begins 08-14, a full 13 days before the index triage went live. gsc_page_impressions 37 today against 122 on 08-15. The mechanism is in the churn, not the level: pages ENTERING the serving set have dried up (1 today, 0-2/day since 08-21, against 10-14/day in mid-August) while 3-11 leave every day. Average position 7.5 today against 82.0 on 08-17..19, which is the signature of losing a deep tail of page-8 impressions and keeping a handful of high-position branded results. gsc_serving_ever 274, +1, and it cannot fall. CENSUS: cohort 455, 408 read, 1 indexed, fetched_pct 20.1 (up from 17.5), accept_pct_mature 0.0% for the FOURTH consecutive day, states unknown-to-Google 310 / crawled-not-indexed 81 / discovered-not-indexed 16. TRAFFIC UP, SEARCH DOWN, same contradiction as yesterday: visitors 107 on 08-28 against 86 / 30 / 25 / 15, organic_visitors 22, ai_visitors 1, direct 84. REVENUE $0: paying_subs 0, reports_sold 0, reports_sold_30d 0, api_keys 3 (0 pro, 0 business). CHECKED YESTERDAY'S CLAIMS: the ever-served rescue in build_seo.py IS live and resolving — build_seo.EVER_SERVED_BBLS parses 261 BBLs out of the 274-URL history, all 261 match rows in buildings.min.json, and promoted_building() now promotes 819 of 47,165 buildings against 569 before the rule. I could not read the droplet's build log from here, so I exercised the code against the real corpus instead, which is the stronger check. changed_urls read 0 again on a 49,371-page corpus, exactly as yesterday's correction predicted; it is still not a deploy check.

**Concluded.** THE FINDING OF THE DAY IS ABOUT THE INSTRUMENT, AND IT HAS BEEN WRONG THREE TIMES. review.py retires a technique when its pages earn no owned visitors and no search impressions, with the verdict 'invisible in search, not merely un-clicked'. That sentence is a claim about Google's JUDGEMENT of the pages — it looked, and it is not showing them — and it has been issued three times against pages Google has never once fetched. The URL Inspection census, which is tracked in git and which nobody had pointed at this decision, says: T002 daily_brief 10 of 10 sampled URLs unknown to Google, 0 crawled; T013 city_seo_expansion 57 of 57, 0 crawled; T023 city_guides 3 of 3, 0 crawled. A page that was never fetched cannot fail a content test. Those three zeros measured the crawler's reach and were recorded as verdicts on the pages. This is the same false-verdict family as the fixes of 08-25 (unconditional works=True), 08-26 (stocks run through a flow test) and 08-27 — a branch asserting more than its evidence carries — surviving on the retirement side, where it is most expensive, because retiring stops the technique rebuilding its own pages. SECOND: THE SERVING COLLAPSE IS NOT THE TRIAGE'S FAULT AND I WANT THAT ON THE RECORD BEFORE ANYONE BLAMES IT. serving_pages was already 68 to 26 before the triage deployed. What the churn actually shows is that Google stopped ADDING pages around 08-20 while continuing to drop them, so the set drains. The triage and yesterday's ever-served rescue are aimed at exactly this and are one and two days old respectively; they have not had a chance to be right or wrong yet. THIRD, THE STRUCTURAL PICTURE THE CENSUS NOW MAKES PLAIN: fetches are almost entirely confined to one tier. Of 408 URLs read, Googlebot has fetched 55 building pages, 14 /neighborhood/, 5 /borough/, 4 /zip/, the root, /developers/ and one /sf/ page. Zero for /guide/, /brief/, /section8/, /available/, /council-district/, /dc/, /la/ and every SF/LA/DC hub. Every hand-written page on this site is invisible because it has never been looked at, not because it was judged and found wanting. Current large-site guidance is consistent with this and names the lever: pages four or more clicks from the entry point get roughly 70% less crawl frequency, and hubs behind an uncrawled hub are effectively orphaned. WHAT ISN'T WORKING, PLAINLY: search share 0.0% and never anything else; 0 of 340 tracked queries in the top 10; mature acceptance 0.0% four days running; the serving set draining at 3-11 pages a day with 0-2 entering; revenue $0 with the API fully built and the $9 report live 33 days at 0 sales. AdSense still has no slot id so no ads render, and api@findacrib.com may still not forward.

**Changed.** TWO CHANGES, ONE OF THEM THE REVISIT. (1) THE CENSUS GUARD. New growth/indexstatus.crawl_evidence(prefixes) answers 'has Google ever fetched anything under these URLs', reading the tracked cohort in index_status.json, counting only URLs the sampler has actually inspected, keying 'fetched' off Google's own lastCrawlTime and re-deriving buckets from the stored raw state so a later bucket() improvement applies retroactively — the same three conventions summarise() already uses. review.py's retire branch now consults it: where the census has inspected at least MIN_CENSUS_READ (3) of a technique's URLs and fetched NONE of them, it holds instead of retiring and says the zero measures crawl reach rather than the pages. It is deliberately narrow and needs POSITIVE evidence of no crawl — a technique whose URLs the census has never sampled falls through and retires exactly as before, because an absent measurement is not a finding and laundering one through this guard would be the same error in the other direction. The known hole, left open on purpose: a technique whose URLs are in no sitemap is in no cohort, and being unsubmitted is its own finding. VERIFIED BY RUNNING IT, not by reading: against real data the guard fires for T002 (10 read, 0 fetched), T013 (57, 0), T023 (3, 0), T001 fresh_section8 (6, 0) and T012 listings_freshness (15, 0), and stays silent for the two tiers Google demonstrably crawls — T019 hub_direct_answers (90 read, 23 fetched) and T046 derived_building_facts (206, 55) — where a retirement verdict would be legitimate. Then reproduced the exact conditions under which T013 was retired on 08-20 with the visitor and impression series stubbed to zero: the never-crawled prefixes now return keep/works=None, /building/ still returns retire/works=False, and an unsampled prefix still returns retire. All 15 live techniques re-evaluated with no verdict changed today — the guard is preventive, and no technique is currently on the retire path. (2) REVISIT DUE TODAY — city_seo_expansion (T013): CHANGE, reactivated with the clock restarted, next revisit 2026-09-28. The 08-20 verdict is withdrawn on the census evidence above. But the harder revisit question is whether the idea still earns its place, and the site's own numbers say it does: NYC's equivalent aggregate tier — 198 neighborhood + 165 zip + 5 borough = 368 pages — has 9 URLs that have ever earned an impression, 2.4%, against 261 of 47,165 building pages, 0.55%. That is a 4.4x higher serving rate per page, and it is NOT a crawl artefact, because the census fetches the two tiers at almost identical rates (NYC hubs 23 of 90, buildings 55 of 206). So aggregates really do out-serve address pages per page; this particular tier is simply the one Google has never reached. The reachability defect is specific and worth naming for whoever fixes it: /<city>/buildings/ is the sole inbound path to these 255 hubs and is itself uncrawled (3 sampled, 0 fetched), so the tier sits behind a door Googlebot has not opened. Reactivation also restores maintenance — today's crawl_paths audit lists /dc/neighborhood/, /dc/buildings/ and /la/buildings/ among 9 published sections under no active technique, meaning 255 live pages including the LA 'likely RSO' and SF block-level caveats had nothing rebuilding or verifying them. I will not oversell it: reactivating a technique does not get its pages crawled, and this is a maintenance and attribution fix, not a growth lever. The reasoning went into growth/seed.py's notes for T013, not ledger.note(), because seed.run() re-applies notes on every build and would have silently reverted it — the trap documented 08-27. ADDED NO KEYWORDS: 340 tracked, 0 ranking, and the universe is not the constraint. Did NOT run keywords.check_coverage(). VERIFIED: py_compile clean on growth/*.py, growth_daily.py and build_seo.py; growth_daily.py build --dry-run --docroot . --build-dir . exits 0 with city_seo_expansion now running and correctly reporting it would publish 255 pages; seed.run() re-applied cleanly and left status, dates and the restarted clock alone; report renders both parts, table-layout:fixed intact x9, no javascript: hrefs.

**Watching.** (1) THE GUARD IS PREVENTIVE, SO THE CHECK IS THAT IT NEVER FIRES WRONGLY. If a future review sees a technique held with 'Google has never fetched any of the N URLs the index census has inspected', confirm N against growth/index_status.json before trusting it — the guard is only as good as the cohort, and a technique whose URLs stopped being submitted will drift out of the cohort and lose its protection silently. If a held technique's URLs do start getting fetched, it becomes judgeable again automatically and should then be allowed to retire on its merits. (2) DOES THE SERVING SET STOP DRAINING? 11 today, entered 1, left 5, and entering has been 0-2/day for nine days. If gsc_serving_pages reaches single digits or zero while gsc_serving_ever keeps creeping up, the site's Google footprint is being withdrawn wholesale and the triage plus the ever-served rescue will have been the right diagnosis applied too late rather than the wrong one. gsc_serving_ever is still the cleaner signal because it cannot fall. (3) THE EVER-SERVED RESCUE'S FIRST REAL TEST. It went live in the droplet's 05:40 build TODAY, after this morning's 05:11 census read, so nothing measured today reflects it. Tomorrow's published_urls should rise from 3,530 by roughly the 250 rescued building pages; if it is still 3,530, growth/gsc_pages.json was not readable from the build's working directory and the rescue is not actually live on the droplet even though it resolves correctly here. (4) THE HIGHEST-VALUE UNSHIPPED LEVER IS CLICK DEPTH, and today's census is the first clean evidence for it: every uncrawled tier on this site sits behind another uncrawled page. T037 crawl_paths audits whether a section has AN inbound link, which it passes 15 of 15 today, but not how deep that link is — and depth is what the crawl data actually predicts. Upgrading it from a binary to a measured click-depth from the homepage is the change I would ship next; I did not ship it today because it needs a built docroot to exercise and a bare checkout reads 4 pages, which is exactly the half-wired instrument that cost 08-26 and 08-27 a day each. (5) THE ORGANIC/GSC CONTRADICTION IS STILL OPEN and still the best-defined question on the site: 22 organic visitors on 08-28 by referrer classification against 11 Search Console page-dimension clicks in an eight-day window. growth/metrics.py:91-101 classifies on SEARCH_HOSTS, which includes engines other than Google; recording the referrer HOST and splitting organic per engine would settle it, and all three of metrics.py, review.py and report.py must be wired in the same change. (6) SCOUT AT 32,000 TOKENS WORKED ONCE. Two clean runs makes it a fix; one makes it a coincidence. If it fails again, read the actual stop_reason before assuming the ceiling — the next lever is cutting web_search max_uses from 8, or splitting research and proposal into two calls. (7) Unchanged and still true: subtract jayshomefinder from every click count, and every serving_pages figure is a floor because the same GSC window read hours later returns ~19% more.


## 2026-08-30 — The click count tripled overnight and none of it was housing: 33 of 33 clicks were people typing the site's own name
*6am review*

**Observed.** CRONS CLEAN: daily 05:00->05:20 rc=0, build --deploy 05:40 rc=0, seo_pipeline ran 2026-08-29T19:03Z (49,371 corpus pages, 61 changed, code synced-1). ANTHROPIC API IS NOT CAPPED — scout ok=true (3 techniques, 25 keywords), outreach ok=true (0 drafted, govtech). THE HEADLINE, WHICH IS A TRAP: gsc_clicks 33 against 5 yesterday, gsc_impressions 47 against 15, gsc_page_clicks 42 against 11, gsc_page_impressions 81 against 37, gsc_position 3.3 against 7.5 — a 6.6x jump in clicks overnight. The committed per-query snapshot says where every one of them came from: 42 of the 42 page-clicks landed on the homepage, and its queries are 'findacrib' 28 clicks / 30 impressions at position 1.0, 'jays home finder' 2/7, 'jayshomefinder' 1/7, 'jayshomefinder.com' 2/2, 'finder nyc' 0/1. NON-BRANDED CLICKS IN THE EIGHT-DAY WINDOW: ZERO. Non-branded impressions: ONE. All 12 building pages that served earned 0 clicks and 19 impressions between them, and queries_by_page is empty for every one — their queries are below Search Console's anonymization floor. SERVING: gsc_serving_pages 13 (11 yesterday), entered 3 and left 1, the first day since 08-24 that entering beat leaving; gsc_serving_ever 277 (+3); serving_stable 10. CENSUS: cohort 455 fully read for the first time (455 of 455, against 408 and 308 the two mornings before), fetched_pct 20.4 (20.1, 17.5), indexed 1, accept_pct_mature 0.0% for the FIFTH consecutive day, unknown-to-Google 346, crawled-not-indexed 92, added 11 / dropped 11, gained 0 / lost 0. The by_crawl_age breakdown says 0-6d 0 of 8, 7-13d 1 of 19, 14-20d 0 of 12, 21-27d 0 of 16, 28d+ 0 of 38 — the single indexed URL is in the 7-13d bucket and every mature bucket is a clean zero. TRAFFIC: 47 visitors on 08-29 (107, 86 the two days before), 14 organic, 2 AI, 32 direct. REVENUE STILL ZERO: paying_subs 0, mrr_usd 0, reports_sold 0, reports_sold_30d 0, api_keys 3. INDEX SITEMAP SIZE: published_urls 3,374, DOWN 156 from 3,530.

**Concluded.** TODAY'S FINDING IS THAT THE INSTRUMENT WOULD HAVE CALLED THIS A BREAKTHROUGH. A 6.6x overnight jump in clicks, at an average position that improved from 7.5 to 3.3, is exactly the shape of a win — and it is entirely a decommissioned brand plus the site's own name. 'Subtract jayshomefinder from every click count' has been carried as PROSE in the review prompt and in journal entry after journal entry for over a month; no series has ever said by how much, so every headline click figure on record is wrong by an unstated amount. That is the same false-signal family as 08-25 (unconditional works=True), 08-26 (stocks run through a flow test), 08-27 and 08-29 (retiring pages Google never fetched): a number asserting more than its evidence carries. It has now been made measurable rather than remembered. THE HONEST READING OF TODAY: zero housing-intent clicks and one housing-intent impression in eight days; search_share_pct 0.0 with 0 of 340 tracked queries in the top 10; mature index acceptance 0.0% for a fifth day, and the new by-crawl-age split kills the 'too early' defence — every cohort older than 13 days sits at exactly 0.0%, including 38 URLs past 28 days. YESTERDAY'S PREDICTION WAS WRONG AND I CAN SAY WHY. It said published_urls should rise from 3,530 by roughly the 250 rescued building pages, and that 3,530 unchanged would mean gsc_pages.json was unreadable on the droplet and the ever-served rescue was not live. It fell to 3,374 instead, and neither branch of that prediction was the explanation. Reconciled against the code: promoted_building() now promotes 662 of 47,165 buildings here — 409 by the 300-unit rule, 264 ever-served, 11 both — against the 819 reported yesterday, and commit 7885faa (08-29 14:46 EDT, after yesterday's review) dropped the class-C violations rule, which build_seo.py's own comment records as contributing 160 URLs. 819 minus 160 is 659, against 662 measured, and 3,530 minus 156 is 3,374. The rescue is live and resolving (EVER_SERVED_BBLS parses 264 BBLs, all promoted); the prediction was untestable as written because refresh_seo.sh had already run at 08-29 05:42, so the 3,530 baseline it compared against ALREADY contained the rescue. Lesson for the next prediction: name the build that produced the number you are comparing to, not the date. VERIFIED YESTERDAY'S DIFF: indexstatus.crawl_evidence() is at growth/indexstatus.py:603 and review.py:310 consults it — the census guard shipped as claimed. ONE GENUINE SMALL POSITIVE, NOT OVERSOLD: pages entering the serving set beat pages leaving for the first time in a fortnight (3 in, 1 out) and fetched_pct has climbed 17.5 to 20.4 over three days. Two days is not a trend and the triage and rescue are two and three days old. STILL BROKEN AND NOT MINE TO FIX: AdSense has no slot id so no ads render at all, and api@findacrib.com may still not be a live forwarding alias.

**Changed.** SHIPPED THE BRANDED / NON-BRANDED SPLIT, end to end. (1) growth/searchconsole.py: BRAND_TOKENS ('findacrib', 'jayshomefinder'), is_branded() — a substring test on the query with every non-alphanumeric character stripped, so 'find a crib', 'find-a-crib' and 'findacrib.com' all collapse to one token — plus branded_split(rows) and saved_branded_split(), which recomputes the same split from the committed gsc_pages.json snapshot so the report and this review can read it without an API key. collect() now records gsc_branded_clicks, gsc_branded_impressions, gsc_nonbranded_clicks and gsc_nonbranded_impressions from the live query rows, and carries the non-branded pair into last_run and its return value. gsc_clicks and gsc_impressions are deliberately NOT redefined, for the same reason the page dimension did not redefine them on 08-28: that series runs back to the start and would stop being comparable to itself. Two metrics, two meanings. The rule is blunt in the direction that UNDERSTATES the non-branded number — 'find a crib brooklyn' counts as branded because the site's name is made of common words — and that bias is documented in the code, because it is the safe direction for a metric whose entire purpose is to stop this loop over-claiming. Both halves are query-dimension numbers and inherit that dimension's anonymization limit; the docstring says so. (2) growth/report.py: a note block under the search stats naming the branded clicks and then the non-branded pair, preferring the recorded series and falling back to saved_branded_split() so it still renders on a morning the collector did not run. No markup hand-written, no second text builder — rendered both parts and diffed the text against the pre-change baseline: the ONLY difference is the new sentence, and table-layout:fixed is intact at 8 (it was 8 before my change too — yesterday's 'x9' was a different day's content, not a regression), zero javascript: hrefs. Also removed two redundant 'from . import searchconsole' statements inside build_blocks() that shadowed the module-level import and made the new call a use-before-assignment; the module-level import at report.py:18 already covered them. (3) SEEDED TODAY'S ROW from the committed snapshot rather than waiting for tomorrow: gsc_branded_clicks 33, gsc_branded_impressions 46, gsc_nonbranded_clicks 0, gsc_nonbranded_impressions 1, with meta naming the source file and window and flagging it as review-seeded. The snapshot accounts for 100% of the query dimension today (33 of 33 clicks, 46 of 47 impressions), so this is a measurement, not an estimate. If a future review sees gsc_clicks or gsc_page_clicks move and gsc_nonbranded_clicks flat, nothing has happened. (2) TOMORROW IS THE SPLIT'S FIRST LIVE TEST. But 346 of 455 are UNKNOWN TO GOOGLE: never fetched at all, despite a year of sitemaps, honest lastmods and nightly IndexNow. That half is discovery, not quality, and for a month it has been treated as a submission problem. Submission is not the constraint; every one of those URLs has been submitted. WHICH MAKES T037 THE INSTRUMENT AT FAULT. It has read 15 of 15 green every single day since 2026-08-23 while the footprint collapsed 63 -> 8 and three quarters of the sitemap stayed unfetched. A check that cannot go red through that is not measuring the discriminating variable. 'Does something link here' is satisfied by one link on one page; what Google's crawl scheduling actually reads as the site's own statement of importance is DISTANCE from the homepage, and this site has never measured it. The 08-29 census made the same point from the other end: every uncrawled tier sits behind another uncrawled page. STILL UNMOVED AND WORTH SAYING PLAINLY: 0 revenue on day 35 of the $9 Building Report, 0 API signups, api_keys flat at 3. STILL BROKEN AND NOT MINE TO FIX: AdSense has no slot id so no ads render, and api@findacrib.com may still not be a live forwarding alias.

**Changed.** UPGRADED T037 crawl_paths FROM A BINARY REACHABILITY CHECK TO A MEASURED CLICK DEPTH, in growth/techniques.py. New _crawl_depth(root, prefixes) does a real breadth-first crawl of the docroot starting at '/', following actual internal hrefs page to page, and returns the smallest depth at which any URL under each audited prefix is LINKED (homepage = depth 0, so a section linked from the homepage is depth 1). _crawl_depth_url() normalises what it follows: same-host absolute, protocol-relative and relative hrefs resolve (including '..' segments), fragments and query strings are stripped, and anything that is not a page — assets, JSON, the sitemaps, mailto:, tel:, javascript: — is dropped. _crawl_depth_note() renders one clause, deepest section first, and t_crawl_paths puts it at the FRONT of the tail on every return path, ahead of the merely-absent lists, because it is a finding about pages that exist rather than context. THREE GUARDS, EACH OF WHICH EXISTS BECAUSE THIS LOOP HAS ALREADY BEEN BURNED BY A HALF-WIRED INSTRUMENT: (1) complete_depth. A breadth-first crawl truncated at a page cap makes 'not found' ambiguous between 'nothing links to it' and 'the cap ran out first', so the function reports the deepest level it fully expanded and the note says 'NOT REACHED within N clicks' — never 'unreachable'. The binary audit still owns the unreachable verdict. (2) The CRAWL_DEPTH_FLOOR=40 guard, which suppresses the whole clause when something is unreached AND fewer than 40 pages were read: on a bare checkout the generated corpus is absent, so 'not reached' means 'not built', and a depth reading taken there would describe the checkout rather than the site. Confirmed: this checkout reads 1 page and reports '/developers/ 1' with no unreached list, and a synthetic docroot with an orphan correctly reports NOT MEASURED when starved and the real distribution when not. The floor deliberately does NOT apply when every prefix resolved — the crawl stops early once all are placed, so a well-linked site legitimately reads few pages and must not be called unmeasurable. (3) It is REPORT-ONLY. Pass/fail semantics of t_crawl_paths are untouched: orphans still fail, depth is only printed. There is no defensible threshold yet because nobody has ever measured this site's distribution, and picking one today would be a guess dressed as a rule — the exact failure family that cost 08-25 through 08-27 a day each. Cost is bounded at CRAWL_DEPTH_MAX=6 levels, CRAWL_DEPTH_PAGES=2000 pages, CRAWL_DEPTH_BYTES=300k per page, with an early exit at a level boundary once every prefix has a depth. It reads the docroot only, never growth_out, for _crawl_link_audit's stated reason: a link that exists solely in the staging dir is not a link Google can follow. TIMING IS DELIBERATE: T037's revisit falls on 2026-09-04, and that revisit has to ask whether this is still the best version of the idea. Four nights of real depth data is the difference between answering that with evidence and re-asking a binary that has passed 15 of 15 while the index collapsed. ADDED NO KEYWORDS: 365 tracked, 0 ranking, 83.0% covered — the query universe is not the constraint and adding to it would be motion. RETIRED NOTHING. NO REVISIT WAS DUE (next is crawl_paths on 2026-09-04). Did NOT run keywords.check_coverage(). VERIFIED: py_compile clean on growth/*.py, growth_daily.py and build_seo.py; `growth_daily.py build --dry-run --docroot . --build-dir .` exits 0 with the floor guard firing correctly and the worktree left clean; _crawl_depth_url unit-checked on 14 href shapes including the '..' and protocol-relative cases; _crawl_depth exercised against a synthetic 87-page docroot where it correctly returned depths 1,1,2,3,4,5 and named the one orphan; report renders both parts unchanged, table-layout:fixed intact at 8, zero javascript: hrefs.

Read it before anything else. The hypothesis it tests is that the sections Google has never fetched are the deep ones — if /building/, /zip/ and /neighborhood/ come back at depth 4-6 or NOT REACHED, click depth is the discovery constraint and the next change is flattening it, not publishing more. If they come back at depth 2-3, depth is NOT the answer and I was wrong; say so and look at the quality half instead, because 93 crawled-not-indexed is then the whole story. (2) SANITY-CHECK THE INSTRUMENT BEFORE TRUSTING IT, exactly as the census guard was checked on 08-30: the note names how many pages it read and how many levels it fully expanded. If it says 'levels 0-1 fully expanded' with 15 prefixes all at depth 1, the crawler is finding a link hub it should not be treating as the homepage. If it reports NOT REACHED for a section the binary audit simultaneously calls linked, that is not a contradiction — it means the one inbound link is deeper than the crawl got — but confirm the page count is in the hundreds before believing it. (3) gsc_serving_ever WENT +0 FOR THE FIRST TIME. It cannot fall, so it is the cleanest signal on the site. Three days at 0 now, against a branded series that went 5 -> 33 -> 49. It is the only click number here that bears on the 90% goal. ## 2026-09-01 — The click-depth reading that confirmed its own hypothesis on night one was an artifact of a 300KB read cap: all five app shells are ~342KB with their nav in the last 2KB
*6am review*

**Observed.** YESTERDAY'S HEADLINE FINDING DOES NOT SURVIVE INSPECTION AND I REPRODUCED THE FAILURE. The 09-01 t_crawl_paths detail read 'click depth from the homepage (2000 pages read, levels 0-5 fully expanded): /zip/ 6, /building/ 5, /dc/neighborhood/ 5, /la/zip/ 5, /sf/neighborhood/ 5, /available/ 4, /borough/ 4, /brief/ 4, +7 shallower'. Measured against this checkout: index.html is 342,512 bytes and its <nav class="seo-nav"> begins at byte 340,385 — the sole crawlable route to /buildings/, /borough/*, /section8/, /brief/ and /council-district/ sits 40KB past the CRAWL_DEPTH_BYTES=300,000 read cap. sf/index.html 341,457, la/ 341,532, dc/ 341,666, westchester/ 341,640 — all five app shells share the layout, nav in the last ~2KB. Direct reproduction with the real index.html and stub targets: at a 300k cap _crawl_depth returns only {/sf/ 1, /la/ 1, /dc/ 1} (those three are in the city switcher at byte ~32k) and misses /buildings/ /borough/ /section8/ /brief/ ENTIRELY; at a 4M cap all seven return depth 1. TODAY'S NUMBERS, ALL FLAT OR WORSE. gsc_clicks 53 (49, 33, 5 on the preceding days) and gsc_branded_clicks is 53 of 53 — gsc_nonbranded_clicks 0 for the FOURTH consecutive day, gsc_nonbranded_impressions 1, gsc_position 2.5. gsc_serving_pages 8, flat for the first time in eleven days (63 on 08-20), with gsc_serving_entered 0 and gsc_serving_left 0 — the serving set is not rotating, it is frozen: 7 building pages plus the homepage, 0 nyc hubs, 0 city hubs. gsc_serving_ever 277 for the third day running, so +0 twice in a row. search_share_pct 0.0, tracked_ranking 0 of 365, keyword coverage 83.0% (303/365). CENSUS: cohort 455, indexed 1 (0.2%), accept_pct 1.1, accept_pct_mature 0.0% for the EIGHTH consecutive day, fetched_pct 20.7, unknown_to_google 353 (346 yesterday), crawled_not_indexed 93, discovered_not_indexed 8; 28d+ bucket 0 of 50. published_urls 3,667, +52 on 3,615. TRAFFIC (08-31): 48 visitors, 12 organic, 1 AI. REVENUE ZERO ON DAY 36 OF THE $9 REPORT: paying_subs 0, mrr_usd 0, reports_sold 0, reports_sold_30d 0, revenue_usd_30d 0, api_keys 3 for the thirteenth day, accounts_with_saves 11. CRONS FIRED: daily 05:00-05:11 rc=0, build --deploy 05:40-05:42 rc=0, seo pipeline 49,371 pages 'ran clean (synced-1)', watchdog ok. ANTHROPIC: scout and outreach both 'Your credit balance is too low to access the Anthropic API' — an EMPTY BALANCE, not the console usage cap, second day.

**Concluded.** AN INSTRUMENT THAT AGREES WITH THE HYPOTHESIS IT WAS BUILT TO TEST, ON ITS FIRST NIGHT, DESERVED THE CHECK IT GOT. Yesterday's watch item (2) said to sanity-check the crawler before trusting it; the check found the reading false. The 300KB per-page cap was justified in the code as 'enough for the link block of any page we publish', and that sentence was never measured against a page. It is wrong for the only five pages that matter: this site's entire crawlable navigation lives in the last 2KB of ~342KB documents, after ~5,900 lines of inline app script, so the depth crawl saw none of it and reached the deep tiers by whatever long way round remained. /borough/ and /brief/ are linked directly from the homepage and are DEPTH 1, not 4. THE HONEST STATE OF THE DEPTH QUESTION IS THEREFORE 'STILL UNMEASURED', NOT 'CONFIRMED'. I do not know this site's click-depth distribution, and neither did yesterday's entry — it just did not know that it did not know. The failure mode is exactly the one this journal has named twice before and named again yesterday: a number asserting more than its evidence carries. Note also the specific danger of the shape it took — the false reading was not noise, it was BIASED TOWARD THE HYPOTHESIS, because truncating the navigation of the five hub pages can only ever inflate depth, never deflate it. An instrument whose error runs the same direction as your prior is the most expensive kind. WHAT IS UNAFFECTED, AND STILL THE REAL PROBLEM. _crawl_link_audit reads whole files with no cap, so the binary '15 of 15 linked' and the 'single inbound source: /zip/ <- building page' finding stand. So does the census, which is the harder number: 353 of 455 sampled published URLs are UNKNOWN TO GOOGLE — never fetched — and mature acceptance has been 0.0% for eight days with 0 of 50 URLs indexed past 28 days. gsc_serving_entered 0 AND gsc_serving_left 0 on the same day is new and worth naming: the eight serving pages are not the survivors of churn, they are a static set nothing is joining. Paired with serving_ever flat twice, the intake is closed rather than slow. TODAY'S RESEARCH ADDED NOTHING THE LEDGER DOES NOT ALREADY HOLD. Current large-site guidance converges on 'important pages within ~3 clicks' and on internal linking as the dominant lever for what a large site gets crawled — which corroborates the depth question rather than answering it, and makes a correct instrument more valuable, not less. On AI citation, the 2026 material says 90-95% of citations come from external sources and that proprietary first-party data is the strongest citation asset; both are already in the ledger as T047 and T025 and neither is a new angle. STILL BROKEN, NOT MINE: AdSense has no slot id so no ads render, api@findacrib.com may not be a live forwarding alias.

**Changed.** FIXED THE DEPTH CRAWLER AND MADE THIS FAILURE UNABLE TO RECUR SILENTLY, in growth/techniques.py. (1) CRAWL_DEPTH_BYTES 300,000 -> 4,000,000, ~12x the largest page this site publishes, with the measurement that justifies it written into the comment (byte offsets of index.html and the four other shells, and the reproduction) so the next reader does not have to re-derive it. The cap is raised, not removed: an unbounded read on an unknown file is how a nightly build hangs. (2) NEW CRAWL_DEPTH_TOTAL_BYTES = 200,000,000, a whole-crawl budget, so raising the per-page cap 13x cannot turn the build into a disk-bound job. (3) _crawl_depth now READS ONE BYTE PAST ITS BUDGET to detect a page it had to cut, collects those paths, and returns them as a fourth element; _crawl_depth_note renders a CAUTION clause naming up to four of them and stating that every depth above is an UPPER BOUND. This is the part that matters more than the constant: a clipped page is indistinguishable from a sparse one by its output, so the only place the fact survives is at the read. VERIFIED, not assumed: with the real index.html a 300k cap now reports clipped=['/'] where before it reported nothing; a synthetic 52-page docroot shaped like the real failure (padding first, nav last) reports '/zip/ 3, /hub/ 2, /borough/ 1 — CAUTION, 1 page exceeded the 200,000-byte read cap and contributed only its opening links, so every depth above is an upper bound: /' against a true distribution of 2, 1, 1 — i.e. the exact 08-31 error, now flagged in the same clause as the numbers. At 4M the same docroot reads the true distribution with no caution. The total-budget exhaustion path was exercised separately and reports the same way. Pass/fail semantics of t_crawl_paths are UNCHANGED — orphans still fail, depth is still report-only, and the CRAWL_DEPTH_FLOOR bare-checkout guard still fires. Recorded the false reading verbatim in t_crawl_paths' docstring with the instruction to discard rather than adjust those depths, because it will be quoted. DELIBERATELY SHIPPED NOTHING ELSE: the obvious second change today was to flatten the link graph, and doing that would have meant acting on the reading I had just proved false — the same error, one day later. The flattening decision needs one true reading first, which lands tonight. ADDED NO KEYWORDS (365 tracked, 0 ranking, 83.0% covered — the query universe is not the constraint). RETIRED NOTHING. NO REVISIT WAS DUE: nearest is T037 crawl_paths on 2026-09-04, which now has a chance of being answered with data. Did NOT run keywords.check_coverage(). VERIFIED: py_compile clean on growth/*.py and growth_daily.py; 'growth_daily.py build --dry-run --docroot . --build-dir .' exits 0 with the expected bare-checkout FAILs and the worktree left clean; _crawl_depth has exactly one caller and it was updated with the signature.

**Watching.** (1) THE FIRST TRUSTWORTHY DEPTH READING LANDS TONIGHT, in tomorrow's t_crawl_paths detail in growth/last_run.json. READ THE CAUTION CLAUSE BEFORE THE NUMBERS. If it is present, the depths are upper bounds and the named pages are the ones to look at — do not quote a depth from a run that carries it. *6am review*

**Observed.** THE TWO PRE-REGISTERED TESTS BOTH RESOLVED, BOTH NEGATIVE. (1) CLICK DEPTH IS NOT THE CONSTRAINT. The first trustworthy reading from the fixed crawler landed in today's t_crawl_paths detail: 598 pages read, levels 0-3 FULLY EXPANDED, NO CAUTION CLAUSE, and all 15 published sections placed — /dc/neighborhood/ 3, /la/zip/ 3, /sf/neighborhood/ 3, /zip/ 3, /available/ 2, /building/ 2, /dc/buildings/ 2, /guide/is-my-apartment-rent-controlled- 2, +7 shallower. They came back 2 and 3. It is dead. (Note the crawl stopped at 598 pages because every prefix had been placed, not because a budget bound — a BFS with levels 0-3 fully expanded gives exact depths for everything at depth <= 3, so these are measurements, not upper bounds.) (2) LINK VOLUME IS NOT THE CONSTRAINT EITHER. build_seo.py's CITY_NAV comment pre-registered a fortnight: 'if those families are still 0% fetched in two weeks, link volume is not the constraint and the next thing to suspect is site-level crawl rationing.' CITY_NAV has linked /sf/ /la/ /dc/ from all 47,165 building pages since 2026-08-18. Today, fifteen days later, the census reads /dc/ 0 of 20 ever fetched and /la/ 0 of 20. THE CENSUS, WHICH IS THE HARD NUMBER. cohort 455, fetched 95 (20.9%), indexed 1 (0.2%), accept_pct 1.1, accept_pct_mature 0.0% for the NINTH consecutive day, 28d+ bucket 0 of 50. Of the 95 URLs Google has ever fetched, 94 read 'Crawled - currently not indexed' and exactly ONE is indexed: the homepage. Not one building, neighborhood, ZIP, borough, guide or city page has ever been kept, and some were crawled as far back as 2026-06-24 — ten weeks. Broken out by family: /building/ 67 crawled-not-indexed + 182 unknown, /neighborhood/ 15 CNI + 23 unknown, /borough/ 5 CNI + 5 unknown, /zip/ 4 CNI + 36 unknown, /sf/ 1 CNI + 19 unknown, /buildings/ 1 CNI, /developers/ 1 CNI; never fetched at all: /available/ 0 of 15, /brief/ 0 of 10, /guide/ 0 of 10, /dc/ 0 of 20, /la/ 0 of 20, /section8/ 0 of 6, /council-district/ 0 of 5. SEARCH, STILL FALLING. gsc_serving_pages 6 — 8 yesterday, 39 on 08-24, 63 on 08-20 — with serving_entered 0 and serving_left 2. gsc_serving_ever 277, FLAT FOR THE FOURTH DAY. gsc_clicks 59, of which gsc_branded_clicks is 59; gsc_nonbranded_clicks 0 for the FIFTH consecutive day, nonbranded_impressions 1. search_share_pct 0.0, 0 of 365 tracked queries ranking, keyword coverage 83.0%. TRAFFIC (09-01): 37 visitors, 6 organic, 0 AI. REVENUE ZERO ON DAY 37 OF THE $9 REPORT: paying_subs 0, mrr_usd 0, reports_sold 0, api_keys 3 for the fourteenth day, accounts_with_saves 11. CRONS FIRED: daily 05:00-05:11 rc=0, build --deploy 05:40-05:42 rc=0, seo pipeline 49,371 pages 'ran clean (synced-1)'. ANTHROPIC: scout and outreach both 'Your credit balance is too low to access the Anthropic API' — an EMPTY BALANCE, not the console usage cap, third day. YESTERDAY'S CHANGED LINE CHECKS OUT: CRAWL_DEPTH_BYTES is 4,000,000, CRAWL_DEPTH_TOTAL_BYTES is 200,000,000, _crawl_depth returns clipped as its fourth element and _crawl_depth_note renders the CAUTION clause. All four shipped as claimed.

**Concluded.** DISCOVERY IS NOT THE PROBLEM AND HAS NOT BEEN FOR SOME TIME. Three separate discovery hypotheses have now been tested and killed on their own pre-registered terms: orphaned sections (15 of 15 linked since 08-23), click depth (everything within 3 clicks, measured today), and link volume (47,165 inbound links to /dc/ and /la/ bought 0 fetches in a fortnight). Google can reach these pages. What it will not do is KEEP them: 94 of 95 fetched, 1 indexed, and the one it kept is the homepage. That is not a crawl-budget story and no amount of internal linking, sitemap work or IndexNow will touch it. THE SURVIVING EXPLANATION IS THAT THE PAGES ARE NEAR-DUPLICATES OF EACH OTHER, AND TODAY IT STOPPED BEING PROSE. This repo has carried the claim since the landlord tier shipped — 'the building tier fails because a template over one building runs out of things to say by the third paragraph, and 47,000 of them say the same three' — and nobody had ever put a number on it. Measured today, rendering the real generator against the real data in this checkout: /building/manhattan/246-10th-ave-1007220003/ and /building/manhattan/299-10th-ave-1006990031/ share 425 of 465 words IN IDENTICAL ORDER, 91.4%. The 40 words that differ are the street number, three counts, a year, a percentile and one sibling link. 67 of the 94 crawled-not-indexed URLs are building pages. WHAT WOULD FALSIFY IT, AND WHAT ALREADY COMPLICATES IT: the /neighborhood/ pages are 1,378 words of genuinely different data and 15 of them are ALSO crawled-not-indexed, so duplication of the building tier is not a complete account of the rejection. The reading that fits both is a SITE-LEVEL judgement — 47,165 near-identical documents are the overwhelming majority of what Google has seen of this domain, and it appears to be pricing the whole domain on them, hub pages included. TODAY'S RESEARCH CONVERGES ON THAT, WITH A CAVEAT ABOUT ITS SOURCES. The 2026 material is unanimous that 'crawled - currently not indexed' at scale is a quality and duplication verdict rather than a technical fault, that Google now derives a SITE-WIDE signal from the ratio of templated to original pages, and that the threshold being applied is roughly 'one piece of information per page that exists nowhere else'. If that last part is right it has a sharp consequence this ledger has never accounted for: the ~43,500 building pages carrying noindex,follow are NOT neutral. noindex keeps them out of the index; it does not keep them out of the site-quality assessment, because Googlebot still fetches them and they are still near-identical. Deindexing was treated here as if it removed those pages from consideration. It may only have removed them from the upside. I am flagging this rather than acting on it: these are secondary SEO sources, not Google primary documentation, I could not verify the July 2026 Mueller/Splitt statement they all cite against a primary source, and acting on an unverified claim is exactly how 08-31 spent a day. STILL BROKEN, NOT MINE: AdSense has no slot id so no ads render; api@findacrib.com may not be a live forwarding alias.

**Changed.** SHIPPED T072 page_uniqueness, a nightly per-section near-duplicate measurement, in growth/techniques.py with its ledger record in growth/seed.py, registered in REGISTRY and slotted into ORDER beside crawl_paths. It reads the live docroot, buckets every .html page under each prefix the LEDGER declares (whatever the technique's status, because a retired section is still in the docroot and still part of what Google prices this domain on), takes a strided 24-page sample per section, and reports the median share of a page's 8-word shingles that also appear on at least half of its siblings — plus the median word count, because '90% duplicate of 460 words' and '90% duplicate of 2,000 words' are different findings. WHY AN INSTRUMENT AND NOT A FIX: the 91.4% above is one hand-measured pair on one checkout, which is precisely the class of number this journal has twice been burned treating as a distribution, and every structural fix on the table (consolidate the building tier, cut the template boilerplate, promote fewer pages) is a decision about HOW MUCH duplication there is per tier, which nobody has. It is REPORT-ONLY BY DESIGN — no duplicate-share threshold, because there is no distribution to set one against yet, and the depth crawler is the standing lesson about setting a bar before you have one. ok is False only when a published section has pages that could not be read at all. VALIDATED BEFORE TRUSTING IT, which is now the house rule: rendered 118 real pages from build_seo.py against the real buildings.min.json into a temp docroot and ran the technique over them. It reads /building/ 43% of 476 words, /borough/ 28% of 480, /zip/ 14% of 1,363, /neighborhood/ 14% of 1,378 — the building tier repeating itself three times as much in a third of the space, and it is the tier holding 67 of the 94 crawled-not-indexed URLs. THE 43% AND THE 91.4% ARE NOT THE SAME MEASUREMENT and the code says so in a calibration comment: the hand figure is word-for-word overlap between two NEIGHBOURING pages, which also share their neighbourhood, ZIP and sibling-link ring, while the audit strides across the whole tier so those differ and a single changed token voids all eight windows containing it. 43% is the floor — the share of a page that is the template speaking to the entire tier. FIXED ONE DEFECT IN IT BEFORE PUSHING: the first version tested os.path.isdir() on each prefix, which silently measured NOTHING for the three page-level prefixes this site declares (/guide/is-my-apartment-rent-controlled- names one document, not a directory). It now walks the docroot once and matches on URL path, and a section too small to compare says so by name instead of vanishing. Same floor guard as the depth crawler: below 20 readable pages it reports 'NOT MEASURED ... this is a bare checkout', verified against this checkout. DELIBERATELY SHIPPED NOTHING ELSE. The obvious second change was to start consolidating the building tier on the strength of one hand-measured pair, which is the 08-31 error with a different subject. ADDED NO KEYWORDS: 365 tracked, 0 ranking, 83.0% covered — the query universe has never been the constraint and today made that clearer, not less clear. RETIRED NOTHING. NO REVISIT WAS DUE (T037 crawl_paths on 2026-09-04, which now has two dead hypotheses to answer for). Did NOT run keywords.check_coverage(). VERIFIED: py_compile clean on growth/*.py, growth_daily.py and build_seo.py; 'growth_daily.py build --dry-run --docroot . --build-dir .' exits 0 with page_uniqueness reporting NOT MEASURED and the worktree left clean; report renders both parts, 8 table-layout:fixed intact, zero javascript: hrefs.

**Watching.** (1) THE FIRST REAL DUPLICATE-SHARE READING LANDS TONIGHT, in tomorrow's page_uniqueness detail in growth/last_run.json, taken over the actual 49,371-page corpus instead of my 118-page render. Read it as a CONTRAST between tiers, not as an absolute. The prediction on record, so it can be wrong: /building/ comes back at 35-55% of ~470 words and is the most duplicated section, /neighborhood/ and /zip/ come back under 20% of 1,300+ words. If /building/ reads under 25%, my whole account of the rejection is weak and the duplication story needs dropping, not defending. If /neighborhood/ reads as high as /building/, the problem is the shared header/footer/CTA chrome rather than the building template, and the fix is a different one. (2) SANITY-CHECK THE INSTRUMENT BEFORE QUOTING IT, exactly as the depth crawler was checked on 09-01 and the census guard on 08-30. The detail line names how many pages it read and across how many sections. If it says fewer than ~200 pages across fewer than 8 sections on the droplet, it is not seeing the corpus and the reading is about something else. If any section reports 'COULD NOT READ', that is ok=False and a real defect, not a duplication finding. (3) DOES ANY PAGE BESIDES THE HOMEPAGE EVER GET INDEXED. index_state_indexed has been 1 since 08-28 and accept_pct_mature 0.0% for nine days with 0 of 50 past 28 days. This is now the single number that matters most on this site; everything else is upstream of it. (4) gsc_serving_ever, fifth flat day tomorrow. It cannot fall, so four days at 277 already says the intake is closed rather than slow, and a fifth makes it the cleanest evidence here. (5) gsc_nonbranded_clicks, five days at 0 against a branded series that went 5 -> 33 -> 49 -> 53 -> 59. It is the only click number that bears on the 90% goal, and the branded rise is jayshomefinder and findacrib residue, not progress. (6) THE noindex,follow QUESTION IS THE NEXT REAL DECISION, and it needs better evidence than I have. If site-level quality is assessed over what Googlebot FETCHES rather than what it indexes, the 43,500 noindex building pages are still counted against this domain and consolidating them — fewer, richer URLs rather than more, quieter ones — is the change. Before acting: find a Google primary source, and check whether the census shows Googlebot still fetching noindexed building URLs at all. Do not act on the SEO-blog consensus alone. (7) THE ANTHROPIC BALANCE IS EMPTY, not capped — 'credit balance is too low', third day. The nightly research loop is off and the ledger stops gaining candidates until it is topped up. (8) published_urls should sit near 3,668. (9) Unchanged: subtract findacrib and jayshomefinder from every raw click count, and treat every serving_pages figure as a floor.


## 2026-09-03 — The first duplicate-share reading falsified yesterday's prediction; the worst live section is the DC hub tier, and its sibling block was both false and identical on all 103 pages
*6am review*

**Observed.** THE READING YESTERDAY PRE-REGISTERED LANDED, AND MY PREDICTION WAS WRONG. page_uniqueness ran on the real 49,374-page corpus and reported: /brief/ 49% of 182 words (n=17 of 17), /dc/neighborhood/ 47% of 472 words (n=24 of 103), /available/ 38% of 230 words (n=24 of 88), /building/ 32% of 492 words (n=24 of 47,165), /section8/ 23% of 800, /la/zip/ 22% of 1,352, /neighborhood/ 19% of 1,020, /sf/neighborhood/ 17% of 1,464 — 211 pages across 10 sections, ok=true, no COULD NOT READ. The instrument passes its own sanity check (211 pages > ~200, 10 sections > 8). I predicted /building/ at 35-55% and MOST duplicated. It came back 32% — below the band — and FOURTH. THE INSTRUMENT IS TRUSTWORTHY: I re-rendered the dc/sf/la hub tiers from this checkout's own buildings.min.json and ran growth.techniques._dup_measure over them, and got dc 46.7% of 472 words, sf 17.0% of 1,464, la 21.7% of 1,352 — the droplet's three figures to the digit. THE INDEX CENSUS, UNMOVED: cohort 455, fetched 95 (20.9%), indexed 1 (0.2%), accept_pct 1.1, accept_pct_mature 0.0% for the TENTH consecutive day, 28d+ bucket 0 of 49, states crawled_not_indexed 94 / discovered_not_indexed 6 / indexed 1 / unknown_to_google 354. SEARCH: gsc_serving_pages 5 (6 yesterday, 8 on 09-01, 39 on 08-24, 63 on 08-20), serving_ever 277 FLAT FOR THE FIFTH DAY, serving_stable 5, clicks 59 of which nonbranded 0 for the SIXTH consecutive day, nonbranded_impressions 1, share_pct 0.0, tracked_ranking 0 of 365, coverage 83.0%. REVENUE: $0/mo, paying_subs 0, reports_sold 0 — day 38 of the $9 report. CRONS ALL FIRED: daily 05:00-05:18 rc=0, build --deploy 05:40-05:42 rc=0, seo pipeline 49,374 pages 177 changed 'ran clean (synced-1)', watchdog ok. ANTHROPIC IS BACK: scout ok=true, 3 techniques and 25 keywords added; outreach ok=true, 0 drafted. Neither the empty balance nor the console cap — the research loop is running again. YESTERDAY'S CHANGED LINE CHECKS OUT: t_page_uniqueness is in growth/techniques.py, registered in REGISTRY and ORDER beside crawl_paths, with its T072 record in growth/seed.py; it walks the docroot and matches on URL path (no isdir test), and the 20-page floor message fired correctly on this bare checkout. All shipped as claimed.

**Concluded.** THE DUPLICATION STORY SURVIVES BUT ITS SUBJECT CHANGED, AND I SHOULD SAY PLAINLY THAT I HAD THE WRONG SUSPECT. /building/ at 32% of 492 words is above my 25% falsification line, so near-duplication remains a live account of the rejection — but the building tier is not the most duplicated thing this site publishes, and I asserted it would be. The two sections above it are the ones nobody was looking at: /brief/ (49%, already retired for earning 0 impressions — its duplication is now the measured reason WHY, which is a satisfying confirmation of a verdict reached on traffic alone) and /dc/neighborhood/, which is LIVE, 103 pages, and the worst-offending section this site actively maintains. THE DC HUB TIER IS BOTH THE THINNEST AND THE MOST REPETITIVE OF THE THREE CITY TIERS, AND THAT COMBINATION IS THE FINDING: 472 words at 47% shared, against sf 1,464 at 17% and la 1,352 at 22%. Same generator, same template, three very different outcomes — so the template is not the variable. The variable is how much per-page data each city's records carry into the address listing, and DC's neighborhoods are small enough that the constant chrome dominates. READING THE ACTUAL RENDERED PAGE FOUND A DEFECT THAT IS NOT PRIMARILY AN SEO PROBLEM. Every place page in all three cities carried a block headed 'Nearby in <city>' listing the twelve HIGHEST-COUNT places in the city — the same twelve links, in the same order, on every page in the tier. On /dc/neighborhood/mount-vernon-square/ that block opened with Bellevue, which is across the Anacostia and about six miles away. THAT IS A FALSE STATEMENT ON 252 LIVE PAGES. This site's credibility is the product; tenants pick where to look with these pages, and a block that says 'nearby' and means 'largest' misdirects them. It would be worth fixing if it cost us traffic, and it is worth fixing anyway. IT WAS ALSO SILENTLY WRECKING THE TIER'S LINK TOPOLOGY, which I measured rather than assumed: because the twelve never varied, only twelve pages per city ever received a sibling link. 91 of DC's 103 neighborhood hubs, 100 of LA's 112 ZIP hubs and 25 of SF's 37 had exactly ONE inbound link in the whole site — the browse hub — and nothing else. TODAY'S RESEARCH BEARS ON THIS DIRECTLY, WITH THE USUAL CAVEAT ABOUT ITS PROVENANCE. The 2026 programmatic-SEO material converges on two claims: link every page you want indexed from two to three crawlable places rather than one, and a flat structure of isolated pages generates far less ranking power than one where authority flows outward across related pages — with proximity linking between location pages named specifically as the pattern for geographic programmatic content. These are SEO blogs, not Google primary documentation, and I am not resting the change on them: the change is justified by the false claim alone, and the link topology is a bonus I can measure rather than a bet I am taking. STILL UNRESOLVED AND STILL THE BIGGEST QUESTION: the 09-02 noindex,follow question is untouched — whether the 43,500 noindexed building pages still count against site quality — and it still needs a primary source before anyone acts on it. STILL BROKEN, NOT MINE: AdSense has no slot id so no ads render; api@findacrib.com may not be a live forwarding alias.

**Changed.** REPLACED THE FALSE 'Nearby in <city>' BLOCK WITH A REAL ONE, in build_seo.py's city_hub_docs() — the SF/LA/DC aggregate tier only; NYC's 47,165 building pages are a different generator and are untouched, so no lastmod churn there. Added three helpers beside _city_stats: _city_centroid() takes the MEDIAN lat and lng of a place's mapped records (median, not mean, so one mis-geocoded record cannot drag a neighborhood's centre across the city and silently corrupt every distance computed from it), _miles() is a plain haversine, and _city_neighbors() returns the twelve closest places nearest-first as (place, count, miles). The block is now headed 'Nearest neighborhoods in Washington, DC' / 'Nearest ZIP codes in Los Angeles' via _place_word, each link reads 'Downtown — 0.2 mi (8)', and a one-line disclaimer states exactly what the distance is: 'Ordered by distance from the mid-point of the mapped properties in each, which is not an official boundary.' THAT CAVEAT IS NOT DECORATION — nothing here reads a boundary file, the centroid is the centre of what THIS dataset maps, and for SF the underlying records are block-anonymized, so claiming an official distance would be asserting precision the data does not have. WHY THIS AND NOTHING ELSE: it is one change that fixes an accuracy defect, cuts the worst live section's duplication, and repairs the tier's internal linking, and every one of those three is measured below rather than argued. DATA COVERAGE CHECKED BEFORE BUILDING ON IT: lat and lng are present on 4,316 of 4,316 DC records, 12,297 of 12,297 SF and 67,511 of 67,511 LA — 100%. _city_neighbors returns [] when fewer than two places carry a position and the old count ordering renders under the honest heading 'Elsewhere in <city>', so a future dataset without coordinates degrades instead of shipping an empty block or a lie. MEASURED, BEFORE AND AFTER, BY RENDERING BOTH VERSIONS OF THE TIER FROM THIS CHECKOUT AND RUNNING THE PRODUCTION _dup_measure OVER THEM. Duplicate share: dc 46.7% -> 37.5% (-9.2 points), sf 17.0% -> 14.9%, la 21.7% -> 20.1%. Pages with NO sibling inbound link: dc 91 of 103 -> 0, sf 25 of 37 -> 0, la 100 of 112 -> 0; distinct sibling link targets per city 12 -> 103 / 37 / 112. Median words: dc 452 -> 508, sf 1,298 -> 1,352, la 1,356 -> 1,412. SPOT-CHECKED THE OUTPUT RATHER THAN TRUSTING THE AGGREGATE: /dc/neighborhood/mount-vernon-square/ now opens Downtown 0.2 mi, Logan Circle/Shaw 0.3 mi, Mount Vernon Triangle 0.4 mi — all genuinely adjacent — where it used to open with Bellevue. ADDED NO KEYWORDS: 365 tracked, 0 ranking, 83.0% covered; the query universe has never been the constraint. RETIRED NOTHING. NO REVISIT WAS DUE — T037 crawl_paths falls tomorrow, 2026-09-04, and arrives owing an answer for two dead discovery hypotheses. Did NOT run keywords.check_coverage(). VERIFIED: py_compile clean on growth/*.py, growth_daily.py and build_seo.py; 'growth_daily.py build --dry-run --docroot . --build-dir .' exits 0 and reports city_seo_expansion would publish 255 pages (dc 104, la 113, sf 38), which is 103/112/37 place pages plus a browse hub each — the counts match the render exactly; worktree left clean apart from build_seo.py.

**Watching.** (1) DOES /dc/neighborhood/ ACTUALLY FALL IN TOMORROW'S page_uniqueness DETAIL. The prediction on record so it can be wrong: dc reads 36-39% of ~500 words, la drops about 1.5 points, sf about 2. If dc comes back near 47% the change did not reach the docroot — check the seo pipeline line in last_run.json before blaming the measurement, because this tier is published by the SEO pipeline and not by the growth build. If it falls much further than 39%, my before/after harness is measuring something the droplet is not, and the harness is the thing to distrust, not the result. Thin, duplicated, unmaintained and never crawled is four reasons pointing one way: the question for a future run is whether it should be consolidated or stopped, not improved. (3) accept_pct_mature and index_state_indexed. Eleven days at 0.0% and one indexed page. This remains the single number that matters most; everything else on this site is upstream of it. Nothing shipped today can move it for weeks — a hub change has to be recrawled first — so do not read a flat reading tomorrow as a verdict on this change. (4) gsc_serving_ever, sixth flat day at 277. It cannot fall, so five flat days already say the intake is closed rather than slow. (5) gsc_nonbranded_clicks, six days at 0 against a branded series of 59. Branded is jayshomefinder and findacrib residue, not progress. (6) THE noindex,follow QUESTION IS STILL THE NEXT REAL DECISION and still needs a Google primary source plus evidence from the census that Googlebot is still fetching noindexed building URLs at all. Do not act on the SEO-blog consensus. (7) THE ANTHROPIC ACCOUNT IS FUNDED AGAIN — scout and outreach both ok=true today after three days down.
